### TREATISE I. BOOK II. THE EXPERIMENT IS AS FOLLOWS. ###

<image-desc>A circular scientific diagram enclosed in a square frame. The circle represents a globe or cosmological model divided into concentric layers. The left hemisphere is shaded with horizontal hatching of varying density to represent different regions or states of matter, with arc labels '1' and '2' at the top and radii labels 'c', 'e', 'g', and 'i' on the horizontal and lower perimeter. The right hemisphere is mostly clear, illustrating radii with points labeled 'A', 'd', 'f', 'h' on the vertical axis and 'b' at the center.</image-desc>

In the beginning, the **potential substance** <term>potential substance: matter that exists as a raw possibility before it has been shaped into a specific element or object</term> of the waters in the world was no different than if a certain part of an artificial globe—divided into various cavities with specific intervals passing from the circumference to the center, such as the arc **a. c. b.** in the previous demonstration—were filled with clear water or air. In such a state, every part of the water is of the very same thickness and arrangement.

But if fire should touch the outer surface of that arc, the thicker air of the upper region (indicated by **I**), which is found to be much larger in relation to that center, would be reverberated toward the center. This is likewise true for the air of the second and third regions. When this occurs, the air will become extremely thick and compact; for it could not be contained in any other way within such a narrow space as that of the center. The air, which was previously held within the spaces **A. C. D. E.** and **D. E. F. G.** and **F. G. H. I.**, is compressed into that tiny corner of the arc, squeezed together, and reduced into a thick and vaporous mass.

From this principle follows that great mystery of the ancient Philosophers: the discovery of the weights and **proportions of the elements**. Indeed, the entire world was fashioned into a spherical shape for this very reason. The **Archetype** <note>original: *Archetypus* — Fludd refers to God as the master architect who designed the universe according to a divine pattern</note> positioned the thicker bodies toward the center of the **world-machine** <note>original: *machinæ centrum* — the center of the cosmic mechanism</note> so that, as they were made subtler through **rarefaction** <term>rarefaction: the process by which a substance becomes less dense or "thinner," usually due to the application of heat</term> and thinning, they could always seek out a wider space for themselves by ascending. For, as was stated above, dense things require less space before they are dissolved than they do after they have been refined. With the completion of this knowledge, it will not be difficult to measure the capacity of the heavens, provided that the size of the earth is first known; nor will it be difficult to determine the proportions of the elements to each other...

<meta>The catchword "invicem" indicates the sentence continues: "to each other."</meta>

<summary>Fludd uses a mechanical model of a sphere to demonstrate how the "potential substance" of the universe is compressed into a dense center by the action of heat at the circumference. This explains why the Earth is small and solid while the heavens are vast and refined, providing a mathematical basis for the proportions of the elements.</summary>

# BOOK THREE.

## *On the Music of the Spheres.* <note>original: *De Musica mundana*. This refers to the ancient philosophical concept that the proportions in the movements of celestial bodies—the sun, moon, and planets—form a cosmic music that is mathematical rather than audible.</note>

## CHAPTER I.

->On the logic of worldly proportions.<-

From what has been stated previously, it is clear that just as the Empyrean heaven <note>The highest part of the universe, believed in ancient and medieval cosmology to be composed of pure fire or light.</note> is the home and seat of supernatural fire and light—from which natural fire proceeds directly, and that other more accidental fire proceeds indirectly—so too its opposite place, which terminates and ends the movement of its rays, is the receptacle of matter and darkness. Furthermore, since the relationship between opposites is the same, it is necessary for the sake of the world’s continuity that the disposition of the moist nature of darkness bears itself toward that fiery nature of light in the same degrees and by the same proportion. Hence, as we have declared above that the physical center <note>The Earth.</note> is naturally cold in the fourth degree, being most dense, most opaque, and most heavy, and by its own nature seeking rest; so also the ultimate region of the world-machine <note>The outer boundary of the heavens.</note> is without a doubt opposed to it in all things, not only in location but in condition. Thus, by the equal action of the one and the proportional "passion" <note>In the philosophical sense of "being acted upon" or receptivity.</note> of the other, all intermediate bodies are constituted; depending on the nature of the subject—that is, according to the abundance or lack of the light predominating in it—they either resist darkness for a long time or more quickly yield and are subjected to it.

Who then will doubt that the highest region of the Empyrean heaven is free and immune from every invasion of cold, whose specific property is to thicken? For indeed, it is the most subtle, pure, and light, transparent in the highest degree of clarity, and is almost nothing else than fire sublimated to the ultimate peak of most sweet and loving heat. Without doubt, therefore, those proportions of things introduce a most noble worldly harmony; through these proportions, as the virtue of the primary light works upon the intermediate matter, an indissoluble concord of things is won, and a certain inexplicable music soothes the ears of the intellect. For the instrument of this melody—namely, the machine of the world—is like a monochord <note>A one-stringed musical instrument used to demonstrate the mathematical ratios of intervals.</note>, whose string, through which the consensus of parts is introduced, is the intermediate matter of the whole world. The Author in this music, however, is the soul of the world or the essence-making light <note>original: *lux essentifica*. The light that gives things their essential being.</note>; it is no different than in that instrumental music of man, in which <term>Γ.</term> <note>The Greek letter Gamma, used to represent the lowest note of the medieval musical scale.</note>, that is, <term>Gamma</term>, takes its beginning from the lower part of the system, ascending through the low, high, and highest voices. Such a progression surely occurs also in the worldly monochord, namely by ascending proportionally from the earth to its <term>ee la</term> <note>The highest note in the medieval Guidonian system of music.</note>, which is the limit of the outermost circumference. And just as in <term>Gamma</term> the sound is lower and deeper because of its extreme distance from <term>ee la</term>, from which the striking of the voice becomes more intense as it ascends; so also, the higher one ascends from the earth toward the Empyrean heaven, the stronger the effect of light and heat is found, and the worldly spirit is rendered thinner by its virtue. In the same way, a greater force—and therefore a greater virtue of the shining soul—is required for the lifting of the human voice, whereas in its lowering, far less is required.

<summary>This chapter introduces the concept of the "World Monochord," explaining how the universe functions as a musical instrument where the tension between the light of the Empyrean and the darkness of the Earth creates a divine harmony.</summary>
<keywords>Empyrean heaven, World Monochord, cosmic harmony, light and darkness, musical proportions, Gamma, ee la, world soul</keywords>

### TREATISE I. BOOK III.

...is found; thus also a greater proportion of the **world-soul** <note>The *anima mundana*, a vital force believed by Neoplatonists to animate the entire universe.</note> is found in the higher and more subtle **spirit of the world**, and consequently it renders the harmony sharper and more excellent; whereas in the lower and thicker [spirit], the proportion is lesser, whence it is recognized to produce weightier and deeper harmonic effects. From these things, therefore, one may very easily gather those harmonic consonances consisting of the proper proportions of numbers, which it is not difficult to prove are derived and arise from the embrace of simple principles—namely, from the mutual intermingling of the **material and formal pyramids**. For each of these has a pyramidal dimension in this **Macrocosm** <note>The "Great World," referring to the universe as a whole in contrast to the Microcosm (man).</note>, through which it is accustomed to operate in no other way (though more in one place and less in another) than as one portion of a physical pyramid gradually requires a wider cavity for itself in which another portion may be contained.

But the base of the **formal pyramid** <note>The pyramid of light or divine spirit.</note> is adjacent to the very Heaven of the Trinity, and its tip extends all the way down to the earth; just as, conversely, the base of the **material pyramid** is in the earth, and its tip ascends all the way to the peak of the **Empyrean heaven**, that is, into the concave surface of the sky. We have sketched these formal dimensions of the world at the end of the fifth book, where that pyramidal dimension is to be understood in physical things. Here, however, we have briefly sketched the proportional embrace of one pyramid with the other, so that we may better lead students toward a more perfect explanation of this, our **world music**, through a more sensible demonstration of proportions.

In this light it is more manifest that three material parts of the pyramid occupy the lowest region of the world, with which one part of the formal pyramid mixes (namely, that part which is near the tip), which gave the essential form to that entire mass of matter; and two parts of the same fill the middle region, which are informed there by an equal number of portions of the formal pyramid; and a single portion of it [the material pyramid] gives substance to the **Empyrean heaven**, which three portions of the formal pyramid inform supernaturally. From which it clearly appears that the material pyramid grants substance to every part of the world, and that the reciprocal reception of the radiant pyramid transforms it from **prime matter** <note>Latin: *materia prima*, the original, unformed substance of the universe.</note> into secondary and formed matter. Consequently, everything between the margins of the earth and the highest periphery of the Empyrean heaven has received its proportions from the action and passion <note>In the philosophical sense, "action" is the doing and "passion" is the being acted upon.</note> of these two pyramids; from which also all the proportions and consonances of the Macrocosm that constitute the world's harmony are derived. The demonstration of this matter is as follows.

The varying ratios of light to matter in different regions of the cosmos determine their spiritual "pitch" and harmonic character.</summary>

### ON WORLD MUSIC.

A "pyramis formalis" (pyramid of light) descends from this divine source with its apex pointing toward the Earth at the bottom. **A.B.C.** is the diameter of the **material pyramid** <note>In Fludd's philosophy, the material pyramid represents the density and darkness of matter, while the formal pyramid represents the light and spirit of God.</note>, whose source is the **Ocean of Darkness** <note>original: "Oceanus tenebrarum." This refers to the primeval chaos or the unformed state of matter before creation.</note>, namely the earth; for it has its base in the earth. The lower part of this pyramid, **A.B.R.G.**, gives substance and physical bulk <note>original: "corpulentiam"</note> to the lower region of the world, with which the smallest part of the **formal pyramid** <note>The "pyramid of light" or divine influence.</note>, which tends downwards, mixes itself in the smallest degree.

Finally, the material portion of the pyramid <note>original: "pyramidis materialis"</note> **O. C. P.** provides as much physical substance <term>bulk: Fludd uses 'corpulentia' to describe the thickness or density of matter</term> as is found in the upper region, where its minimal substance is shaped by a larger portion of the radiant pyramid, namely **D. E. S. I.**

From this it is clear that the larger part of the **formal pyramid** <note>The "pyramid of form" or light, representing divine influence</note>, in shaping the smaller part of the material one, has fulfilled the "appetite" <note>In Fludd's Neoplatonic philosophy, matter is thought to have an 'appetite' or longing to be shaped and perfected by divine form.</note> of that matter exceedingly and beyond its own nature. Its middle part is perfected by an equal portion of the shining pyramid, and this is done excellently, satisfying the hunger of that substance. Its lower portion, however, stands in a triple proportion to the formal pyramid—that is, as 3 to 1. Thus, the appetite of matter in the lowest part of the physical pyramid is not satisfied; it is therefore corruptible and subject to change. Indeed, the formal pyramid does not act upon the base of the material pyramid, nor does it penetrate to the very center of the earth except by accident <note>A philosophical term meaning 'indirectly' or as a secondary effect, rather than by its essential nature.</note>; for its tip <term>conus: the apex or point of the pyramid</term> only penetrates the surface of the earth, just as the tip of the material pyramid, which is opposite to it at the base, ends at the point of most beautiful glory. From these things, we have most beautifully discovered the **sphere of equality** of the whole world, and the location of the **middle soul** itself, where the uncreated light placed its tabernacle and situated the **Soul of the World** <note>original: "anima mundi"</note>. Indeed, this sphere receives the perfection of a most perfect harmony <term>consonantia: a musical and mathematical agreement</term> equally from both the upper and lower parts; for from it ascends a spiritual **Diapason** <note>The musical interval of an octave, representing a 2:1 ratio.</note>, and to it ascends a material Diapason. For it stands in a double proportion both to the earth and to the place and seat of the simplest form. In its region, therefore, they produce a **vital music** of the most perfect harmony. If the more spiritual part of this **monochord** <note>A one-stringed instrument used to measure the mathematical intervals of music; Fludd envisions the entire universe as a giant monochord.</note> is struck, it gives eternal life, while the more material part gives that which is transitory. Having understood these principles, it will not be difficult to derive the proportions from them, from whose harmony we say the **world music** <note>original: "musica mundana"</note> arises.

## CHAPTER II.

->How the worldly proportions are derived from the<-
->position and location of the parts of each pyramid.<-

<image-desc>Decorative drop cap 'Q' featuring dense floral scrollwork and vine motifs within a square border.</image-desc>

Since in every part of **Hyle** <note>original: "hyla" — the Greek word for "matter" or "primeval substance."</note>, and likewise in every portion of the most beautiful light, there was no difference at all but rather supreme equality before their mixing took place, it follows necessarily that any alteration—both in the now-formed matter of Hyle and in the now-created form itself—consists in the greater or lesser action and passion <note>In this context, 'action' is the influence of light/form, and 'passion' is the receptivity or being-acted-upon of matter.</note> of these principles. But because the rays of natural light, tending downward with the tip of their pyramid, have not yet penetrated to the inner bowels of the earth itself, it is necessary that the interior parts of the earth—since any thing is composed of four quarters—should retain and possess four "witnesses" of coldness due to the total absence of light. But the **diaphanous region** <note>The transparent atmosphere or air.</note> floating above it, since it is clear, transparent, and bright, denotes by this very fact that a single portion of light has been granted to it, by which it obtains its transparent and mobile nature. From this it appears that the proportion between the earth and that lowest region is clear, since the earth has four "witnesses" of density, while the elemental diaphanous region has only three. Thus, they stand in a **sesquitertia proportion** <note>The ratio of 4:3, which in music creates the interval of a Perfect Fourth.</note>, since the earth relates to that transparent elemental region as 4 to 3. Next, the three parts of this lowest transparent region are found to be in a **sesquialtera proportion** <note>The ratio of 3:2, which in music creates the interval of a Perfect Fifth.</note> to the two parts of the ethereal region (in the middle of which the sun is exalted), and they relate to each other as 3 to 2; just as, further on, two parts of that middle region constitute the physical bulk...

# ON WORLDLY MUSIC.

...observing these, if they are related to that single portion of their pyramid which is found in the highest or Empyrean region, they are found in a double proportion <note>original: "dupla proportione"; a 2:1 ratio, which in music corresponds to an octave</note>, and relate to each other as 2 to 1. And it is certain that these proportions of the **material pyramid** arise according to the multiplication or diminution of the parts of light; just as the proportions of the **formal pyramid** itself arise from the greater or lesser presence or absence of the material portion. 

For its source—purely formal and uncreated, from whose base the formal pyramid descends—insofar as it is purely and absolutely formal, possesses no portion of matter within itself. Therefore, it is known to consist of "four fourths" of heat <note>original: "quatuor caliditatis quartis"; a reference to the medieval system of degrees where a quality at the fourth degree represents absolute, undiluted perfection</note>, which is to say, it consists of the most perfect simplicity and absolute homogeneity. 

If those three portions of created light in the highest region of the air—namely, the **Empyrean** <term>Empyrean: the highest part of heaven, thought by Fludd to be composed of pure light and fire</term>—are related to this source of the pyramid, they will stand in a **sesquitertian** proportion <term>sesquitertian: a ratio of 4:3, which produces the musical interval of a perfect fourth</term>, specifically as 4 to 3. 

Similarly, three portions of the light of that heaven, when compared with the two portions of the intermediate heaven, will constitute a **sesquialteran** proportion <term>sesquialteran: a ratio of 3:2, which produces the musical interval of a perfect fifth</term>, relating to each other as 3 to 2. 

Moreover, two portions of the middle heaven are compared to that single portion of the lower heaven in a double proportion, relating to each other as 2 to 1. From these things, it is manifest that the **formal pyramid** descends from its base toward the earth by the same proportions as the **material pyramid** ascends toward the heaven; consequently, in their intersection, they relate to one another proportionally. From these general proportions, the harmonies—both general and particular—of the **world monochord** <note>original: "monochordi mundani"; Fludd’s famous concept of the entire universe as a single-stringed musical instrument tuned by the hand of God</note> are produced, as we shall declare in the following chapter. 

**The demonstration of these Macrocosmic proportions is made thus.**

-> **THE INTERSECTING PYRAMIDS OF FORM AND MATTER** <-

**Numerical Axis:**

<note>These numbers represent the Pythagorean "Tetractys," the foundation of all musical intervals: the octave (2:1), the fifth (3:2), and the fourth (4:3).</note>

**The Three Spheres of Creation:**
*   **The highest region of created things.** <note>original: "Regio suprema ex creatis." This is the Empyrean heaven, the realm of pure fire and spirit.</note>
*   **The middle region.** <note>original: "Regio media." This is the ethereal realm of the stars and planets.</note>
*   **The lowest region.** <note>original: "Regio Infima." This is the elemental world of earth and water.</note>

**Harmonic Proportions of the Spheres:**
*   **Quadruple proportion of quality** <term>Quadruple proportion: a 4:1 ratio, known in music as a "bis-diapason" or double octave, representing the total span from the highest spirit to the lowest matter.</term>
*   **Double proportion** <term>Double proportion: a 2:1 ratio, the musical "diapason" or octave.</term>
*   **Sesquialteran proportion** <term>Sesquialteran: a 3:2 ratio, the musical "diapente" or perfect fifth.</term>
*   **Sesquitertian proportion** <term>Sesquitertian: a 4:3 ratio, the musical "diatessaron" or perfect fourth.</term>

**Sphere Alpha**
<note>original: "Sphæra α"; likely designating the primary sphere of the Sun or the beginning of the created order.</note>

<meta>This page continues the technical explanation of Robert Fludd’s "World Monochord." Having previously introduced the two intersecting pyramids of light (Form) and darkness (Matter), Fludd now explains how these two forces interact like a musician and an instrument to create the "Music of the Spheres." He argues that neither matter nor spirit can create harmony alone; they must be tuned together in specific mathematical proportions.</meta>

<header>OF WORLD MUSIC.</header>

## CHAPTER III.

->*On the world monochord and the discovery of its simple and compound consonances, from which the world harmony is composed.*<-

<image-desc>Decorative initial Q with foliate scrollwork within a square border.</image-desc>

**SINCE** all these proportions in either of the aforementioned pyramids are worth nothing on their own to produce musical consonances without the mixing of both (for neither is a lute <note>original: "barbiton," an ancient stringed instrument similar to a lyre or lute</note> without a musician, nor a musician without a lute sufficient to make musical consonances), therefore both pyramids must necessarily concur to establish the world harmony: namely the **material** pyramid, which takes the place of the musical instrument, or the string of the monochord, or the instrument and breath of the singer; and also the **formal** pyramid, which performs the office of the soul striking or singing and producing the voice. Therefore, an increase of formal substance <note>The "formal substance" refers to divine light or spirit.</note> produces a sharper and more subtle air, and consequently brings forth rarer and more excellent world symphonies; no differently than the more intense spirit of a man or the string of an instrument will give sharper voices and more subtle air through more violent vibrations. Its decrease, however, makes a thicker air and consequently a heavier <note>In the musical context of the time, "heavy" (gravis) meant lower in pitch.</note> world harmony, no differently than the relaxed voice of a man or the loosened string of a monochord returns softer sounds; and this happens because of the excessive thickness of the air. Light, therefore, acts upon the world matter by the same means as the spirit of man acts upon the air. From which it is manifest that one nature, whether material or formal, is worth nothing without the other, and hence World Music arises from both pyramids mixed in the smallest degree. For without the presence of light, humid matter would not have a pyramidal form, nor would created form have its own without humid matter. Whence it is certain that they are mixed with one another according to "more or less," based on the nature of the portions of each pyramid moving oppositely in the Macrocosm <term>Macrocosm: the "Great World" or the entire universe as a single organism.</term> and concurring with each other.

These things being known, let us take our beginning from the matter of the world, which we have likened to the string of a monochord, whose great instrument is the Macrocosm itself, so that we may aptly compare every degree—through which some difference of places is discerned between the center of the world instrument and its periphery—to musical intervals both simple and compound. Wherefore it must be known that just as the string of an instrument is accustomed to be divided by metric proportions by reason of the ascent from **Γ** <note>original: "Gamma," the lowest note in the medieval musical scale or "gamut."</note> through intervals, so also both the matter and its form are distributed by quantitative degrees, and distinguished by similar proportions constituting musical consonances. For if a monochord were imaginatively extended from the summit of the Empyrean heaven <note>The highest, most spiritual part of the universe.</note> to the base of the earth itself, constituting the radius of either pyramid, we will perceive that it is divided into parts constituting consonances; of which if the half part were pressed, it would produce the <term>Diapason: the musical interval of an octave.</term> consonance, just as experience teaches that the same thing happens in an instrumental monochord.

It must be considered, however, that in a world monochord of this kind, both simple and compound consonances, and similarly the proper intervals measuring them, can be delineated no differently than when we divide an instrumental monochord into proportional parts. For the effect of coldness in the earth, and consequently the matter of the earth itself in thickness and weight, stands naturally in relation to the coldness and matter of the lowest region, in which only a single

Having established that the "Formal" (light) and "Material" (darkness) pyramids must interact to create sound, Fludd now applies specific musical intervals—the Fourth, the Fifth, and the Octave—to the physical layers of the universe, from the center of the Earth to the highest heaven.</meta>

A fourth part of light and natural heat is present, in a ratio of 4 to 3, which is the <term>sesquitertial proportion <note>A ratio where the greater number contains the smaller plus one-third part of it (4:3).</note></term>, as was said above; in this proportion consists the <term>consonance of the Fourth <note>original: "Diateſſaron"</note></term>, composed of three intervals: namely, water, air, and fire. For Earth in world music behaves like the <term>Gamma <note>The lowest note in the medieval musical scale, represented by the Greek letter Γ.</note></term> in music, the unit in Arithmetic, and the point in Geometry. It is, as it were, the starting point from which the ratio of proportional matter must be reckoned, since it is the base and foundation of the material pyramid. Therefore, water will occupy the place of one whole tone, and air also the place of one further interval. But the sphere of fire, because it is nothing other than the ignited summit of the region of air, holds the place of the <term>minor semitone <note>A musical interval smaller than a whole tone, often used to describe the "step" between different elemental spheres.</note></term>. 

However, insofar as two portions of this matter are raised up pyramidally all the way to the point of the middle heaven to resist the actions of supernatural heat, and just as many parts of light also act pyramidally downward into those two portions of matter, they create the composition of the sphere of the Sun, and naturally grant it the name of "Equality." In that place, the <term>sesquialter proportion <note>A ratio of 3 to 2, which produces the interval of a musical Fifth.</note></term> is produced, precisely because the three parts of spirit or inferior matter of the middle heaven are related to those two parts of the solar sphere, and consequently produce the <term>consonance of the Fifth <note>original: "Diapente"</note></term>. For such is the difference between the Moon and the Sun: since between the curve of this heaven and the middle of the sphere of the Sun there are four intervals—namely, the spheres of the Moon, Mercury, and Venus, compared to whole tones, and the half-part of the solar sphere, which we have composed as a semitone. 

Now, since the <term>consonance of the Octave <note>original: "Diapaſon"</note></term> is constituted from the Fourth and the Fifth, it is necessary that this Octave consonance be produced there; and this is the more perfect consonance of matter, which can in no way acquire perfection unless it has filled its appetite with the solar form. Furthermore, although this middle heaven ends in its very heart (namely, the Sun) at a more perfect consonance of matter and begins the motion toward the formal Octave, yet in its concavity—both above the sphere of equality and below it—it sounds with nothing but the consonance of the Fifth. This interval is therefore more suitable to this place than other consonances because, just as the Fifth is less than perfect and placed midway between the perfect and imperfect, so also this heaven, although it is perfect and free from corruption, is nevertheless called "less perfect" in respect to the higher heaven, and holds a middle position between both heavens (namely, the perfect and the imperfect). 

Furthermore, the point of exact equality, which is the exact intersection of the two pyramids (namely, N.H. <note>These letters refer to labels on Fludd's famous "World Monochord" diagram.</note>), is the true boundary of the more perfect consonance, where there is such a union and embrace of matter with form that a separation and division can never occur. But truly, because the parts of the pyramids in the ethereal region—both above the sphere of intersection (namely, S. Q. O. P. and N. H.) and below (namely, R. M. Q. G. and N. H.)—are equal enough for perfection, such that the ethereal matter outside the sphere of the soul has enough form to fulfill its appetite (though not to the perfection of the solar sphere), for that reason the proportion joining the intervals is called a "perfect consonance," but less complete than that of the Sun, which the more excellent musicians have called the <term>Fifth <note>original: "Diapente"</note></term>. 

And this was the sole reason why some of the Philosophers distinguished its substance by the name of the <term>Fifth Essence <note>original: "Quinta eſſentia," or Quintessence.</note></term>, since its composition, in respect to both extreme heavens, participated more in the consonance of the musical Fifth. For descending from the sphere of the Sun, it stands in a 3:2 ratio to the lowest heaven, and ascending to the borders of the spiritual heaven, it is found under the same proportion. But the higher consonance differs from the lower because the latter refers to matter, while the former refers to form. Therefore, by as much as form is more excellent, by so much are the natures of its consonances more eminent than those of matter. Thus, just as an Octave was produced from the center to the sphere of the Sun, which was material, so also another Octave is raised upward from the head of the material pyramid to the supreme peak of the <term>Empyrean heaven <note>The highest, most spiritual part of the universe, associated with pure light and the divine.</note></term>, which exists as a spiritual Octave. For the higher part of the middle heaven, from the sphere of intersection...

...of the triangles, in which the center of the sun exists, reaching back toward the crystalline region, is more spiritual than the lower region because of its proximity to the <term>Empyrean heaven <note>The highest part of the universe, believed in ancient and medieval cosmology to be the realm of pure fire or light and the dwelling place of God.</note></term>. For this reason, the consonance produced there is also spiritual: for insofar as two portions of this superior light of the heavenly part are related to those three portions informing the matter of the Empyrean heaven, we find their arrangement governed by the <term>sesquialter proportion <note>A ratio of 3:2, which in music produces the interval of a Perfect Fifth.</note></term>. Indeed, from that part of the material pyramid contained between the dwelling of the sun and the borders of the Empyrean heaven, four intervals are drawn out due to the relationship and respect of each portion of light to the other, from which the spiritual <term>Fifth <note>original: "Diapente"</note></term> is composed and perfected. 

For from one portion of the solar sphere—specifically the upper part, extending from the intersection of the pyramidal sphere to the summit of the solar sphere—that interval is found which is compared to a <term>minor semitone <note>A small musical step; Fludd uses these intervals to map the distances between celestial bodies.</note></term>. Thus, the lower part of the same solar sphere retains the place of a minor semitone for the composition of the material Fifth, while the upper portion serves as the minor semitone for the formal <note>In Fludd’s philosophy, "formal" refers to the "form" or spiritual essence (light), as opposed to "material" (darkness/matter).</note> Fifth. However, the double excess by which two major semitones exceed as many minor ones—namely, two <term>commas <note>In music theory, a "comma" is a very small interval resulting from the difference between two other intervals; here Fludd uses it to describe the fine-tuning of the universe's scales.</note></term>—concurs in the exact composition of the "sphere of equality," in which the center of the solar body (the vehicle of the <term>soul of the world <note>original: "anima mundi"</note></term>) is suspended in balanced weights. 

Then, the sphere of Mars, and that of Jupiter, and finally even that superior sphere of Saturn, are counted as the three other intervals of the spiritual Fifth, which we have compared to whole tones, since each of these orbs has distinct natures and operations both in the middle heaven itself and also upon these lower things. Upon this spiritual consonance (which is less than perfect), therefore, is founded that most perfect consonance of all, which is called the spiritual <term>Octave <note>original: "Diapason"</note></term>. 

And at this point, a divine mystery will reveal itself to those who look diligently at the matter: just as the formal Octave is more excellent than the material one, so also its foundation is more excellent and far more noble, both in the proportion of its harmony and in the base of each. Therefore, just as the Earth is far more ignoble than the solar body, so also the material Octave and the consonances included within it are to be placed far behind those spiritual ones. For the Earth is the basis of the material <term>Fourths <note>original: "Diatessaron"</note></term> and Fifths that constitute the material Octave; but the solar orb itself is the foundation upon which the spiritual Fifth, Fourth, and Octave are raised. Furthermore, the fundamental consonance of the material Octave is the Fourth, which is the most imperfect of all concords; but the fundamental concord of the formal Octave is the Fifth, which is a truly perfect concord. From this, therefore, the grades of all material things are seen as if in a mirror, and from there also the spiritual ladder and order is made manifest to the eyes of the intellect. 

All generations <note>Referring to the birth or creation of physical things.</note> therefore take place beneath the sun and receive their power from the lower minor semitone of the solar sphere, which we called "material" above. But truly, all regenerations <note>Referring to spiritual rebirth or the refinement of the soul.</note> have their origin from the upper semitone of the solar orb, from which the sublimation toward the summit of spirituality occurs. Accordingly, those things which have not reached the summit of the solar orb cannot acquire the perfection of regeneration, nor become spiritual things from corporeal ones. Blessed, therefore, are the bodies to which it is permitted to reach such great height and dignity. 

But, to return to our purpose, those three parts of light informing a single part of the thinnest matter, related in a <term>sesquitertial proportion <note>A ratio of 4:3, which produces the musical interval of a Fourth.</note></term> to those four parts that are purely formal and constitute the complete form (in which there is no matter at all, such as is the nature of uncreated light), agree to constitute the spiritual Fourth. Its most subtle spirit—as if transformed and liberated from corporeal substance—is further divided into three intervals, which are attributed to the three <term>Hierarchies <note>The three traditional tiers of angels (Higher, Middle, and Lower).</note></term>. Of these, the two lower orbs are compared to whole tones, but the superior one is likened to a semitone; and insofar as the orders of the <term>Epiphany <note>Here referring to the manifestation of divine orders.</note></term>, and especially the <term>Seraphim <note>The highest order of angels, associated with light and fire.</note></term>, are said to be ministering to GOD himself, they extend themselves beyond the limits of matter...

...they extend, and because of the ineffable splendor which they receive from the divine presence, they are considered almost entirely formal <note>In this context, "formal" refers to the spiritual "Form" or essence of a thing, rather than its physical matter.</note>. From these joined harmonies, therefore, arises a spiritual <term>Diapason <note>An octave, which in Fludd’s system represents a 2:1 ratio of spiritual light to matter.</note></term>, the perfection of which cannot be grasped by human understanding, since it concludes in the triangular nature of the purest form. It is, therefore, the highest peak of all worldly perfection, height, and purity, beyond which nothing exists except the one and only GOD. 

And this same consonance is produced as much from the side of matter as from the side of form: For if two parts of the material substance giving rise to the ethereal heaven are related to that single corporeal mass communicating with the <term>Empyrean heaven <note>The highest, most spiritual realm of the universe, filled with pure light.</note></term>, a double proportion will result, in which the <term>Diapason</term> consonance consists. From this, it is certain that there is a quadruple proportion <note>A 4:1 ratio, which in music creates a double octave.</note> between the Earth and the convex surface of the spiritual world, since its semidiameter is composed of a double <term>Diapason</term>, which in the following demonstration we have imagined to be the string of our <term>Macrocôsmic monochord <note>A "Monochord" is a single-stringed instrument used since antiquity to demonstrate the mathematical ratios of musical intervals. Fludd uses it here as a metaphor for the entire universe (the Macrocosm).</note></term>. 

Hence it is manifest that, just as a musical instrument called a <term>Monochord</term>—if it is pressed in the middle between both ends—will sound a <term>Diapason</term> from that middle to each end; so also the action in the middle height of the world (or the middle of the semidiameter from the center to its circumference, which is especially revealed in the solar body) is perceived to give out a double <term>Diapason</term> of harmony at its center point. From this it shines most clearly that the perfection of the spiritual heaven extends itself to the center of the sun, and that the earth seeks and receives from that solar boundary all the perfection it possesses, of whatever kind and magnitude it may be. For this reason, the motion of light tends downward to impart perfection to the lower things; the boundary of this motion is in the Sun. Thus that solar body, which is the collector of the emitted rays of light, hurls them with the same proportion toward the surface of the earth. Therefore, the formal heaven gives form, which matter eagerly seizes.

We conclude, therefore, that the Sun is the GOD of nature—but a created one—which, by the power of spiritual harmony through the formal <term>Diapason</term> ordered proportionally with its intervals, receives all formal and lucid power from the greatest GOD of all, the supernatural uncreated creator; but the earth receives the influences of the same GOD through a material <term>Diapason</term>, and from there holds the same correspondence with the Sun as the Sun holds with the most high GOD. This is why it is said by the *Royal Psalmist* <note>King David, the traditional author of the Psalms; Fludd is referring to Psalm 19:4, "In them hath he set a tabernacle for the sun."</note> to have "set his tabernacle in the sun." 

And we have proven in this, our Music, that both <term>Diapente <note>The musical interval of a Perfect Fifth (3:2 ratio).</note></term> consonances are found in the intermediate part of the world, so much so that philosophers have called its substance the <term>Fifth essence <note>Also known as the "Quintessence" or Aether, a celestial substance believed to be different from the four earthly elements.</note></term> from that point. Both <term>Diatessaron <note>The musical interval of a Perfect Fourth (4:3 ratio).</note></term> consonances are established in the outer parts of the world, while the <term>Diapason</term> consonance is found in the Sun, and the <term>Disdiapason <note>A double octave (4:1 ratio), representing the greatest harmony.</note></term> is found in GOD himself. This, then, is the natural harmony of the universal machine, which no one until now, as far as I know, has explained so succinctly and clearly. ### TREATISE I. BOOK III.

Here, moreover, we have more precisely constructed the **world monochord** <note>original: *monochordum mundanum*. A single-stringed instrument used by ancient Greeks to demonstrate the mathematical ratios of musical intervals; Fludd uses it here as a metaphor for the harmony of the entire universe.</note> with its proportions, consonances, and intervals, and we have depicted its **mover** <note>original: *motorem*. Referring to the "Unmoved Mover" or God, who "tunes" the universe from outside the physical realm.</note> outside the world in this manner.

<image-desc>A complex engraving of the World Monochord. A single vertical string is stretched over the entire cosmos, from the Earth at the bottom to the highest spiritual realm at the top. At the very top, the hand of God emerges from a cloud to adjust the tuning peg of the instrument. 

**Structure of the Instrument:**
The neck is marked with musical letters (the gamut) from bottom to top: **Γ, A, B, C, D, E, F, G, a, b, c, d, e, f, g, aa**. 
The spaces between these are labeled as **Whole Tone** (*Tonus*) or **Semitone** (*Semitonium*).

**The Left Arcs (Mathematical Proportions):**
- **Quadruple Proportion** <note>original: *Proportio quadrupla*. A 4:1 ratio, representing two octaves; here encompassing the distance from Earth to the highest spiritual sphere.</note>
- **Double Proportion** <note>original: *Proportio dupla*. A 2:1 ratio, representing a single octave.</note>
- **Triple Proportion** <note>original: *Proportio tripla*. A 3:1 ratio.</note>
- **Sesquialter Proportion** <note>original: *Proportio Sesquialtera*. A 3:2 ratio, representing the musical interval of a fifth.</note>
- **Sesquitertial Proportion** <note>original: *Proportio Sesquitertia*. A 4:3 ratio, representing the musical interval of a fourth.</note>

**The Right Arcs (Musical Consonances):**
- **Formal Octave** <note>original: *Diapason formalis*. The upper "spiritual" octave located in the heavens.</note>
- **Formal Fourth** (*Diatessaron formalis*)
- **Formal Fifth** (*Diapente formalis*)
- **Octave plus a Fifth** (*Diapason cum diapente*)
- **Material Octave** <note>original: *Diapason materialis*. The lower "physical" octave located in the elemental realm.</note>
- **Material Fifth** (*Diapente materialis*)
- **Material Fourth** (*Diatessaron materialis*)

**The Levels of the Universe (Bottom to Top):**
1. **The Elements:** Earth, Water, Air, Fire.
2. **The Planets:** The Moon, Mercury, Venus, The Sun (at the center), Mars, Jupiter, Saturn.
3. **The Zodiac:** The starry firmament.
4. **The Super-Celestial Regions:** Labeled as the **Region of Epiphany** <note>original: *Epiphioniae regio*. The realm of divine manifestation or the Empyrean.</note>, divided into "Part of the Seat of Epiphany" and "Place of Epiphany."</image-desc>

<page-num>91</page-num>

<header>ON WORLDLY MUSIC.</header>

The superior **fourth** <note>original: *Diateſſaron*. A musical interval of a fourth, based on the ratio 4:3.</note> corresponds with the material **fourth** in this way: just as the lower fourth—though more perfect in relation to the earth placed beneath it, since it possesses more of the **form** <note>In Fludd’s Neoplatonic view, "form" represents spiritual light and activity, whereas "matter" represents darkness and passivity.</note> informing it than the earth does—is nevertheless found to be imperfect when compared to the higher things (that is, to the **fifth** <note>original: *Diapente*. A musical interval of a fifth, based on the ratio 3:2.</note> of its own nature), and can no more be compared to the substance of the ethereal heaven than the consonance of a fourth can be compared with that fifth; so also the formal fourth behaves in all respects. 

For if it is compared to the nature of the ethereal heaven, it is more perfect than that; indeed, it is even more perfect than its own formal fifth, insofar as the intervals of the latter are more material, while those of the fourth are more spiritual. But if it is compared to the pure Form above itself, the intervals of this consonance are still imperfect. Thus, this consonance is found there so that our intellect may see that this very region—even if it be the purest and most perfect of all the world's regions in our eyes—is nonetheless removed from the perfection of the **heaven of the Trinity** by an immense distance.

The logic of this **world monochord** <note>original: *monochordi mundani*</note> is proven by its effect, which aptly explains to us that pyramidal proportion and quality of worldly matter. Let there be constructed, therefore, a musical instrument built in the shape of a **pyramid** <note>original: *pyramidaliter*. Fludd often uses the "pyramid of light" and "pyramid of matter" to show how spirit and substance interpenetrate.</note>. From one point on one side of it, let a **brass string** <note>original: *chorda ænea*</note> be stretched to a point on the other side diametrically opposite to it. Then, let a second string likewise be extended from another point to its opposite, and then a third and fourth, proceeding in the same way with the rest until the thickness of the entire pyramidal instrument is filled with strings differing proportionally among themselves. 

We say, then, that these same strings, stretched with equal force, maintain the intervals of **Tones** and **Semitones**, so that from the base of the pyramid toward its **cone** <note>The "cone" refers to the pointed apex of the pyramid.</note>, through those musical scales (which we more rightly call intervals), we shall always ascend from the heavier sounds to the sharper ones until the cone is reached. Beyond this, it will be no more possible to ascend higher than it is to penetrate further by proceeding beyond an **arithmetical unit** <note>The number one.</note> or a **geometrical point**. This instrument is depicted in the following manner:

### ON THE MUSIC OF THE WORLD.

<margin>M 3</margin>

We can also draw out many hidden secrets from the nature of this instrument by carefully considering the proportions of its low and high sounds. For the string **A. B.**, if it were of the same dimension and length, it would sound in the very thin spirit of the higher heaven just as the string **C. D.** does in the airy medium of the lower region. That is, string **A. B.** would produce a sound just as high and excellent as string **C. D.** However, the reason string **A. B.** sounds so low in this airy medium is that the medium exists as something too thick and drowns out the clarity of the sound, and the moving soul <note>Fludd uses "anima" (soul) to refer to the animating force or energy that initiates a vibration.</note> is found to be too weak to refine that thick matter.

Note, therefore, that the "soul" of sounds in a string proceeds from the action of the agent; but the medium through which the dimensions of sound are produced is the substance of the air itself, which receives the modulations of the soul through vibration. For the same string, when stretched or loosened, produces various voices, just as is seen in the human voice, which produces various notes as it is raised through the same windpipe. <note>original: "tracheam arteriam"</note> Therefore, air is the medium and, as it were, the instrument in which Music is made. Just as a small instrument usually produces higher voices and a large one lower ones, so too thinned and subtle air produces finer sounds, and conversely, thick air produces lower ones. It is not the string itself, but its soul—sent into the medium or instrument by the vibration of the string—which produces the sounds. Nor does the principal power of sounds reside in the windpipe, but in the vital soul pushing the surrounding air. 

Indeed, just as a thicker medium refracts and hinders the rays of sight more the thicker it is (as can be seen in foggy and cloudy air), while in a clearer and thinner medium the effects of sight are sharper and more certain, so too a thick medium offers lower sounds to the hearing, while a thinner one offers higher sounds—so much so, that the thinner the medium, the more excellent the sounds produced.

Furthermore, we see that when the soul of the player mingles with the string by striking it vigorously, the string produces harder and more intense sounds through stronger vibration; but where that soul presses the string more weakly, it forces the string to emit a lower voice with a more relaxed vibration. This is also seen in the human voice: by a stronger impulse of the moving soul, more intense and clearer sounds are produced, while by a weaker impulse, more relaxed and obscure ones are made. Likewise, we see that wind blowing strongly through a crack in a house produces a higher and sharper sound, and yet that same wind blowing weakly immediately produces a lower sound through that same crack; this is also clearly demonstrated in the method of playing the trumpet. <note>original: "tuba"</note> 

The reason for this lies in the relaxed or intense powers of the moving soul, and consequently in the medium into which the soul enters through the vibration of the string or voice. For a weaker moving soul does not have enough power to refine the air so as to produce higher sounds; conversely, a motive soul acting powerfully produces higher and more sublime voices in the air because of its great force and virtue in "subtiliating" <note>To "subtiliate" (subtiliatio) means to make something thinner, finer, or more spiritual.</note> the air. Thus, the sounding soul expands itself more and more easily in the air and acts more sharply within it. 

The demonstration of this matter is seen in the stronger or weaker plucking and vibration of a string, and especially in the raising or lowering of the human voice. Similarly, a string achieves the same thing through tension and relaxation by another means: namely, when the motive soul contracts the string with greater force, it sounds higher, because according to the greater force and proportion of the contracting soul, it vibrates with greater violence and refines the medium with its vibrations, so that higher and sharper voices are produced. In the same way, a string of less bulk <note>original: "corpulentiæ"</note> requires less power from the motive soul to produce high and elevated sounds than a larger string does, because it makes its way into the air with an easier motion and lighter vibration due to the thinness of its body. Hence, it fills less space in the air, and yet its power extends widely and sharply. This is something a string of greater bulk cannot do, which is why it requires a stronger contraction of the soul. Is it not also the case...

<summary>Fludd explains that musical pitch is determined by the "soul" of the sound interacting with the density of the medium (air or spirit). He argues that higher pitches result from a stronger "soul" or thinner medium, comparing cosmic harmony to the physics of the human voice, wind, and stringed instruments.</summary>
<keywords>Cosmic Monochord, anima, subtiliation, pitch, vibration, acoustics, air density, human voice, Robert Fludd, Musica Mundana</keywords>

...the world-instrument, whose harmonies <note>original: "consonantiæ"</note> of the upper region are excellently high-pitched and elevated because of the strength of the divine soul residing there, or because of the abundance of loving light <note>Fludd refers to the Neo-Platonic idea of "Amor" or divine attraction as a physical, illuminating force.</note> predominating in that place, which perfectly thins its medium into the nature of spirituality? For this reason, just as that place is higher than others in the world, so its Music consists of the highest and most excellent harmonies. Indeed, because of the thinness of its medium and the most intense power of the radiant string, a string of length **A. B.** would produce a more excellent sound in that location than string **C. D.** does in our airy medium. From these points, it will be hidden from no one versed in Philosophy how apt this comparison of world-music with our instrument truly is; this will be described even more fittingly through the nature of the pipe <note>original: "fistulæ," referring to a flute or whistle-pipe.</note> in the following chapter.

## CHAPTER VI.

->*How everything aforesaid is more clearly demonstrated through the pipe, an instrument invented by art for Music?*<-

Behold before your eyes a notable demonstration hardly perceived by the inventor himself; through which it clearly appears that even great and huge secrets often lie hidden in common things. However, ordinary eyes by no means perceive the reason and root of these things, as they look at objects superficially rather than searching for their roots.

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s exploration of cosmic harmony by moving from the stringed monochord to the analogy of a wind instrument (the pipe). It then transitions into Chapter V, which discusses how these same musical proportions apply to the four elements—Earth, Water, Air, and Fire—based on their physical qualities of heat and cold.</meta>

ON WORLD MUSIC 95

For from this musical instrument, called a *pipe* <note>original: "fistula," referring here to a recorder or flute-like instrument</note>, the true proportion of the whole world is gathered. It is divided into three regions; the two lower ones have three holes denoting the beginning, middle, and end of each region; but the highest region, consisting of only one large hole, designates the nature of the super-celestial heaven, where every part is of the same condition, as if filled with divine unity. But just as this instrument in its own nature and by itself does not sound, move, or have any power without a soul to move it, so neither can the world or the parts of the world move or act by themselves without the stirring of the infinite Mind. 

Therefore, just as the supreme Mind, GOD, at the apex of the whole machine and, as it were, outside the outermost surface of the world, makes the joints of the world exhibit their music—heavier <note>meaning lower in pitch</note> in the lower part, and sharper and clearer as they approach closer to that very summit—so also, with the musician blowing life and motion from outside the pipe's structure at its summit, the further the holes are from the power of the blowing breath, the lower the voices they produce. However, the higher one ascends toward the bellows of that spirit <note>original: "spiritus folles," likely referring to the source of the breath or the lungs</note>, the more excellent the sounds that are returned. And just as that single hole, **A.** <note>This refers to a diagram in the original text where the top hole is labeled 'A'</note>, gives life and soul, as it were, to the other lower holes—since without it they return no voices—so also does the Empyrean heaven <note>The highest, most spiritual part of the universe in Renaissance cosmology</note> act upon all the lower spheres. O, how great and how celestial this contemplation would be in so trivial a thing, if it were considered diligently and somewhat more deeply by an intelligent mind!

---

## CHAPTER V.

->*On elemental music.*<-

It is established that there is also a certain harmony in the elemental part of the world. Its consonances proceed from proportions based more on the heat of the celestial fire—which is not of the true essence of light, but rather forced and external—than from that essential Light itself. For this reason, they produce symphonic compositions whose harmony, because of the scarcity of true luminous form within them, is not permanent, but rather transitory and vanishes with a certain sudden speed. 

However, we shall perceive more clearly than light itself, if we look more exactly and deeply into their natures and positions, that every element is disposed by a natural consent according to musical proportions, both in its own "heaven" and in any composite of that heaven. For it is certain that their matter or corpulence is either thicker (and consequently heavier and harder) or thinner (and therefore lighter and softer). This occurs according to the greater predominance of the coldness of the earth or the heat of fire within it. 

Since, therefore, coldness has dominion in the earth alone (where, because of the absolute absence of fiery heat, those four quarters <note>Fludd used a system of "four quarters" to quantify the primary qualities of elements</note> of coldness are present, which constitute the integral nature of earth); yet the element of water possesses no more than three quarters of coldness joined with one of fire (which happens to it because of its participation with the middle sphere of humidity); similarly, the middle sphere of humidity contains only two portions of coldness and just as many of heat; and finally, the element of air has acquired only one portion of humidity, while it embraces three quarters of the quality of the neighboring element of fire; it is necessary that earth be denser, heavier, and harder than water, and water than that counterfeit <note>original: "spuria," implying a mixed or secondary state</note> sphere of equality, and that sphere, finally, than the air, which is the upper part of that humid sphere;

<meta>catchword: CA-</meta>

And consequently, the coldness, density, and weight of the earth relate to those of water in a **sesquialter** <term>sesquialtera: a ratio of 3:2, corresponding to the musical interval of a perfect fifth</term> proportion; and these same dispositions of water relate to the middle sphere of humidity in a **sesquialter** ratio; finally, the condition of that matter relates to that of air in a **double** proportion <note>A 2:1 ratio, or an octave</note>. Therefore, a **fourth** <term>Diatessaron: the interval of a perfect fourth (4:3 ratio)</term> is found from the convex surface of the base <note>the Earth</note> to that of water, and from that of water to the other sphere of humidity, a **fifth** <term>Diapente: the interval of a perfect fifth (3:2 ratio)</term> is measured; from which all the way to the concave surface of fire, an **octave** <term>Diapason: the interval of an octave (2:1 ratio)</term> extends, such that between fire and earth a **double octave** <term>Disdiapason: an interval of two octaves (4:1 ratio)</term> is produced. 

For in the sphere of false and corruptible equality, ascending, a **fifth** ends after a **fourth**, which joined together constitute an **octave**. Furthermore, descending from the sphere of fire, in which four degrees of adventitious <note>external or added</note> heat reside, these combined with the three degrees of air produce a formal **fourth**; and the aforementioned three degrees of air, joined to those two of the sphere of humidity, make a **fifth**: which two joined together generate a formal **octave**; and again, those two quarters of heat of the sphere of equality, coupled with the single degree of air, bring forth another **octave**. 

Whence in the element of air, the **octave** of matter symphonically agrees with the **fourth** of form; and in the middle sphere of humidity, the **fifth** of matter agrees with the **fifth** of form, which there also resonate as an **octave**; hence in that place is a more perfect seat of the elements and a temperament <note>a balanced mixture</note> tending toward justice; and in the element of water, an **octave** of form is heard to resonate by the ears of reason with a **fourth**. 

Therefore, these elements, disposed within physical bodies according to these musical proportions, produce the best temperaments in them. But where they are poorly ordered—that is, by a less harmonic consensus—there they cause **dyscrasia** <term>discrasia: a medical term for an imbalance of the bodily humors</term> and intemperance, stir up strife and discord, produce diseases and sicknesses in bodies, and finally lead to most certain corruption, unless they are corrected more speedily. Moreover, the proportions of this music are described in the following way.

TREATISE I. BOOK III.

# CHAPTER VI.

->*On the intervals of any element, and on the consonances that arise from*<-
->*them, by which the elements peacefully concur toward*<-
->*composition.*<-

<image-desc>Ornamental woodcut drop cap 'I' decorated with foliage and floral motifs within a square frame.</image-desc>
In our general treatise on Music, we proved that the **perfect fourth** <note>original: Diatessaron</note>, which is an imperfect consonance, is found only because of its impurity in every sphere of the lowest region of the world. In this place, however, we shall provide a most clear demonstration of this matter, through which we will evidently show that every elemental sphere of this region is composed of the entire consonance of a perfect fourth. Indeed, unless the sphere of friendship—namely that of **imperfect equality** <note>original: æqualitatis spuriæ; Fludd refers to the middle point of the cosmos where spirit and matter meet in an "imperfect" or "false" balance compared to the divine realm</note>—were placed in the middle of the extremes, it would be impossible for one of these elements to correspond to another in symphonic melody. By reason of this middle sphere, all the aforementioned consonances are rendered both less perfect and more perfect, as appears most clearly in the following demonstration. 

There, we have outlined three regions of the terrestrial globe: one central, which is simply cold and dry and excelling in weight, whose interval we have likened to a **whole tone**; another intermediate region, specifically between the central and the superficial, which we call the **mineral region**, verging more toward the simplicity of the center than to the nature of water, and whose interval we have compared to a **whole tone**, from which we find a terrestrial **ditone** <note>An interval of two whole tones, equivalent to a major third</note>; and finally, the superficial region, which communicates more with water than with the purest nature of the center. From its circuit, springs burst forth and plants rise up, whose nourishment is water and air; and we have assigned a **semitone** <note>A half-step interval</note> to the space of this terrestrial region. From these terrestrial intervals, the consonance of the **perfect fourth** <note>Diatessaron</note> arises.

Similarly, we have demonstrated that any sphere of the transparent elements is distinguished into three regions. Thus, the lower region of **water** is muddier and more filled with dregs, communicating with the nature of earth, to whose interval we have attributed a **whole tone**. Then its middle region, imbued with the saltiness of the earth, is like "burnt water," whose subtler parts are extracted and evaporate, joining with fresh water. Hence, this portion of water participates in both extremes—namely, the nature of terrestrial dregs and the nature of fresh water—wherefore its interval is also compared to a **whole tone**. The upper region, however, is fresh water, participating in the purer region of the false orb and the cloudy nature of thick air, joining itself with the sphere of **imperfect equality** <note>æqualitatis spuriæ</note>. We call it such in respect to the sphere of most pure and incorruptible equality, which we have named the **soul of the world**. Thus, from these final intervals of the sphere of water, a **minor third** <note>original: Semiditonus</note> arises, which, combined with the tone of its lowest region, composes another **perfect fourth**. 

Furthermore, ascending higher to the sphere of **air**, we find there also three regions: the lowest, somewhat inclined toward the nature of water, we compared to a **whole tone**; the middle also to a **whole tone**; and the highest, more occupied by fire, we compared to a **semitone**. These intervals joined together constitute a third **perfect fourth**. Finally, the sphere of **fire** is also divided into three regions: to the lowest, familiar with air, we attribute a **whole tone**; and likewise to the middle, where a simpler portion of the element of fire exists, we give another **whole tone**. But the space of that highest region, participating with the **Quintessence** <note>The "fifth element" or ether that makes up the heavens</note>, is assigned a lesser **semitone**...

<meta>catchword: lesser</meta>

<summary>Fludd explains that each of the four elements (Earth, Water, Air, Fire) is divided into three sub-regions. These sub-regions correspond to musical intervals (tones and semitones) that together form a "perfect fourth" (Diatessaron), providing the harmonic structure that allows the physical world to exist in balance.</summary>

# ON MUNDANE MUSIC

<meta>sig: N 2</meta>

...we referred to as the lesser. From these things it is manifest that in the pure natures of the elements, no consonance is found except for that imperfect one <note>The "imperfect" consonance Fludd refers to here is the **perfect fourth**, which he considers less stable than the octave or fifth in a cosmic sense</note>. The logic for finding these intervals is this: in every degree, the more one ascends from the earthly nature toward the fiery nature, the more the matter is altered by the rising, gradually increasing operation of fire. Since it is certain that these operations are found sensibly in three places of the said elements, we have therefore taken the intervals of our musical consonances from these degrees of alteration.

Therefore, so that all these **fourth** <term>Diatessaron: A musical interval encompassing four notes of the scale; in Fludd's system, it represents the span of a single element's domain</term> consonances may act naturally and peacefully with one another, it is necessary to link the two corporeal ones (which belong to the visible elements <note>Earth and Water</note>) to those two of the invisible elements <note>Air and Fire</note>. We shall clearly demonstrate that this connection arises from the **sphere of spurious equality** <note>original: æqualitatis spuriæ; Fludd's term for the middle region of the cosmos where the "pyramid of light" and "pyramid of matter" meet in equal measure, though it is "spurious" or "false" compared to the pure unity of God</note>, to which sphere we have assigned the interval of a **whole tone**, which is indeed the most perfect interval. If this interval is joined to the **fourth** of the sphere of water, there is produced by this kind of link and coupling a less perfect consonance; when this is united with the earthly **fourth**, it gives birth to that most perfect corporeal consonance called the **octave** <term>Diapason: The interval of an eighth; here it represents a completed cycle of elemental transformation</term>.

Similarly, ascending again to the invisible elemental regions: if the **fourth** of the aerial sphere is joined with the perfect **whole tone** interval of the sphere of spurious equality, immediately a less perfect aerial consonance will be brought forth. When this is joined with the fiery **fourth**, it will constitute an incorporeal—that is, invisible—**octave**. In this way, fire will communicate with air in an **octave** composed of fire’s **fourth**, the aerial **fourth**, and the interval of the middle sphere. Likewise, fire in the air communicates with water in an **octave** composed of the **fourth** of air (animated by fire through the preceding **octave**), the **whole tone** of the middle sphere, and the **fourth** of water—for these indeed constitute another **octave**. Finally, fire in the air and air in the water (as if in vehicles of the invisible elements) communicate with the earth through a third and lowest **octave**, composed of the **tone** or interval of the middle sphere, the **fourth** of the sphere of water, and the earthly **fourth**; all of these joined together give birth to an **octave**.

And by this path it is most clearly demonstrated by what chains the elements are linked to one another, and why fire is more present in air, less in water, and least of all—or in the most meager portion—in the earth. <margin>Fire</margin> From this it is also declared how one element is, as it were, a matrix, vehicle, and receptacle for another—namely, for its nearest superior: as air is to fire, water to air, and earth to water. For this reason, earth is the receiver of all elements; hence it is not without merit that the earth is called by Philosophers the "mother of all elements." This, then, is that **elementary harmony**, by the virtue of whose consonances the parts are connected in the mixture of a composition most exactly and according to weight, and are peacefully preserved within it.

We conclude, therefore, that the true way of connecting the proportions of contrary elements to each other in a composition is the **sphere of equality**. Its interval, the **whole tone**, is that natural bond filling the space between the superior and invisible elements and the inferior and visible ones; without this, the four elements can in no way concur to form any natural composition.

<summary>Fludd describes the musical "ligaments" that bind the four elements together, arguing that the harmony of the world is maintained by octaves and fourths centered around a middle "sphere of equality." He explains the hierarchical relationship of the elements, where each serves as a "receptacle" for the one above it, with Earth acting as the mother and receiver of all.</summary>

ON WORLD MUSIC.
N 3

## CHAPTER VII.

->*On world music, and the application and action of its consonances,*<-
->*as infused from the ethereal spheres of heaven into these elementary ones.*<-

JUST AS in vocal or instrumental music, theoretical study is not enough unless it is translated into practice and its use is recognized through practical experience; so too in this harmony of the world, our present discourse might perhaps be considered a fiction, unless we extend it—to the best of our ability—to a practice familiar not only to the intellect but to the senses themselves. We shall therefore discuss, first, by what proportions and consonances <term>consonantia: a harmonious sounding together of intervals</term> each sphere of the world pours its harmony into another; and thereafter, in the second place, we shall declare the nature of worldly discords.

Since the space of the transparent region <note>the atmosphere or lower air</note> relates to the earth in a <term>sesquitertia: a 4:3 ratio, corresponding to the musical interval of a Fourth</term> proportion, it follows that accidental fire corresponds to it, though by an imperfect consonance; for in respect to dryness alone—which is an accidental quality shared by both fire and earth—it exists in such a concord with the earth. But the reason why this region generally accords with the earth lies in the abundance of "corpulence" <note>physical density or matter</note> which it originally received from the earth. Thus, the intervals of the transparent region serve to connect this more material region to its foundation with an imperfect consonance (according to the nature of the place, of course), so that a symphony may arise from the fit joining together of its spheres.

But if we ascend into the first step of the ethereal region, we shall find that the sphere of the **Moon** has a relationship to the earth in a <term>sesquialtera: a 3:2 ratio, corresponding to the musical interval of a Fifth</term> proportion. In respect to this proportion, the earth "tastes" of the **Fifth Essence** <note>original: "Quinta essentia"; the celestial ether, a substance purer than the four elements</note>, and by this same proportion, a worldly melody is infused into the earth, composed with it from a less perfect consonance. For insofar as four things are found between the earth and the *sphere of the Moon*—namely water, air, and the sphere of the Moon, which carry the whole tones, and the sphere of fire, which takes the place of a Semitone—the Moon will sound a <term>Diapente: a Perfect Fifth</term> with the earth, and a <term>Diatessaron: a Perfect Fourth</term> with the water. This consists, as has been said, of two tones (namely the air and the sphere of the Moon) and one Semitone (namely fire). And this is the reason why the Moon acts so constantly, strongly, and vigorously upon these lower things, and especially upon the earth, through its influences. 

Similarly, the *sphere of Mercury* will resonate a Fifth into the sphere of water. From this, therefore, come the floods of waters and the disturbances of the seas, because of the thin and "windy" Mercurial spirit hidden within them; likewise, by this means and with the help of the Moon, the ebb and flow of the tide occurs. Furthermore, by a similar consonance, **Venus** relates to the sphere of air; she possesses a mild and sweet symphony in the air because of the exact concord of her proportions. Hence the air retains within itself the same qualities that Venus has—namely heat and moisture—which are always favorable to animal life; indeed, they even serve the plants themselves in the place of their "radical moisture" <note>original: "humidi radicalis"; the vital fluid thought to sustain the life and growth of organisms</note> along with a spirit proper to them. Finally, the lowest part of the sphere of the **Sun** scatters its influences into the sphere of fire; these are carried down together into these lower regions and contribute to the multiplication and perfection of plants by the virtue of the Fifth consonance. 

But, to arrive at the powers and faculties of the more perfect consonance which is found between the earth and the "sphere of equality," it is certain that all the perfections of the life of every terrestrial animal proceed from the intervals of this consonance—namely, the growth of corporeal matter and of that lively form...

Fludd argues that natural phenomena like tides and plant growth are the "practice" or physical result of these heavenly harmonies.</summary>
