<meta>This page continues the discussion from page 101 regarding how the celestial proportions of the "World Monochord" manifest as physical influences. Having established the mathematical ratios, Fludd now explains why certain planets are considered "benefic" or "malefic" based on whether their musical intervals harmonize with the qualities of the four elements (Earth, Water, Air, and Fire).</meta>

### TREATISE I. BOOK III.

...all vital actions and motions; hence, for the exact perfection of material things, this is the most effective of all the previously mentioned consonances. **Mars**, although in its own nature and by itself it is very useful, nevertheless, because its *Diapason* <term>original: *Diapason*; the musical interval of an octave, representing a 2:1 ratio</term> extends to the sphere of water, this star is unlucky for lower living things. Because its nature is contrary to water <note>Mars is traditionally hot and dry, while water is cold and moist</note>, it produces operations that are rebellious and malicious toward life in these lower regions, and it is held by Astrologers to be the **lesser misfortune** <note>original: *infortunium minori*; in astrology, the "Lesser Malefic"</note>. 

**Jupiter**, however, immediately embracing the sphere of **Mars** within its own orbit, is benevolently received by the sphere of air, which is wonderfully delighted by Jupiter's melody consisting of a double proportion <note>a 2:1 ratio or octave</note>. For they agree in every way in the proportion of their natures and qualities—except that air partakes of the lower nature while the sphere of **Jove** <note>another name for Jupiter</note> partakes of the higher; for both are hot and moist. It is for this reason, therefore, that they contribute especially to animal life. That aerial orbit receives its benevolent disposition from **Jupiter**, who is therefore called the **greater fortune** <note>original: *fortunium majus*; the "Greater Benefic"</note> by Astrologers, and from **Venus**, who is called the **lesser fortune** by the same. 

Finally, the last interval of this region is governed by **Saturn**, which stands in a double proportion to the interval of the element of fire, in which the *Diapason* <term>octave</term> consists. But in truth, because the nature of **Saturn** and that of the fiery sphere are contrary <note>Saturn is cold and dry, while fire is hot and dry</note>, such a melody—though it may perhaps benefit one or two species—is nonetheless turbid and unpleasant, and indeed malicious to the animal kingdom. For when the cold nature of **Saturn** terminates in that heat of fire, the quality of heat is repugnant to coldness. Thus, even though that quantitative proportion is consonant on the "world monochord," the contrary qualities of the giver and the receiver produce no small *dyscrasia* <term>original: *discrasiam*; a medical or philosophical term for a disharmonious mixture of humors or qualities</term> in that concord through their opposite resistance. Living creatures, because of the weakness of their forms, very easily feel and perceive this. It is for this reason, therefore, that **Saturn** is called the **greater misfortune** by Astrologers, by the same reasoning that they call **Mars** the **lesser misfortune**.

From these points it is therefore clear that the **Sun** and **Moon** transmit a general "World Music" into these lower regions through their influences, while the other stars transmit a more particular music. Furthermore, some of these stars send their melodies into these lower parts auspiciously, while others do so inauspiciously. Those that flow in auspiciously do so clearly, purely, and simply; but those that are inauspicious send their natures down into these lower parts through the intervals of the *Diapasontis* <term>the octave</term> in a fragmented or interrupted way, which acts quite disruptively upon all bodies unless they are very well disposed.

Finally, the natures of the stars also concur with the natures of the Planets themselves by a certain harmonic consensus. Thus **Leo** <note>the zodiac sign the Lion</note>, on account of the singular vitality of its life, receives the **Sun** as its Lord; hence **Leo** is, as it were, in *unison* <term>sounding at the same pitch or quality</term> with the nature of the Sun. So also is **Cancer** with the **Moon**, **Aries** with **Mars**, and so on for the others. For every "house" <note>zodiac sign</note> of the Planets must be conformable with the nature of its Lord and be as a unison—that is, it must resonate with its Lord in the same sound. From this, they flow in with the same manner and the same consonances as their Lords.

Lastly, as pertains to the *Diatessaron* <term>original: *Diatessaron*; the musical interval of a Fourth</term> of the superior region, it must be understood that the higher Hierarchy pours its virtues into the middle hierarchy, and the middle into the lowest. This lowest hierarchy communicates with the ethereal heaven, which expects not only its splendor from that fiery heaven, but also the messages and commands of GOD from His creatures, and executes them once received. These operations occur by the virtue of spiritual consonances, in which is contained that fiery love which fills the entire Empyrean heaven and extends all the way to the center of the Sun. By the consonances of this love, all rational spiritual beings descend into well-disposed bodies by means of that ethereal vehicle. Since we shall discourse more extensively upon these matters in our books on the **Macrocosm**, we shall omit them in this place so that we may more rightly proceed to more natural subjects.

ON WORLD MUSIC.

# CHAPTER VIII.

->*On the practice of elemental melody and its effect on both simple and composite bodies, and on its discordance and strife.*<-

Just as the more subtle dispositions and natures of the higher realms are poured into the lower bodies, so it is also necessary that bodies be composed from the region of the "material pyramid" of substance (which is divided into elements) in order to suitably receive such influences. And indeed, this material composition derives its essential form not from the elements themselves, but from heaven itself. I have experienced this with my own eyes: for I have separated the four distinct elements from a living thing; then, at the very last, I perceived and saw with the clearest vision the "life" of the thing. I recognized this life to be from heaven, from the Fifth Essence <note>original: *Quinta essentia*; the "aether" or spirit that transcends the four physical elements</note> and the solar nature, once the four lower elements had been set aside.

Our intention here, therefore, is to treat the proportions and those consonances by which a fit connection of one element with another occurs in the mixing and composition of worldly bodies. Thus, in **Elemental Music**, Earth relates to the sphere of Water as 4 to 3. This is because there exist in Earth four parts of "fourth-degree coldness" compared to the three parts found in Water. This is because a single part of the element of Fire (along with the tip of the "pyramid of light") exists within the sphere of Water. Indeed, if more parts of heat existed in Water, it would be of a thinner substance and would even be turned into Air; for that reason, its sphere would neither remain closest to the Earth, nor would it be burdened by such great heaviness or weight. 

That Water contains some heat—that is, an igneous form—is testified by its transparency and fluidity, as was said before in its proper place. Because of this **sesquitertial proportion** <note>original: *proportionem sesquitertiam*; a ratio of 4:3</note>, Earth agrees with Water; however, this combination of elements is imperfect and less absolute because the moist parts of one are contrary to the dry parts of the other. Nevertheless, they harmonize with one another through the consonance of the <term>Diatessaron</term> <gloss>the musical interval of a fourth</gloss>, because they agree in the active qualities that predominate within them. 

So too, Earth relates to the "sphere of spurious equality" <note>Fludd’s term for a transitional zone where material and formal qualities are balanced 2:2</note>—in which there are two material parts and just as many of fire—in a **double proportion** <note>a 2:1 ratio</note>, namely, as 4 parts of earth to 2 parts of that sphere; for the terrestrial nature produced these moist parts by the power of light joined with it, being dissolved and refined. 

Similarly, Water relates to that sphere of equality as 3 to 2, from which the **sesquialter proportion** <note>original: *proportio sesquialtera*; a ratio of 3:2</note> is born. And thus we have obtained the *Diatessaron* (4:3), <term>Diapente</term> <gloss>the musical interval of a fifth (3:2)</gloss>, and <term>Diapason</term> <gloss>the musical interval of an octave (2:1)</gloss> of the terrestrial pyramid. 

In the same way, the sphere of equality relates to the sphere of Air by comparing the two igneous parts of the sphere of equality to the three portions of Air, from which an "igneous" *Diapente* arises. The three igneous parts of Air compared to the four parts of the sphere of Fire produce the consonance of a *Diatessaron* <note>The Latin text here says "Diapason," but the 3:4 ratio mathematically describes a Diatessaron/Fourth</note>. 

Therefore, Earth agrees with Water in a *Diatessaron*, and with the sphere of equality in a *Diapason*. Water agrees with the sphere of spurious equality in a *Diapente*, and the sphere of spurious equality with Air in a *Diapason* (since they exist in a double proportion). So also Fire agrees with Air in a *Diatessaron*, and with the sphere of equality in a *Diapason*. Air agrees with the sphere of equality in a *Diapente*, and the sphere of equality with Water in a double proportion or *Diapason*. 

Thus, the two visible elements [Earth and Water] best concur for composition through the bond of the consonance of the *Diatessaron*, and the two invisible elements [Air and Fire] mix themselves with them by the power of the embrace which they have with the corporal proportions. For Fire is referred to

### TREATISE I. BOOK III.

[Fire connects to] air through the <term>consonance of a Fourth</term> <note>original: *Diateſſaron*; an interval of four notes</note>, and air to the sphere of equality through a <term>Fifth</term> <note>original: *Diapente*; an interval of five notes</note>, and both fire and air are joined to the sphere of equality in the proportion of an <term>Octave</term> <note>original: *Diapaſon*</note>. This sphere of equality links these invisible elements—which are chained together by formal musical harmonies—with the visible elements through bodily bonds or consonances. Similarly, air is joined to earth in a quadruple proportion, and consequently fire (which is already glued to air by the bond of a Fourth) is usually united with earth and water. However, they are joined to one another in such a way that the proper "seat" of the consonances and the legitimate order are observed, lest discord and strife arise between them. For this reason, bodily consonances ought to concern the more bodily elements, and fiery harmonies should concern the fiery elements.

The reason for this is that both the earthier, visible region and the fiery, invisible region are bounded and perfected by the most perfect consonance. Thus, the limit of the <term>material pyramid</term> <note>Fludd’s concept of a triangle representing the "bulk" of matter reaching up from Earth</note> or the visible realm is contained within a "bodily" Octave; likewise, the fiery pyramid is within its own "less-bodily" Octave. But truly, insofar as both Octaves are joined by the power of the middle sphere, to that extent the physical elements are joined and linked to the invisible ones. Therefore, no single proportion should look beyond its own region; otherwise, discord would arise in place of concord. 

For example: fire consists of 4 parts of heat, and water of three parts of coldness. Although there is a "four-to-three" proportion here, it sits poorly between these bodies because of their qualities; it creates a progression from the fiery kingdom to the physical one, whose natures are entirely opposite. Similarly, earth relates to air as 4 to 3, but they can in no way agree for the same reason. Instead, fire is enclosed in air, and through the air (by means of the sphere of equality) it passes. This sphere of equality should be understood as the medium for joining fiery and airy forms with watery and earthy bodies, just as the ethereal spirit is the medium that joins the soul to the lower body. Thus the air, along with its "fiery soul," is grasped by the water, and that water—by virtue of the consonance of a Fourth—is most easily and naturally joined with the earth. This is the harmony by which the composition of mixed, "sublunary" <note>Existing beneath the moon; i.e., the earthly realm of change and decay</note> bodies is achieved.

However, these types of bonds are dissolved by strife and discord. For if the proportion of <term>Martial fire</term> <note>Fire dominated by the qualities of the planet Mars—excessive heat and aggression</note> is increased beyond the proper nature of its consonance, it very quickly absorbs the moisture of its "airy vehicle." Having violated the harmony of the fiery Fourth, it breaks out of its prison into the watery region and causes war instead of peace within the composite body. From this, desperate and incurable diseases arise. That fiery nature, violently penetrating through the water and earth and breaking out from them, snatches away the "celestial soul" (which is the fire’s closest kin in the body due to the shared nature of the heavens and the sphere of fire). In this way, the composite body is dissolved, suffers corruption, and is reduced step-by-step into its primary matter. 

The same thing happens when <term>Saturnine earth</term> <note>Earth dominated by the qualities of the planet Saturn—extreme cold, dryness, and leaden heaviness</note> destroys the airy heat and moisture with its own coldness and dryness, so that it may find a way, alongside water, to extinguish the fire and suffocate the life of the thing. But of this worldly strife we shall say more 

->in the following chapter.<-

<summary>Fludd explains that the four elements are held together in a healthy state by specific musical intervals (fourths, fifths, and octaves). When these harmonic proportions are disrupted—such as by excessive "Martial" heat or "Saturnine" cold—the elemental bonds break, leading to disease, the escape of the soul, and the decay of the body.</summary>

->ON WORLD MUSIC.<-

# CHAPTER IX.
### *On worldly discords.*

AFTER we have discussed worldly concord, it follows that we should provide a discourse on its discords and the reasoning behind them. From what has been stated previously, it has been clearly demonstrated that <term>Chaos</term> <note>The formless state of the universe before creation</note>, before the completion of the world, was composed of litigious proportions, and that in that crude mass there was no concord, according to that saying of the Poet: *Cold things fought with hot, and moist things with dry.* <note>original: "Frigida pugnabant calidis, humentia siccis." A famous line from Ovid’s Metamorphoses describing Chaos.</note> From these causes, therefore—the hostile incursions of the <term>Macrocosm</term> <note>The "great world" or universe</note> and its peaceful friendship—various effects are produced. For in the beginning, when heat was born, its opposite, cold, also arose; between these two, while they waged war against each other, moisture intervened as a third thing, as a certain compound of those two primary qualities. Yet, it does not entirely agree with either of those extremes, because it participates in both, and thus it holds the middle place between them. From this, then, dryness, never departing from either extreme, has a perpetual war with its contrary, moisture, so that it pursues the other with an indeterminable enmity.

Therefore, interrupting these hostile collisions in the nature of the Macrocosm, the infinite nature <note>referring to the Divine Creator</note> made distinct and balanced things out of indistinct ones. Once this was done, the light things flew up into the higher regions, while the heavy things settled beneath the moist sand, so that each of them was carried by a fiery spirit into its own proper and proportional place, to speak in the words of <term>Trismegistus</term> <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure to whom many mystical and scientific works were attributed</note>. These things being so disposed, every <term>Planet</term> in the ethereal heaven was found in its own proper <term>Exaltation</term> <note>A specific sign of the Zodiac where a planet is thought to have its greatest influence</note>. Indeed, they were so situated in the celestial Zodiac that those of contrary conditions were placed furthest apart from one another. Hence, the **Sun** found its exaltation in **Aries** at the first instant of its creation; the **Moon** had its exaltation nearest to it, because the Sun granted it something as if from his own substance, and she is like a wife or female, and she possessed **Taurus**. Similarly, **Jupiter**, being most friendly to the Sun and Moon, was exalted in **Capricorn** <note>original: *Capro*; standard astrology usually places Jupiter's exaltation in Cancer, but Fludd specifies Capricorn here</note>; **Mercury** in **Virgo**; but **Saturn**, being most malicious toward the Sun, was exalted in **Libra**, standing apart from him by the entire semi-diameter of the whole heaven. Finally, **Mars**, exalted in **Capricorn**, was equally distant from both the Sun and Saturn. And so all these creatures of heaven were arranged so that no manifest strife existed between their qualities.

But truly, after they fell into <term>malevolent aspects</term> <note>Geometric angles between planets, such as squares or oppositions, believed to cause tension</note> through their natural motion—which commonly happens every year from their very origin—they produce rebellious effects in these lower regions. By these, these lower compositions are either utterly destroyed and uprooted, or wonderfully afflicted and weakened. This occurs because of the malevolent influences of the planets when they look upon each other poorly; these influences are more depraved the more depraved that planet is which, at the time of that aspect, is stronger and more powerful than the other. Now, they call **Saturn** and **Mars** "depraved" planets; for they naturally extend their <term>double proportions</term> <note>musical intervals of an octave, here used to describe the "reach" of a planet's power</note> tending downward into spheres that are incongruous and contrary to their natures. From this, they create a corrupted harmony, and produce effects in these lower things (especially in living beings) that are accidental rather than natural, as was said before. For all planets are good in themselves, but their influences—even though they too are good in themselves, especially those of these two for the reasons already alleged—work evil effects in bodies that are not aptly disposed to receive their influences and natures. Thus, truly, the fault is not in the giver, but in the receiver. This is also seen to be confirmed by **Iamblichus** <note>A Neoplatonist philosopher</note> in these words: *Celestial things are not evil, although here they are received as evil; for all the powers of the celestial beings descend from there as good, but in this mixture of contraries they are changed; and therefore the quality which causes harm on earth is something other than that which...*

<sig>O</sig>
<meta>catchword: which</meta>

<summary>This chapter explains the origins of discord in the universe, shifting from the initial chaos of warring elements to an ordered astrological system where planets were originally placed to minimize conflict. It concludes by explaining that "evil" astrological influences are not inherent to the stars but result from how their power interacts with the imperfect, mixed nature of the earthly realm.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of "Worldly Music" (Musica Mundana), shifting from the ideal harmony of the spheres to the practical reasons why discord, disease, and weather phenomena occur on Earth. It explains how celestial influences interact with the "disposition" of matter.</meta>

# TRACTATE I. BOOK III.

...which had reached thus far from heaven; the reason for which is confirmed by the effects of our philosophy. For first, speaking universally, the sphere of water receives the influences of <term>Mars</term> <note>Associated with heat and conflict</note> through the consonance of the <term>Diapason</term> <note>The interval of an octave, considered the most perfect consonance</note>, but the contrary nature of that sphere receives them differently. Whence, fire invisibly fights with water, and the water, by resisting, is unnaturally disturbed. In the same way, <term>Saturn</term> <note>Associated with cold and restriction</note> emits its influences in the same proportion into fire, which, in the reception of them, both acts and suffers in specific ways. 

We see that the power of Saturn is restrictive and, by accident, frequently causes harm—specifically when it is received by colder matter. In that case, it is harmful because it is received mostly by way of freezing. Similarly, we know that the power of Mars is motific <note>Moving or driving</note>, and we see that its influences are harmful when they are received by hotter matter; then it harms because it is received through intense heat. These harms do not occur because of a wicked operation of the celestial influences themselves, but because of the poor disposition of the matter. 

Similarly, the density and rarity of matter can be at fault. We also sometimes find the weakness of the subject to be the cause, so that even good rays and influences produce bad effects in it. For it happens sometimes that the light of the <term>Sun</term> offends the eyes of the weak, and its summer heat and rays injure those who are stronger or more <term>bilious</term> <note>One of the four temperaments, associated with yellow bile and a hot, dry nature</note>. 

So too, we find that the influence of <term>Mercury and Venus</term> sometimes stirs up blasts and winds in the air. Experience also teaches that in a bilious nature mixed with superfluous <term>phlegmatic humor</term> <note>original: "humore pituitoſo"; the cold, moist humor</note>, foul ulcers are born through the influences of Mars and the Moon when they are in a bad <term>aspect</term> <note>An astrological position</note>—such as a square, conjunction, or opposition—because the fiery nature of Mars acts constantly with the watery nature of the <term>Moon</term> toward corruption, until purulent matter is produced. 

In the same way, Mars and Saturn, finding contrary subjects in a single man or animal, affect them mostly with plague, putrid fever, or similar diseases; meanwhile, other animals of the same species—due to a lack of matter contrary to the Martian and Saturnine nature—receive the influx of these planets freely, without danger and without any manifest alteration. From this it is clear that although all heavenly influences are inherently healthy, the perversity or weakness of the subject is sometimes unable to tolerate the natures of the higher powers. 

For the same reason, in the <term>Elementary region</term> <note>The Earthly realm of the four elements</note>, after each of its natures (which were once indistinct and disturbed) had been reduced to harmonious proportions by the rules of concord, the earth obtained a place further away from fire, as if from its opposite. Similarly, water was placed between air and earth so that they would not fight again; indeed, a "sphere of false equality" <note>A transitional zone where opposing qualities are balanced</note> placed itself between the coldness of water and the heat of air. Thus, by reason of this middle sphere, which participates equally in both, peace is maintained between these elemental spheres that are otherwise opposed to one another, as was said above. 

Therefore, wherever these bonds of peace are broken in simpler bodies, we see the effects of strife and discord in the elements. For example, if fire is mixed with water, thunder and lightning are caused; if fire is mixed with air in too great a proportion, flashes, comets, and other fiery <term>meteors</term> <note>In the 17th century, this term referred to any atmospheric phenomenon</note> arise. If the coldness of the earth is mixed with the air, snow and hail are driven and gathered in the sky; if water is mixed with air, mists and clouds appear. And so, a confusion of consonances and even of the musical tones themselves sometimes occurs; all of these accidents produce "diseases" in the lower region of the world within simple bodies or elements. 

Furthermore, even in composite bodies <note>Living organisms made of multiple elements</note>, a great strife sometimes arises that is fatal to the body. This happens either because of the violation of the "elemental ligaments" <note>The spiritual or physical bonds holding the elements together</note> contained within the composite, or because of an inequality in the proportion of one element to another. To discuss these matters more extensively here is not our purpose, for such a vast field of study would be more tedious than is appropriate for this musical discourse of ours. We therefore conclude this "World Music" of ours with this axiom: *The Sun thunders its Octave toward generation, and the earth thunders its own toward corruption.* <note>original: "Tonat Sol Diapaſon ſuum ad generationem, & tonat terra ſuum ad corruptionem."</note>

# CHAPTER I.

## On the difference between the spiritual creatures of the Empyrean heaven, according to both the ancient Philosophers and the Fathers of Theology.

<image-desc>A large historiated woodcut initial 'D' depicting two figures, a man and a woman, standing in a lush garden or forest setting with detailed foliage and trees.</image-desc>

The word "Demon" is used not for the Devil alone, but for a spirit so called by the proper meaning of the word <note>original: "ex vocabuli proprietate"</note>, as if meaning one who is "knowing," "intelligent," and "wise." For this reason, such a being is known by the name of "Intelligence," because it is an intelligent substance free from the mass of any gross body subject to decay, immortal and imperceptible, assisting all things and influencing all things. It is also sometimes called an *Angel*, that is, a "messenger," because it is commanded by **GOD** to reveal His mandates to humans. Thus, the name "Demon" seems to be a general term pertaining to all spiritual creatures, the soul alone excepted; although I am aware that some have existed who distinguished Demons from other creatures of the highest heaven. Iamblichus <note>A Neoplatonist philosopher (c. 245–325 AD) who wrote on theurgy and the divine orders.</note>, making mention of four orders of celestial spirits—namely, the first, of *Gods*; the second, of *Demons*; the third, of *Heroes*; and the last, of *souls*—called these three latter ones the "attendants" of the **GODS**. He demonstrates that one of these orders is more powerful than another, and that according to their own dignity, they each possess the more excellent places or regions of this heaven (which we named "elements" a little earlier), as he declared quite extensively in his descriptions and the differences of their properties.

The same author elsewhere distributed the choir of celestial spirits into eight species or differences: namely into **GODS**, *Archangels, Angels, Demons, Heroes, Principalities, Princes,* and *souls*. However, the tradition of the *Magi* <note>Refers to ancient practitioners of sacred wisdom or "natural magic," often associated with Zoroaster or Hermes Trismegistus.</note> seems to include all these species—except perhaps the very last—under the name of "Demon," taking the word "Demon" to mean a "knowing, intelligent, and wise spirit." Hence they called spirits—both the pre-celestial and celestial ones, as well as the lower ministers—"Demons."

The *Theologians* (among whom I name *Dionysius* <note>Pseudo-Dionysius the Areopagite, a highly influential 5th-century Christian mystic who established the nine-fold hierarchy of angels.</note>) wished to condense these species of Demons into three hierarchies. Each of these they distinguished into three orders differing in rank and property, which they called "choirs." *In the superior hierarchy, therefore, which Dionysius calls the <term>Epiphania</term> <note>Greek for "Manifestation" or "Appearance from above."</note>, the first order is the Seraphim, the second the Cherubim, and the third the Thrones; in the middle hierarchy, named by him the <term>Epiphononia</term> <note>Greek for "Resounding" or "Voice upon voice."</note>, are the Dominations, Virtues, and Powers; in the lowest (which he calls the <term>Ephionia</term> <note>Greek for "Sounding below."</note>) are the Principalities, Archangels, and Angels.*

Finally, all who have written on divine matters have been accustomed to divide Demons into good and evil. Those who are good are called "celestial" by Iamblichus; he asserts that such beings are beneficent and strive for and work at nothing but the good itself. *Proclus* <note>A 5th-century Neoplatonist philosopher who systematized Greek theology.</note> calls the evil Demons "material" and "earthly," and says that the good Demons are more inclined toward the mind <note>original: "animo magis expositos esse"</note>, while the evil and earthly ones must be driven away with the most diligent caution we can muster. So that we may agree with the ancient Philosophers and the modern <note>original: "neotericis"</note> Theologians of this age, the celestial or spiritual...

<meta>catchword: ...spiritual</meta>

<summary>This chapter defines "Demon" as a general term for spiritual intelligences and outlines their classifications. It compares the four-fold and eight-fold divisions of Neoplatonic philosophers like Iamblichus with the nine-fold hierarchy of angels established by Pseudo-Dionysius.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion from Chapter I regarding the classification of spiritual beings. It transitions from general definitions of "Demons" (as intelligences) to their specific categories and then begins Chapter II, which investigates their physical or quasi-physical substance.</meta>

[spiri]tual creatures, created on the first day in and with the spiritual heaven, are divided into two species: these are *Demons* and *souls*. The Demons, whom the Theologians call angels, we further subdivide into good and evil; others call these *beneficent* and *maleficent*, and John in the Apocalypse <note>The Book of Revelation 12:7–9.</note> names them favoring and harming angels. The good ones they have arranged into three hierarchies; but the evil ones—whom *Plato* calls *Cacodæmones* <note>original: "Cacodæmones," meaning "evil spirits."</note>, deserting angels, and cultivators of crimes and impiety—they have named Lucifer and his companions, whose legions are infinite. Again, the good are called *angels of light*, while the evil are called *angels of darkness*. The soul, however, is the lowest species of the intelligences; this is proven by the fact that it is naturally accustomed to mingle with lower bodies, so that it may adorn them as an essential form.

## CHAPTER II.

->*On the composition of Demons and Angels; How they are said to be incorporeal? Some arguments proving the composition of demonic substances.*<-

<image-desc>A large decorative drop cap 'A' featuring floral and vine-like woodcut patterns.</image-desc>

Augustine, in his work *On Genesis*, says that *Demons* are aerial and fiery animals: by "aerial" he denotes pure spiritual matter; by "fiery" he understands the form. *Damascene* <note>John of Damascus (c. 675–749 AD), a prominent Christian monk and scholar.</note> in Book 2, Chapter 3, calls the Angels intellectual lights, which *Pythagoras* believed were made from fire alone. They are said to be more lucid than the Sun, Moon, and other Stars for this reason: because they took their origin from the thinnest fire enclosed in a most subtle spirit, whereas the stars arose from a thick fire participating in the air or ethereal moisture. Therefore, it is manifest that the Angels do not participate in the matter of the lower heavens at all, as is held in the *third book On the Soul*, but are certainly a formal spirit, whose matter—with respect to lower substance—is entirely form, and as if fused from a single essence. Hence *Dionysius* <note>Pseudo-Dionysius the Areopagite, an influential 5th-century Christian Neoplatonist.</note> calls the Angels *Agalmata* <term>Agalmata</term> <note>original: "Agalmata," a Greek term for "divine mirrors" or "images" that reflect the light of God.</note>, that is, the brightest mirrors, receiving the light of GOD, representing His image, and revealing the hidden things of reality, as he testifies in his book *On the Divine Names*. However, that these spirits differ from one another in rank, dignity, and splendor, *Iamblichus* attempts to prove by their appearances to priests in this manner:

> *The spectacles of the GODS,* he says, *occur to us as exceedingly bright, shining to the highest degree, and wonderfully distinct in their splendor. Archangels have around them, both before and behind, a very great multitude of fire, and a light precedes them divided within itself; the light of Angels is bright, but less than that of Archangels, and it is divided; Demons appear as if they were a turbulent fire; Heroes have a light mixed from many sources; the light of a Principality* <note>One of the nine choirs of angels.</note> *is purer; Princes possess a light confused from dissimilar and contrary things; Souls render a fire full of many generations, admixtures, and divisions. They possess these differences according to their station: for the closer they are to GOD, the brighter they are; but the more remote, the darker they are.*

From these points, therefore, one may gather that Demons are composed of the matter of the most subtle elements of the spiritual heaven. This matter, with respect to lower things, is most simple and aerial, and is derived from a divine form proceeding from the most splendid beatitude of GOD. Their composition, if compared with the creatures of the lower heavens, is called incorporeal; but with respect to the simplicity of the lucid substance of the Empyrean heaven—in which, with which, and from whose elements they were created on the first day—they must be considered as a body fused from spiritual elements, no differently than plants, herbs, and their seeds were made on the third day with the earth, which stands opposite the Empyrean heaven.

<meta>catchword: must be considered [habenda]</meta>

Using authorities like Augustine and Iamblichus, the author argues that while angels are "incorporeal" compared to us, they possess a subtle, fiery "spiritual body" derived from the Empyrean heaven.</summary>

--- Page 112 ---
<meta>This page continues from page 109, which concluded by discussing the radiant nature of angels and the differing views of philosophers like Pythagoras and the Church Father John of Damascus on their fiery or intellectual substance.</meta>

must be considered. And this is why Augustine asserts that they have had **aerial bodies** from the beginning of their creation; that is, they were formed from the higher part of the air to be suitable for acting, and not for being acted upon <note>The Latin "patiendum" (suffering or being acted upon) refers to the Aristotelian concept of "passion," where a substance is changed by an external force.</note>; and Basil also affirms this same point. Indeed, what could more clearly denote the composition of Demons than their actions and passions? As for the fact that they act and are acted upon (*Theological Resolutions, treatise 2, chapter 2*), since they both receive and give forth illuminations, and produce the act of understanding and also receive it, I certainly think no one is so grossly blind or deluded as to deny it. It is certain, therefore, that within them there is one thing that acts, and another that is acted upon; and truly, that by which they act can be nothing other than **act**, which is called **form**; nor is that by which they are acted upon anything other than **potency**; and this is called **matter**. <note>In medieval philosophy, "form" is the active principle that gives a thing its identity, while "matter" is the potentiality to receive that form.</note>

Indeed, it must be especially observed here that the composition of matter and form has a three-fold aspect, according to the diversity of the three heavens. For in **lower bodies**, matter is subject to the form, to quantity, and to contrariety <note>Contrariety refers to the conflict between opposing qualities, like hot and cold, which causes physical change and decay.</note>; in **celestial bodies**, however, matter is subject to form and quantity, but not to contrariety; but in **spiritual creatures**, it is subject to form, but in no way to corporeal quantity or contrariety. We conclude, therefore, that Demons are of the most subtle composition among all creatures, and are more glowing and clear than all others, because they are more filled with formal essence, and consequently more spiritual, more noble, and as it were, incorporeal, always mobile, free, and—as Damascene says—participants in **immortality**. For their essence, from which they exist, is most simple, and almost immaterial, lucid, pure, distinct, and discrete; their power, from which they hold authority, is free, intelligent, lovable, and eternal; finally, their operation, through which they are engaged, is voluntary, sudden, useful, and honorable; for they work without delay or hindrance, and serve us entirely without ceasing, as is found in *Theological Resolutions, treatise 2, part 2*.

## CHAPTER III.

->*How are angels said to be in a place?*<-

<image-desc>Ornamental woodcut initial 'A' featuring floral and foliate scrollwork.</image-desc>
IN THE SECOND chapter of the first book, we declared that God exists in a place differently than Angels, and Angels differently than corporeal creatures; since **GOD** is said to be here and everywhere only according to His presence—namely, in the **Empyrean heaven** <note>The highest part of heaven, believed by medieval scholars to be occupied by the element of fire or pure light, and the dwelling place of God.</note> as His blessed tabernacle, and in every part of the world as the realm of His empire. **Angels**, however, without the assumption of bodies, are judged to be in some part of the world not only by presence, but also **definitively**; and when they have assumed corporeal substances, they are judged to be there both by presence and definitively, as well as **circumscriptively** <note>original: "circumscriptivè." In scholastic theology, a body is "circumscriptively" in a place because its boundaries are limited by the space around it; a spirit is "definitively" in a place because it acts there and nowhere else at that moment.</note> within some concave surface.

Wherefore, according to the resolution of the Theologians, **Angels** are said to be **formally** in a place—that is, through their presence; yet they cannot be everywhere at the same moment of time, because they are said to be in a place *definitively*. Nor, truly, are they necessarily in a place, because they can be carried along even if no containing body exists: for they can exist outside of all corporeal nature. And this is why they are held to be in a place **potentially**; for they have the power of existing within some containing body, but not in *act*, because they do not cause the sides of the container to move apart, nor do they produce any containing surface in *act*. From these things, therefore, it is clear that Angels, on account of their composition, are in some manner...

<summary>This page explains that demons and angels are composed of a very subtle "spiritual matter" and "form," making them immortal and swift. It also introduces the complex theological rules for how spiritual beings occupy space compared to God and physical objects.</summary>
<keywords>Aristotelian matter and form, Augustine, Basil the Great, John of Damascus, angelic location, Empyrean heaven, definitive presence, spiritual substance</keywords>

The catchword "opta-" at the bottom suggests the next page will likely begin with a word like "optata" (desired) or "optamus" (we wish).</meta>

merely being in a place. But here it must be observed that Angels, insofar as they have taken on thicker bodies, are in a place **circumscriptively** <note>original: "circumscriptivè." In scholastic philosophy, this means being contained within a specific physical boundary or location, unlike God, who is everywhere.</note>. For it is certain that Angels, both good and bad, have frequently donned "accidental" bodies of every kind; which they soon after cast off, as has been found in various places in the Holy Scripture, and proven by the apparitions of witches and illusionists.

# CHAPTER IV.

->*Concerning the differences of Demons, and their offices, according to both the*<-
->*tradition of the ancient Magi and the moderns.*<-

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut drop cap 'M' featuring stylized foliage and scrollwork within a rectangular border.</image-desc>
THE MAGI <note>The "Magi" here refers to ancient sages and practitioners of "natural magic" or high philosophy, rather than simple sorcerers.</note>, establishing three kinds of **Demons** <note>In this context, "Demons" (from the Greek *daimones*) refers to intermediary spirits or "intelligences," which can be good, neutral, or evil, not solely "demons" in the modern sense of devils.</note> and intelligences (for some, according to them, are **pre-celestial**, others **celestial**, and the rest are **ministers of lower things**), called the pre-celestial ones "minds." These are entirely separate from any body and are like intellectual spheres, worshipping the one GOD as their most stable point and center. Indeed, they claim that these beings are full of GOD and, as it were, intoxicated with divine nectar; they believe they are occupied with GOD alone, and they think they take no care whatsoever for mundane bodies, nor do they believe they serve in any way the administration of lower things. They conclude, however, that their office is to pour the light received from GOD into the lower orders soon after, and to distribute individual duties to each. And this species of Demon is, without doubt, that which **Iamblichus** <note>original: "Jamblicus"; a Neoplatonist philosopher who wrote on the mysteries of the Egyptians and the nature of gods.</note> calls "Gods following the first GOD, who gives all things to all," who bestow some things upon everyone. This is also the first and higher Hierarchy of Angels, comprising the orders of **Cherubim, Seraphim,** and **Thrones**, whose office it is to contemplate the order of divine providence. For the Angels of the first order look upon the goodness of GOD, those of the second upon the essence of GOD as form, and those of the third upon His wisdom. Finally, these are the Angels closest to GOD, whom they magnify with continuous hymns and everlasting praise, and they pray to GOD for our salvation, as **Athanasius** <note>An influential 4th-century Church Father known for his defense of the Trinity.</note> says.

The Wise Men call the **celestial intelligences** "worldly Demons" because, beyond divine worship, they are suited to the spheres of the world and preside over each of the heavens and stars. From this it follows that they are distributed into as many orders as there are orbs in the world and stars counted within those orbs. And it is for this reason that some are called **Jovial** (of Jupiter), others **Saturnine** (of Saturn), and so forth. **Hermes Trismegistus** <note>original: "Mercurius Trismegistus"; the legendary Hellenistic figure to whom the *Hermetica* are attributed.</note>, in his *Sacred Sermon*, calls these kinds of Demons "Gods." He says that after the heaven had shone forth in seven circles, they were seen in the "ideas" of the stars along with their signs, and he says that the stars were numbered according to those Gods who inhabit them. These, without doubt, are those Demons of Iamblichus whom he calls the "ministers of the Gods," acting as architects in the mundane workshop, who express those things that are inexpressible and hidden within the Gods. These are also those Angels who are placed in the middle hierarchy, as if having dominion in the middle region of the world. They are called by **Abbot Trithemius** <note>Johannes Trithemius (1462–1516), a Benedictine abbot and famous scholar of cryptography and angel magic.</note> "second intelligences," who, by the ordination of the first intellect (that is, GOD), are said to govern the ether and the orbs of the Planets.

The Magi call the Demons of the lower order **ministers**, who are assigned to those things that must be arranged in the lower realms. **Origen** <note>An early Christian scholar and theologian (c. 184–253 AD).</note> calls these certain "invisible powers" through which any earthly things are arranged; he asserts that they, visible to no one, complete our business, direct our journeys, avert the dangers of battles while being present, and prevent the outcomes of secret traps...

<vocab>Demons, intelligence, pre-celestial, Hierarchy of Angels, Cherubim, Seraphim, Thrones, Iamblichus, Hermes Trismegistus, Trithemius, Origen</vocab>

<summary>This page discusses the classification of angels and demons into three hierarchies (pre-celestial, celestial, and lower ministers) according to both Neoplatonic philosophy and Christian theology. It describes their various roles, from the eternal contemplation of God by the highest orders to the governance of stars and the management of earthly human affairs by the lower spirits.</summary>

# TREATISE I. BOOK IV.

...and to provide the desired success to friends, namely by procuring prosperity and breaking down adversity. In this species of **Demons** <note>original: "Dæmonum." In the Neoplatonic tradition used here, "demons" are not necessarily evil; they are intermediary spirits or "intelligences" that bridge the gap between the divine and the physical world.</note>, we judge that the **Heroes of Iamblichus** <note>Iamblichus (c. 245–325 AD) was a Syrian Neoplatonist philosopher who organized spirits into a complex hierarchy of gods, angels, demons, and heroes.</note> should be numbered, whose duties—as he himself believes—are exercised concerning earthly matters. For they give life to these things, grant them reason, and guard their souls, which they also loosen and set free from them. From these things, he seems to intimate especially that the Heroes are mostly concerned with souls. Theologians have placed this lowest kind of intelligences entirely in the final Hierarchy, called **Ephionia** <note>A term derived from Greek used in Renaissance Christian Kabbalah and Hermeticism to describe the third and lowest triad of the angelic hierarchy (typically consisting of Principalities, Archangels, and Angels).</note>; its Demons or Angels descend as ministers to care for lower things. They asserted that the office of the first order of this hierarchy is public—namely, to care for princes, magistrates, provinces, and kingdoms. The duty of the second order is to be present at sacred rites and to report the prayers and sacrifices of men to **GOD**. Finally, the third order of Angels is mandated with the province of appropriately arranging each minor thing; for individuals are present as guardians to individuals, and they supply proper virtues to herbs, minerals, and all lower things. From the aforementioned, we therefore conclude that just as the elements of the lower heavens received their forms and essences from the luminous source of the higher heaven, so also their creatures, molded and composed from those elements, are ordered, directed, and preserved by the creatures of that spiritual heaven. And *Augustine* confirms this, who proclaims that no visible thing exists among us which an Angelic power does not preside over, and he constantly decrees that all bodies are ruled through a rational spirit of life; *Origen* <note>An influential early Christian theologian (c. 184–253 AD) who speculated on the pre-existence of souls and the spiritual nature of all created things.</note> seems to agree with this, who in his commentaries assigned a proper spirit not only to humans but also to brute animals, plants, minerals, and the growth of all other things.

### CHAPTER V.

->Concerning the evil Angel Lucifer, concerning his rebellion and pride against<-
->**GOD**, and concerning his expulsion with his companions from the<-
->**Empyrean Heaven.**<-

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut initial letter P set within a square frame decorated with floral and vine scrollwork.</image-desc>

**On the first** day of creation, Lucifer was created, so called because he shone before all Angels in clarity and splendor, as *Gregory* says: For just as he was the first Angel, preceding all others in creation, so he stood out far among all by the excellent quality of his light. And from this it happened—as *Augustine* supports in his *Book on Questions of the Old Testament*—that he, blinded by his own elevation, longed to become like **GOD**, that is, so that he might preside and rule over all. Hence *Isaiah 14* <note>Isaiah 14:14.</note> says in the persona of Lucifer: *I will ascend into heaven and I will be like the Most High.* This malice of Lucifer, which according to *Scotus* <note>John Duns Scotus (c. 1266–1308), a highly influential medieval Franciscan priest and philosopher.</note> began from love of self and was consummated in hatred of **GOD** (which was the greatest possible sin), is determined by theologians to have a three-fold state: *Beginning, Increase, and Completion*. The *Beginning* came from presumption; for as soon as he contemplated his own beauty, he immediately became presumptuous. The *Increase* began in ambition; for when he had first presumed, he furthermore desired that which was entirely above him, and which he could not possibly obtain, carried away by a certain ambitious air. Its *Completion* was terminated in the most ruinous affections of hatred and envy; for when a most foul ambition had tickled him with a burning desire for divine command, and yet he perceived it to be impossible that he could achieve it by any efforts—

<meta>catchword: queretur (completing the word "assequeretur," meaning "he might achieve" or "he might attain")</meta>

# CONCERNING THE CREATURES OF THE EMPYREAN HEAVEN.

...he sought, and was soon set ablaze with abominable envy against the most high GOD: Wherefore that Angel, on account of that horrendous pride, along with the others of the lower order who showed themselves as impious fellow-soldiers in that same **Theomachia** <term>Theomachia: From the Greek, meaning a "war against God" or "battle of the gods."</term>, was most deservedly precipitated out of the Empyrean heaven <note>The Empyrean was considered the highest, most spiritual part of heaven, composed of pure light and fire.</note> and its most blessed seat of light by the good Angels. Downwards indeed both he and his consorts fell headlong, and were enclosed and imprisoned in the dark air or a horrendous and opaque shadow, which glided beneath them in an oblique motion. Not even the smallest spark of hope remained for recovering their former state, so that **Lucifer**—so named for his extraordinary light and splendor <note>The name Lucifer literally means "Light-Bringer" in Latin.</note>—fell through his excessive pride from angelic radiance into **Diabolical** obscurity. As **Gregory** <note>Saint Gregory the Great (c. 540–604), a Pope and theologian whose writings on the hierarchies of angels were foundational for medieval demonology.</note> says, he migrated from the seat of love into the hatred and envy of all, and tumbled from the highest peak of prosperity into the lowest abyss of unhappiness and misery. Therefore, after he had fallen in this manner, he was no longer called Lucifer, but <gloss>from "to cast down" <note>original: "ἀπὸ τοῦ βάλλειν" (apo tou ballein)</note></gloss> **Diabolus** [the Devil], as if cast down because of the heavy weight of his pride. And by this name also the rest of those once-renowned commanders and soldiers, stained by the same mark of betrayal, are distinguished.

But it will not be out of place if we observe with the Theologians that there is a triple order between the sin of Lucifer and that of the rest of the Angels who fell with him. The first is in the *degree of weight*; for the sin in him was heavier; whence it happened that he fell to the very center <note>In the geocentric model, the "center" of the universe was the center of the Earth, the location of Hell.</note>, surely much deeper than the rest, and from **Lucifer** [the Light-bringer] he became **Lucifugus** [the Light-fleer]. The second is the *order of occasion*, because he provided the way and the occasion for sinning to the others. The third is the *order of duration*; for Lucifer first presumed and opened the sentiment of his mind to certain other Angels: some of whom indeed supplied aid to this horrendous wickedness, while others, shrinking from such a great crime, adhered to **GOD**, as was fitting. 

Hence the most powerful creator of all retained the innocent Angels in heaven, shining with most bright glory, but drove the traitors out of his kingdom, to be walled in by perpetual darkness. Furthermore, for these Angels, according to the nature of their sin, a seat of exile—either lighter or heavier—is established. For some are nearer to the Empyrean heaven, namely the *Fiery* and *Aerial* ones; but others are more remote, namely the *Watery* and *Terrestrial* ones, who truly flee the light of heaven as much as possible, because they are weakened by its presence. Whence they are called *Light-fleers* <note>original: "Lucifugi"</note> by Mark <note>Possibly a reference to Marcus Eremita or a specific demonological commentator rather than the Evangelist.</note>, as we shall demonstrate below. 

After Lucifer was cast down with his companions from the face of the Empyrean heaven into the lowest heaven, from that time he is certainly called at times the **calumniator** or **accuser**, because he tormented the good with false slanders: Whence <margin>Revelation 12.</margin> *The accuser has been thrown down*; at other times he is called by the name of **Dragon** and **Serpent** on account of his venomous cunning. He is also compared to a **Lion** on account of his bold violence: Whence Peter [says in his] first and last [letters], *Like a roaring lion*, etc. And, because he is persistent and rooted in his wickedness, he is also sometimes named the **Boar**, as in <margin>Isaiah 25.</margin> <note>While the Boar is usually associated with Psalm 80, commentators often linked the "wild beasts" of Isaiah's prophecies to specific demonic archetypes.</note> He is also called the **Snake**, as one who hides under the darkness. **Augustine** in his *Literal Commentary on Genesis, book 8*, following **Plato**, marks him with the name of **Cacodæmon** <term>Cacodæmon: From the Greek, meaning "evil spirit." In Neoplatonic thought, "demons" could be neutral, but a "cacodæmon" was explicitly malevolent.</term> as if with a brand. Yet Plato calls all the subterranean and dark ones "deserting angels," "evil demons," and finally "wicked spirits"; because they are led spontaneously by a certain wonderful zeal for doing harm, as we shall 
->declare in the following chapter.<-

### CHAPTER VI.
->On the bodies, sufferings, characteristics,<-
->& types of evil Demons.<-

<margin>Augustine's *On the Literal Meaning of Genesis*</margin>
It is necessary that these Demons, according to the nature of the place to which they are banished, should accidentally take on bodies that are either thicker or thinner; for those of Fire are more subtle than those of Air, and those of Air more so than those of Water, and those again more so than the Earthly ones. I am of the same opinion as *Augustine* <note>Saint Augustine of Hippo (354–430 AD), whose views on the "ethereal" or "airy" bodies of demons influenced centuries of theology.</note>, that all Angels at the beginning of their creation possessed airy bodies. This same point also seems to be confirmed by *Psellus* <note>Michael Psellos (c. 1017–1078), a Byzantine philosopher and politician famous for his work *On the Operation of Demons*.</note>, who says that the bodies of good Angels are as if immaterial, while he asserts that those of evil Demons are in some way compacted of matter, like the bodies of shadows. He proved through various examples in his booklet that these are even subject to passions <term>passions: in this context, physical sensations or sufferings inflicted from the outside</term>, such as being sliced and divided by the stroke of a sword, feeling pain in such a division, and finally being scorched by fire and turned into visible ashes. For without a doubt, if they had possessed only airy bodies, it would be necessary for them to be entirely subject to physical impacts—no differently than the air itself, which constant experience teaches us retreats suddenly, makes a noise, and is divided by a blow if it is struck anywhere. Indeed, they could by no means yield to or give place to such sufferings in respect to their first composition, which was the same as the substance of the Angels (which we said a little earlier was fashioned from the spiritual elements of the Empyrean heaven); but they do so because of their participation in corruptible and material elements, with which they have been mixed and more or less burdened according to the gravity of their sin. This is also quite clearly proven by *Orpheus* <note>The legendary Greek poet and prophet; the author is likely referring to Orphic hymns or Neoplatonic interpretations of them.</note> from various descriptions of demonic bodies; he affirms that those of the Earthly Demons are certainly more visible to us, and more frequently meet and occur to our senses. *Agricola* <note>Georgius Agricola (1494–1555), a German scholar and "father of mineralogy," who wrote *De animantibus subterraneis* (On Subterranean Creatures) about mine spirits.</note> also made mention of these in his book *On Subterranean Animals*. The natures and properties of these Demons exist as being as utterly opposed as possible to the best GOD and His creatures. For when the Devils had unsuccessfully attempted to attack the Divine Majesty, they immediately directed their malicious thoughts here: since they themselves are unable to harm God, they would at least lay wicked snares for His most excellent creatures, who are endowed with the divine image. For they harass quiet and innocent men, bringing them loss, misery, and harm; and, unless such audacity and malice were restrained by the good Angels, every single person would daily feel their implacable tyranny. For, as *Porphyry* <note>Porphyry of Tyre (c. 234–305 AD), a Neoplatonist philosopher who wrote extensively on the nature of spirits.</note> says, there is no evil that they do not dare to perpetrate; for they have unhealthy and entirely violent habits. Furthermore, it happens not infrequently that their malice is exercised, by divine permission, in punishing impious men. For this reason, the *Platonists* called the dark Demons "avengers of crimes and impiety" (looking toward the ordaining of divine justice). Here, however, the merciful and most divine goodness of GOD offers itself for our consideration: For what is more cruel than this troop of Demons? What is sharper or more ingenious toward any kind of evil? For according to *Isidore* <note>Isidore of Seville (c. 560–636 AD), an archbishop and encyclopedist.</note>, they know some things by the subtlety of their nature, others they understand through the duration of time and experience, and others they perceive by revelation from the good Angels; and they most greedily desire the harm and unhappiness of men. But behold, nevertheless, they conquer no one by their own strength and overthrow no one, but only seduce the minds of men with certain deceitful and crafty persuasions, as *Gregory* <note>Pope Gregory the Great, whose *Moralia in Job* is cited here.</note> testifies in book 32. Sometimes, therefore, they lull human minds to sleep with sluggishness; at other times they vehemently disturb their peace, either by violently agitating the elements, or by storms...

### ON THE CREATURES OF THE EMPYREAN HEAVEN.

P 2

<meta>Catchword: hiatus</meta>

...states, stirring up thunders, rains, winds, lightnings, and other things of that kind, or driving the swelling waves of the sea, or destroying the fruits necessary for human use, and casting infinite harms of this type against the honest desires of men. Many legions of these Demons are numbered, who are categorized according to the names of the stars and elements, and the regions of the world. Some among them are more powerful, while others are subject to their authority. For according to the Magicians, some are governed by Kings and Princes; but according to the school of Theologians, the evil Demons are distributed into nine ranks or classes, just as the good angels above are divided into three hierarchies containing nine orders.

In the **first rank** are those who, usurping the name of God, wish to be adored with divine worship, demanding sacrifices and similar ceremonies. They are called *False Gods* <note>original: *Pseudothei*</note>. From their number was that Demon who quite persistently tempted Christ at the highest peak of the temple’s pinnacle to adore him; and indeed, even today such Demons are held as Gods by the *Indians*, who worship them with various fumigations and innumerable sacrifices. The Prince of these is named **Beelzebub**.

The **second rank** is made up of the Spirits of Lies, who suggest false prophecies to seers. The Prince of these is **Python**; hence the *Pythian Apollo*, and the *Pythian* woman <note>Referring to the Witch of Endor from the First Book of Samuel.</note> mentioned in the book of *Samuel*, who had a spirit of divination <note>original: *Pythonem*</note> in her belly. This kind of Demon meddles in oracles and deceives the human race with uncertain and doubtful divinations or predictions.

Under the **third rank** are contained those Demons who, because of their impiety, are called vessels of iniquity, vessels of wrath, death, fury, and destruction. These are the inventors of evil things, such as that Demon **Theutus** <note>The Egyptian god Thoth, whom Plato discusses in the *Phaedrus* as the inventor of writing and games of chance.</note> in *Plato*, who taught games and dice. The Prince of these is **Belial**, which means "the disobedient one" and "Apostate," of whom it is written in *2 Corinthians 6*.

In the **fourth class** are the avengers of crimes, whose Prince is **Asmodeus**, which signifies "one who brings judgment."

Under the **fifth series** are placed the **Deceivers** <note>original: *Praestigiatores*, literally "jugglers" or "illusionists"</note>, who seem to work miracles. These, for the most part, serve sorcerers and *Evil-Magicians* so that they may deceive the people with imaginary apparitions or illusions. Their Prince is **Satan**, who, as it is written in the *Apocalypse*, seduces the whole world by performing great signs.

The Demons of the **sixth place** are those who involve themselves in thunders, lightning bolts, and flashes, infecting the air with pestilence and bringing other atmospheric evils. Among these are also counted those four Angels who rule the winds blowing from the four corners of the world. Their prince is **Meririm**, that is, the "Scorching Spirit," the raging Demon of the Midday <note>A reference to Psalm 91:6, often interpreted as a specific demon of heat and sickness.</note>, whom *Paul in his letter to the Ephesians* calls the prince of the power of this air, and the Spirit who works in the children of distrust.

In the **seventh**, the **Furies** are counted, the sowers of evils, the mothers of discord, war, and ruin; and their prince is **Abaddon**, that is, the "Exterminator" and "Devastator."

The **eighth dwelling** is possessed by the accusers and calumniators, whose prince is called **Astaroth**, who joyfully and swiftly exaggerates and presses our sins before God.

The **last place** is filled by the **Tempters** or those who lie in wait, who are present individually to every person, and have received the name of "Genii." Among these was that *Genius of Brutus* <note>The "evil spirit" that allegedly appeared to Marcus Brutus before the Battle of Philippi, telling him "I shall see thee again at Philippi."</note> who urged him toward that battle in which he breathed his last breath. The Prince of these is called **Mammon**.

</margin>

But *Marcus*, addressing *Psellus* <note>Michael Psellos, an 11th-century Byzantine scholar whose work on demonology was highly influential in the Renaissance.</note>, established only six kinds of Demons. The **first** he calls *Lehireon* <note>likely a Latinization of a Greek term for fiery spirits</note>, which is fiery, because it wanders around the higher part of the air. The **second** wanders in the air close to us, which he therefore specifically called the "Airy" kind. The **third** is the "Earthly" kind, which mostly dwells around the earth and opposes all human reasonings and machines. The **fourth** is "Watery" and "Marine," which freely frequents lakes, rivers, and marshes, and overwhelms and destroys many in the waters; and, stirring up terrible waves and storms at sea, it utterly sinks ships laden with men. The **fifth** kind, since it hides under the earth, is called "Subterranean." This kind lethally attacks the diggers of minerals in deep wells and caverns, [causing] the earth...

...creates a gap, shakes its foundations, and stirs up flame-vomiting winds. The sixth and final kind is the **Light-shunning** <note>original: *Lucifugum*</note>, inscrutable and entirely dark, violently destroying all things with cold passions. Indeed, all these kinds of Demons were said to be so evilly disposed that they pursue GOD and the Angels with a completely fatal hatred, and oppose the human race with perpetual hostility, although the madness of their hatred does not agitate them all equally: for the **Water-dwelling** <note>original: *Aquatile*</note>, the **Light-shunning**, and even the **Subterranean** ones seem far more malicious and destructive than the others. For these spirits wander here and there like wild forest beasts, greedily searching for whom they may devour, rushing most swiftly toward the destruction of men. The Aerial ones deceive human minds and distract them with the most foul and ruinous disturbances. This opinion of Marcus <note>Likely the character Marcus from Michael Psellus’s 11th-century dialogue *On the Operation of Demons*.</note> seems to be strengthened and established partly by several passages of Holy Scripture, and partly by the authority and examples of both ancient and modern writers.

# CHAPTER VII.

->*Which region of the lower sky does Lucifer and his Angels frequent the most?*<-

<image-desc>A large decorative woodcut initial 'D' containing intricate floral patterns and scrolling vines within a square border.</image-desc>
The DEVIL, the Prince of rebellion—formerly called **Lucifer** <note>Light-bringer</note> but now indeed called **Light-shunning** <note>original: *Lucifugus*</note>—dwells more frequently in the Northern <note>original: *Septentrionalibus*</note> parts of the earth with his legions, those closest to him in the gravity of their sin. This aligns with Holy Scripture, which teaches us that Satan holds his seat in that part of the world; it would be hardly difficult to confirm this through both clear reasoning and living examples. For if we exactly consider the nature of that extreme part of the world, we will see its location agrees perfectly with the disposition of the Light-shunning Demons. For *Olaus Magnus* <note>A 16th-century Swedish archbishop and cartographer.</note> says that the region of **Biarmia** <note>A historical region on the northern shores of the White Sea.</note> has the Arctic Pole as its zenith and the Equinoctial as its horizon. He says further that in its outer parts (namely those facing the Tropic of Cancer), only a single natural day shines in a solar year. For there, the year is divided into two equal parts: namely, into one single artificial day, and then into one single night. The day emerges when the Sun enters Aries; the night, however, begins when it leaves Libra. But in the parts of this region further from the Tropic, and placed more directly under or beyond the Pole, an immense part of the earth is found where the Sun never shoots its rays, but which is obscured by a perpetual gloom of darkness. *Freigius* <note>Johann Thomas Freigius (1543–1583), a German polymath.</note> also maintains this in his *Geography*, asserting that the part of the Frigid Zone closer to the pole is neither ever illuminated nor warmed, but shudders and stiffens with cold and gloom. Hence, it is demonstrated by clear reason that these Light-shunning Demons delight in this region of the earth with greater pleasure, since they naturally hate the presence of light as the image of GOD; to avoid it, they withdraw into such dark places. And the ceremonies of the ancients seem to confirm this,

<margin>Apuleius, book 2 of the *Metamorphoses* <note>Also known as *The Golden Ass*.</note>.</margin>
among whom it was the custom that very large lamps with enough oil for light were placed next to the corpses of the deceased, so that such Demons serving evil sorcerers <note>original: *Cacomagis*</note> might be deterred from their usual slaughter by the great brightness of the light. But truly, since in this place the absence of light is always found, it is a wonder how they gather there in crowds; because this has been so ordained by GOD Most High, it can in no way be avoided. Furthermore, here the misery of these Demons,

# ON THE CREATURES OF THE EMPYREAN HEAVEN.

<margin>P 3</margin>

...and the greatest unhappiness can be observed; who, although in their creation they were composed of an aerial and fiery substance, later, after they had committed horrific crimes, their fiery soul was suffocated by a vapor that was, as it were, cloudy and extremely cold; their aerial bodies were frozen by earthly dryness, and they were deprived of the freedom of their spiritual moisture: wherefore they are afflicted by contrary actions in every way, punished, and tortured in the near-perpetual abyss of Gehenna <note>A term often used for Hell or a place of fiery torment.</note>. 

Truly, authors provide us with many examples that prove the frequency of these Demons in the aforementioned places: among whom Olaus Magnus <note>A 16th-century Swedish archbishop and cartographer, famous for his descriptions of Northern folklore and geography.</note> proclaimed that in the outer parts of Biarmia <note>A historical territory in the northern part of modern-day Russia/Scandinavia.</note>, especially in Sweden; and likewise also, though not so frequently, in Denmark, Finland, and Iceland, the household spirits or masks <term>larvae</term> dance at night; that ghosts <term>lemures</term> appear in the form of beasts, and the spirits <term>manes</term> of the nations terrify the inhabitants. Fauns also, and Satyrs, and Pans, and Screech-owls <term>Striges</term>, and infinite species of Demons of this kind, visibly walking about in the storm of night, strike the gravest terror into the natives. 

Furthermore, almost every part of these nations abounds in sorceries that transform into miraculous forms, and is everywhere filled with evil-magicians <term>Cacomagi</term> <note>From the Greek 'kakos' (evil) and 'magos' (magician).</note>, who have the most intimate familiarity with these Demons, as writers on the Northern regions have recorded. Nor indeed does the region of the opposite pole <note>The South Pole</note> teem with any less destructive specters, since it is also mostly surrounded by darkness. But nevertheless, this genus of Demons differs by one degree from that which inhabits the northern pole. For these expose themselves somewhat more familiarly to light and heat than those Northern ones; indeed, they sometimes willingly look upon the solar rays, not because they love them (for they hate light most destructively), but so that their chilly limbs may be refreshed for a little while by the pleasant heat; which freedom was permitted to them over the **Light-shunners** <note>original: *Lucifugos*</note>, because they did not sin as gravely as those others. 

Moreover, this species secretly contains within itself a certain fiery glow, as if suffocated by darkness; nor indeed are the parts of their composition so contracted and constricted by earthly cold and dryness as happens to those Light-shunners; because they are clearly hot on account of an internal fire somewhat blunting the operations of cold. And this hidden sulfurous glow of theirs prevents the dark and liquid vapors of their substance from being frozen. 

Therefore the Divine will, wishing to inflict a lighter punishment on them according to the degree of their sin, thrust this second order of the worst Demons into this part of the world as exiles, where the sky neither rages so persistently with cold, nor are heterogeneous things so easily gathered by earthly dryness, on account of the proximity of the Sun existing in its perigee <note>The point in an orbit where a body is closest to the Earth.</note>, which there approaches closer to the center of the world, just as in its opposite, namely in its apogee <note>The point furthest from Earth; original: *Auge*</note>, it is elevated and, according to Astronomers, stands further from the earth. Hence in the Northern region there are indissoluble snows and perpetual ice, giving its name to the sea of that part, and violent earthly blasts inducing hail, frost, snow, and ice by their freezing spirit; all of which frequently happen to be stirred up by the breath of the **Light-shunning Spirits** to the great detriment of men and beasts. 

And these indeed are the most true and marvelous operations of Satan, the Prince of this region. Other things, which seem miraculous to his evil-magicians, are to be considered as feigned and fantastic imaginations, and to be strongly exploded as Diabolical illusions. But the other part [of the world], not so abundant in snow nor so hardened by ice (whence its seas are plowed at any time of the year), although it is covered by a veil of darkness equal to the opposite seat, nevertheless vomits flames and fiery meteors everywhere; whence it is called **Terra del Fuego** <note>Land of Fire</note> by Cosmographers. Therefore the Demons of this region are accustomed to stir up thunders more frequently than those Northern ones: Whence their Prince is called **Meririm**, that is, the **Meridian Demon** <note>Also known as the "Noonday Demon," historically associated with sloth or sudden destruction.</note>, a burning Spirit, a Demon raging at midday: Paul calls this one the **Prince of the power of the air** <note>A reference to Ephesians 2:2.</note> in the Epistle to the Ephesians; he also rules the Southern wind; whose legions, as the...

### TREATISE I. BOOK IV.

As *Cornelius Agrippa* <note>Heinrich Cornelius Agrippa (1486–1535) was a famous German polymath, physician, and legal scholar known for his influential works on the occult and "natural magic."</note> asserts, they involve themselves in thunders, lightning bolts, and flashes, as was said above; from this, it happens that storms of this kind rage more frequently in those parts. Furthermore, they also infect the air with foul vapors, seizing the people with pestilence and other epidemic illnesses. But we shall discuss these things more copiously elsewhere. There are also intermediate legions of Demons, who participate more or less in the nature of the two extremes. For Demons are found in almost infinite places between the Tropic of Cancer and the polar circle, hiding in colder places and mountain caverns. Among these places, the *Alpine, Apennine, and Pyrenean* mountains are judged to be much more crowded with them; this shines forth more clearly from the fact that such a great multitude of enchanters and sorcerers is found there. For I remember hearing from a person of good faith in upper *Switzerland* that an entire crowd of a whole parish, both men and women—with only a single minister excepted—were found guilty of <term>Cacomagia</term> <note>From the Greek 'kakos' (evil) and 'mageia' (magic); refers to malevolent or "black" magic.</note> and were punished with death by Christian law. These Demons, however, are as if from the race of the <term>Lucifuges</term> <note>Literally "light-shunners"; spirits that avoid the day and dwell in darkness.</note> and are of an extremely cold nature, as the judges of that place remember from the confession of a certain woman from the aforementioned village; she indeed confessed that she had coupled with a Demon of that place, and in the act she felt a thoroughly intolerable coldness. 

So also the intermediate Demons living beyond the equator toward the Antarctic pole participate more or less in the nature of the southern spirits, except that the closer they are to the Tropic of Capricorn and the Equinoctial line, the hotter and more burning they are to be esteemed. Hence, flashes, thunders, and lightnings are perceived almost daily by those who are accustomed to pass through those parts in summer. Among these also are some <term>Pseudothei</term> <note>False gods or spirits that demand divine worship.</note>; from whose number is that Demon whom those in *Madagascar* honor with divine worship out of fear. To this kind of Devil, the *Chinese* also offer sacrifices and prayers, as reported by travelers who have had commerce with those nations.
