# TREATISE I. BOOK V.
<page-num>156</page-num>
<header>TREATISE I. BOOK V.</header>

That the earth is indeed the center of the heavens is clear; for the Sun and the rest of the stars are known to maintain the same distance from the earth in their natural motion as we observe here in our hemisphere. This is something that surely could not happen if, as some falsely claim, the center of the whole were in the Sun. And we further confirm this by the following reasoning: that which is more dense and heavy is the center of the world. Now, the earth is more dense; this is proven by the fact that the higher one ascends from the earth, the thinner and more subtle the mediums are always found to be. Thus, water is thinner than earth, air than water, fire than air, and the <term>Fifth Essence</term> <note>Also known as the Aether or Quinte-essence; the pure, incorruptible material that makes up the celestial bodies, distinct from the four earthly elements.</note> or ethereal matter is thinner than fire, and so on. Therefore, [the earth is the center]. We say, then, that the preceding experiments clearly demonstrate that the exterior parts of the globe—that is, those tending more toward the circumference—are more fit by nature for movement than the more central parts; for they are led around by the soul more easily than those at the center. Consequently, it has been demonstrated that the earth itself moves more slowly by nature, since it is the physical center of the <term>macrocosm</term> <note>The "Great World" or the universe as a whole, viewed as a large-scale reflection of the human "microcosm."</note>. For this reason, it is more likely that the <term>diurnal motion</term> <note>The apparent daily rotation of the heavens around the Earth every 24 hours.</note> of the world-machine proceeds from the circumference rather than from the center. This is clearly proven by two reasons: first, because a hundredth part of the vigor moving the center will proportionally move the circumference; just as is clearly seen in those mechanical machines described above, and conversely at the end; though these machines are made by art, it is certain they follow a natural logic. Second, because we have declared above that in the beginning of the world, the origin of actions proceeded from the highest fountainhead of forms; from whose source only the smallest part could descend to the earth, both because of its great distance from the <term>Empyrean heaven</term> <note>The highest point in the universe, believed by medieval and Renaissance scholars to be the dwelling place of God and composed of pure light or fire.</note> (for the center is furthest distant from its circumference) and because of the immense coldness of the earth. Indeed, in the earth there are four degrees of coldness <note>In Aristotelian physics, qualities like heat and cold were measured in four degrees; the earth was considered the "coldest" element, possessing the maximum fourth degree.</note>, and nothing of heat, unless it comes to it accidentally, either from the light enclosed in darkness during creation, or from the influences of the stars flowing into the bowels of the earth, as we said above. Nay, it is certain that the heaven is moved infinitely many degrees faster than the earth, because the entire appetite of its substance is filled by the vigor and virtue of the moving soul; nor is it anything other than the most active and clear substance of light itself. According to <term>Augustine</term> <note>St. Augustine of Hippo (354–430 AD), whose writings on the nature of light and spirit were foundational for later Christian natural philosophy.</note>, nothing is more mobile than light, nothing is swifter or more forceful than its motion, and nothing is subtler or more penetrating due to its refinement. Whence it is beyond doubt that, just as in mechanical things a greater union of forces usually moves and revolves a heavy thing more lightly and quickly, so also the multitude of these Spirits (which are all found in greater number and by a certain natural guidance more frequently in heaven, but more rarely and only by accident on earth) is the cause why the heavens are moved swiftly, and infinitely many degrees faster than the earth; because surely there the mover is multiplied unto infinity, and consequently moves its subject far more effectively. Hence, the arguments of <term>Gilbert</term> <note>William Gilbert (1544–1603), an English physician and physicist who argued in 'De Magnete' that the Earth rotates on its axis, a view the author is refuting here.</note> are certainly ridiculous, as he believes it impossible for the heavens to be led around in the space of 24 hours on account of their infinite magnitude; for if he had considered with a more exact judgment both the infinite nature of the agent and the disposition of the patient <note>In scholastic philosophy, the "agent" is the thing doing the action (the mover), and the "patient" is the thing receiving the action (the matter being moved).</note>, he would have very easily found the matter to be far otherwise than he thought. For, since there is in those regions an agent so immense and powerful, and the matter to be moved is also so well-disposed to receive the impressions of the agent lightly—so that it is very easily turned here and there by the impulse of the mover—who will allow himself to be persuaded that motion happens more slowly there than in that place where the agent is weak and fortuitous, and the matter to be moved is ill-disposed to endure motion? Now, no one has doubted that the seat and empire of the mover is in the heavens; and the matter to be moved is also subtle, spiritual, and light beyond imagination; whence it is more easily agitated here and there according to the disposition of the mover. Do we not see that the earth is heavy, and ill-suited to motion because of its weight, like a ston-

# ON THE CREATURES OF THE ETHEREAL HEAVEN.
<page-num>157</page-num>

...and that something so great should be raised by many hands, which because of its weight cannot be sustained by the power of a single living soul? But, if a part of the earth is dissolved into water, ten parts of that water will equal that single portion of earth in its weight. Similarly, one part of water corresponds in its weight to ten parts of air made through <term>subtiliation</term> <note>The process of "thinning" or refining a substance into a more spiritual or vaporous state.</note> from water; and the same proportion will exist between the substances of air and fire, and between that of fire and the <term>Fifth Essence</term> <note>The Aether; the purest element that makes up the stars and heavens.</note>. From these things it is manifest that earth dissolved into water requires a space ten times larger to contain it than it occupied before the dissolution and subtiliation; just as appears in the subtiliation of some brittle mineral performed by a sudden fire. For by violent heat, <term>vitriol</term> <note>A sulfate mineral, often used in alchemy to produce acidic vapors.</note> or <term>antimony</term> is suddenly dissolved into so many aerial and aqueous spirits that the body of the <term>Retort</term> <note>A glass vessel with a long neck used for distilling or heating chemicals.</note>, which previously held the mass calmly, can no longer contain that rarefied matter; rather, with a great crash, it is burst asunder by those spirits seeking a wider space. We say, therefore, that the proportion of the heaven in weight is equal to the proportion of the earth, and its body is well-disposed toward motion because it is subtle, thin, and easily moved. That of the earth, however, is ill-disposed toward movement, because it is thick, heavy, and very close to rest—a receptacle of darkness, which is the opposite of motion. For we see that the Sun, by the action of its light, daily raises the more subtle parts of the earth upward; but against the denser parts, namely rocks and others of that kind, it strikes in vain and without any sensible motion or subtiliation of parts. Finally, what should be thought of the nature of the earth, which all Philosophers have maintained is cold? Has not *Gilbert* <note>William Gilbert (1544–1603), an English physician and scientist who argued that the Earth is a giant magnet and rotates on its axis.</note> himself confessed, insofar as he was well-versed in Medicine, that coldness is the cause of the body's rest and stupidity, while heat—which has no dominion in the earth—grants motion, sense, and action to all things? And yet, by his "magnetic virtue," he was so led away from himself that he dared to call the whole body of the earth a *magnet* or *magnetic*; because he observed that some mineral of it, namely the lodestone, draws iron to itself and turns toward the poles of the world, and marks the variations of the poles; and so he seems to take the whole for its smallest part. In such a way we might also call the Sun a *Carbuncle* <note>A legendary glowing gemstone or a deep red garnet.</note>, because we see it shine at night <note>The author likely refers to the Sun's reflected light or its persistent power even when hidden.</note>. We conclude, therefore, that the heaven, in respect to its matter and its weight, can be led around in the space of twenty-four hours just as easily as the earth, since it is proportional to the earth in its weight; but in respect to its preparation, it is better and more easily moved than the earth, as was demonstrated above. But if even all these things are not enough for the reader, we finally call upon Holy Scripture as a witness of the truth in this matter, which Christians acknowledge it would be most impious and wicked to call false. It is found, therefore, in the book of **Joshua chapter 10**: *The Sun and Moon stood still in the midst of heaven, and did not hasten to set for the space of one day.* For it is not permitted to answer there that the passage in **Joshua** is to be understood as the natural motion of the Sun; for he speaks of the day and the diurnal <note>Daily.</note> motion, of which he confesses the Sun is the author, since without its standing or staying, the day could not be continued. But if we desire an even more manifest testimony, it is recorded in **2 Kings 20**: *God made the Sun go backward*; for the shadow of the dial was moved back by ten degrees. Similarly, this daily motion is excellently declared by the Psalmist in **Psalm 19:5**, where he clearly expressed that *the Sun, coming out of its tabernacle, moves from the rising to the setting.* Does it not also agree with our intention when Saint **Matthew** says in **chapter 24:27** that *lightning comes from the East to the West*, and that in a similar way the Lord Himself shall come from that part toward the setting on the last day? Indeed, in these passages it is expressly taught that the motions of the uncreated essence—such as the Son who is to come from the East to the West on the last day, and the Holy Spirit of the Lord moving upon the waters in the same way on the first day—[are the source of this motion]. And from this also the name of the East, as the "point from which" (*terminus a quo*), is always placed before the West in the sacred letters, as we said before; just as adoration is made toward that part, and tem...

## CHAPTER XVI.

->*On the nature of the natural motion of the Planets—that is, from west to east—and against those who say that the stars move according to the motion of their own spheres, from which comes the observation of Astrologers regarding the motion of the Epicycle.*<-

<margin>1</margin>
<note>The letter M is a decorative drop cap featuring figures and foliage.</note>
The motion of the heavens of the middle region from East to West is not called their natural motion, but rather a "violent" and <term>rapt motion</term> <note>Latin: "motus raptus." This refers to the idea that the outer spheres of heaven "snatch" or carry the inner spheres along with them in their daily rotation.</note>; (although in its own nature, that is, with respect to the <term>Empyrean heaven</term> <note>The highest, most spiritual part of the universe, composed of pure light and fire.</note> from whose lucid source it is derived, it also occurs naturally). For the natural motion of bodies is not to follow their source, but rather the motion of that specific mass in which the movement occurs. And here, indeed, a great mystery must be observed: namely, that just as celestial motion is distinguished from inferior things because of the excellence and perfection of its essence—having a motion entirely contrary to them, specifically a circular one, which befits all the more perfect parts of the world—so also a great contrarity of motion arises between the heavens of the perfect part of the world. 

For although there is no difference in the order and figure of the motion of either superior heaven, whether spiritual or corporeal, there is nonetheless a supreme contrariety in their starting points; since the former, being spiritual, is carried from the rising [East], and the latter, being material, is carried from the opposite point of the same hemisphere [West]. This opposition of circular motion is derived from that difference which exists between <term>matter and form</term> <note>The basic building blocks of reality in Aristotelian philosophy: "form" provides the identity and essence, while "matter" provides the physical substance.</note>; it is natural for each of these to produce contrary motions due to their separation from one another. 

Hence, heavy things (on the side of matter) tend to move downward, as was said regarding the heap of darkness, while light things move upward toward the natural seat of light, as we have sufficiently declared in the discussion of the fiery disposition and the production of the Sun. But indeed, where there is an optimal proportion in the agitated matter and the embrace of the ascending and descending form, there the lucid and radiant form is not sufficient to drive down the upward movements of the vaporous matter; nor is the ascending matter able to rise higher, since it is hindered by the proportionally descending rays. From this it happens that a double circular motion is induced in the same body, because of the diverse dispositions of the things—namely, matter and form—converging in its composition. For it is necessary that the ethereal bodies move circularly because of the equal proportion of their parts, which hinders any universal motion upward or downward. 

In what manner the first motion, which we called "rapt," is induced, we have described above. Here, however, we shall say a few things concerning the motion opposite to it, namely from west to east, which we have called the <term>proper motion</term> <note>The individual path a planet takes through the zodiac, independent of the daily rotation of the sky.</note> of the stellar bodies. Although it is the duty of the "form" to provide the name and the "being" to the body in which it exists, we nevertheless call that whole thing which is moved a "body" and a "mobile object." If it is moved by itself, it is moved by that intrinsic active principle, which we call natural motion—that is, the motion by which any body is moved according to the appetite of its own nature. But if it has its motion from an external principle, such as that "rapt" motion, in that case it is not a natural motion, but a motion by acci-

And here it must also be observed that the more thick matter abounds in the planetary body, the more violent its natural motion becomes, as is clearly apparent in the Planets situated from the Sun toward the earth, and especially in the moon. Similarly, this motion is that through which the stars produce vital, vegetative, and multiplicative effects in lower things, which in no way happen to bodies from the <term>rapt motion</term> <note>Latin: "motus raptus." The fast, daily rotation of the entire heavens from East to West that "snatches" everything along with it.</note> of the heavens; However, the Philosophers were certainly least able to give a natural reason for this motion of the stars; therefore they ascribed it to metaphysical causes, namely to <term>Intelligences</term> <note>Spiritual beings or angels traditionally thought to guide the planets in their orbits.</note>; Since we have attempted to refute their opinions above, we will explain the natural reason for the same in this place to the best of our ability. It is certain and proven that in every movable body there is action and resistance; action from an intrinsic moving principle, but passion and resistance from the movable subject: Thus that body of the Planets, agitated by the rapt motion of the heavens, appears to go backward by resisting; no differently than in the execution of gunpowder in a <term>bombard</term> <note>An early type of large-caliber cannon.</note>, where a great weight of matter is seen to move against the motion of the ignited Spirit, and because of its resistance against the fiery nature of the powder, it is perceived to go backward; and similarly, balls thrown against a wall are known to bounce back and move in the opposite direction; through which a great discrepancy between the motion of matter and form is clearly seen. Regarding this kind of resistance of matter, three things must be observed; namely, *the thick matter of the resisting substance; whose sole nature it is to be carried against the rapt motion;* because its natural disposition is to rest; Thus we see that if a small amount of water or oil, or even a stone, is placed on the peripheral surface of some wheel, and the wheel is rotated violently from East to West, as is demonstrated in this place,

...moves frequently <note>original: "tit," likely the conclusion of the verb "motitat," meaning to move frequently or keep in motion.</note>, it seems perhaps to seek the first origin of that snatched motion <note>original: "rapti illius motus." In period cosmology, the *Primum Mobile* (First Mover) "snatches" the lower spheres along with its rapid daily rotation.</note> in a circular movement, from whose center it was first derived, and toward which it desires to strive as if toward a more perfect place. From this, it is necessary that the body in which it exists is moved by its own motion. Or, because that first mover used to emit its effects from the East toward the West, it perhaps happens from there that it always strives toward that direction, whether faster or slower, without ceasing, according to the magnitude of its matter or the greater or lesser portion of its shining form. Does not that divine contemplator among men <note>A reference to the philosopher or mystic whose soul mirrors the heavens.</note> usually search more and more for God and God’s dwelling place by such a motion of his own form, and strive through a more probable path—namely, that path through which the greater effects of His power are perceived to descend?

It is manifest, therefore, that celestial bodies—both in respect to their imprisoned essential forms <note>The "form" here refers to the spiritual spark or soul of the planet trapped within physical matter.</note> and because of the dense arrangement of the matter of their bodies, whose nature is to resist the actions of light—move slowly against the action of the first heaven. This is not unlike a river descending from its origin and running toward the sea, which is violently struck and repelled by the contrary ebb of the tide; and yet the river is perceived to move by its own natural motion in that swelling flow, even against the movement of the sea. In this contrary motion, the sea—both in its magnitude and the force of its motion—is certainly to be compared to the Empyrean heaven <note>The highest, fiery level of heaven, associated with the presence of God.</note>, while the natural motion of the river is like that of a planetary body struggling against the motion of the sea.

We conclude, therefore, that those ruddy celestial fires are led around spherically and constantly revolved because of the matter of heaven moving upward and their rays striving downward. For the primary light turns the shining globe of the Planet, along with the entire matter of heaven, from the East to the West, elevating it in a certain way so that it might restore liberty to the light that is enclosed and captivated. But the heavier and more stubborn disposition of its matter, by striving downward, forces the Planet to struggle in the opposite way. Thus we also see a wooden ball or an apple dance in the air by the motion of water, and be carried more upward and downward, as is declared by the following experiment.

## CHAPTER XVII.

### *On the opposition of the form and matter of universal motions, and on the demonstration of their proportion in any heaven, and of the most effective placement of the true Sun in the middle heaven.*

Since in the preceding discourse we have demonstrated that **formal light** <note>original: *lucem formalem*. In this context, "form" refers to the spiritual, active principle that gives shape and life to the world.</note>, the further it tends downward, the more meager and weak it becomes; while **matter**, the further it ascends upward, becomes thinner, more spiritual, and smaller in its proportion; it is certain from this that the **formal splendor** descends from its source—that is, from the summit of the **Empyrean heaven** <note>The highest, most spiritual part of the universe, believed to be composed of pure fire or light.</note>—in the manner of a **conical pyramid** <note>original: *pyramidis coni*. The author envisions the universe as two overlapping cones: one of light pointing down, and one of darkness pointing up.</note> from its base toward the earth. Thus, its base is touching the concave surface of the Empyrean heaven, while its point (the apex) is at the earth. For **GOD** is its root, source, origin, and father. Conversely, because matter arose from the abyss of darkness, and all darkness is gathered into the terrestrial mass—whose prince is the **Devil**—it follows that the motion of matter is contrary to the motion of light; for the base of its pyramid is pressed into the center of the earth, while its point is raised up to the concave surface of the Empyrean heaven.

From this arises the composition of every heaven out of matter and form; hence the heavens are more **formal** (filled with light) the higher they are, and more subject to **matter** the lower and closer they are to the center. From this comes the most brilliant description of the **soul**, which is of a "middle nature," and the best possible demonstration of its ratio and composition. From this also comes the placement and arrangement of the **Sun** in the very middle of that middle nature; hence its nature is exactly tempered and life-giving, infusing **natural heat** and **radical moisture** <note>original: *calidum naturale & humidum radicale*. These were the two essential fluids believed by early physicians and philosophers to sustain life and vitality.</note> into lower things. From this arises the harmonic proportion of the world and the heavens, and even the various dispositions of the elements themselves. From this, finally, the lives of creatures and the receptacles of those lives, having been brought into actual existence, proceed from this kind of mixture. Even the infinite differences between species arise from the infinity of points emanating from the rays or diameters of these pyramids.

Indeed, from this come the **orders of angels**, where some are more eminent than others in their administration and rank, and closer to **GOD**. From this come the various temperaments of the ethereal bodies; from this, the distinction of the **sublunary elements** <note>The four elements (earth, water, air, fire) found below the moon, which are subject to change and decay.</note>, from whose diverse natures diverse creatures are born—some hotter or colder, others moister or drier than their peers. But in this place, as if in a mirror, an admirable mystery shines forth, by which it is clear that all these things flow from the one and only **GOD**. For outside the convexity of the final heaven, no "angle" is found except for that one and only Angle, which is altogether incomprehensible. And because the inner side of that **incomprehensible Triangle** has its position within the convexity of the Empyrean heaven, it is declared by this very fact that **GOD** communicates Himself to the world; as the heavens and all their virtue are strengthened by His verbal and spiritual essence. Thus, two angles of the one triangle are touching and, as it were, continuous with the convex surface of the Empyrean heaven, since the light of the world is derived from their rays, and by their presence (as uncreated beings) the world's most spiritual matter is now informed and created out of nothing.

In this way, we say that **GOD** is present everywhere; that is, His rays are seen in the **Universal Spirit** <note>original: *Spiritu universali*. The subtle substance that pervades the entire universe, acting as a medium for God's influence.</note> as if in a mirror. From this it is clear and manifest that **GOD** Himself, both outside the world and insofar as He exists present within the world, is one and triune. His **Monad** <note>original: *Monas*. A term for the indivisible "One" or the ultimate unity of the Divine.</note>, or the head of the triangle, descends all the way to the earth, so that by its presence it might restrain all darkness within the bosom of the earth. And so, since His triangular form is dispersed through the capacity of the **universal machine** <note>original: *universæ machinæ*. A common Renaissance metaphor for the universe as a complex, God-designed mechanism.</note>, **GOD** is said to be present everywhere—not indeed because He is contained by any place, but rather because He Himself contains

all things in itself; but just as in unity, the rays of some triple light are seen in a mirror to illuminate the mirror itself, so the image of that triple uncreated form in the material spirit of the world, illuminated by its presence, renders a **triangular form of light**; Hence the wise have called the world the image of GOD, for according to *Tris-megistus* <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure to whom various "Hermetic" philosophical and alchemical writings are attributed.</note>: GOD *before the creation of the world shone only within himself, and reflected his ardor back into himself.* <note>original: "DEUS ante mundi creationem in se ipso tantummodo reluxit, & in seipsum reflexit ardorem suum."</note> From this it follows, therefore, that the real Trinity is outside all things, and its reflection is within all things: yet that formal and lucid pyramid exists in the world in such a way that it is not separated from that which is outside the world; for the base of one is also the base of the other; and the splendor of the Trinity is as continuous with its real and incomprehensible essence as the rays of the Sun are to its body. And here the immense strength of GOD presiding over the light is perceived over that of the Devil, the Prince of darkness; for the **Light-shunner** <note>original: *Lucifugus*, a name for the Devil or a creature that avoids light.</note> hiding in the center of the earth is not outside the ray and power of GOD; but insofar as he exists as a creature, to that extent only the single base of the material pyramid arises from him; because he is contained and does not contain; for the base of the material pyramid is drawn from the center of the earth to every part of the spiritual circumference, so truly that the Devil is known to have nothing of infinity within himself. *Plato*, however, in his *Timaeus*, seems to take the **soul** for a portion of the spiritual pyramid composed with the formal splendor of its place; which he therefore calls a *harmonic number*, because it is constituted from the **monad, binary, ternary, and quaternary** number <note>These represent the numbers 1, 2, 3, and 4, which form the Pythagorean Tetractys, the sum of which (10) was considered the foundation of the universe.</note>; From the **monad**, namely from the ray of the unique mind; From the **binary**, for the primary substance of spiritual light, which is intellectual and rational, is fused from simply formal light and material spirit; From the **ternary**, because the life-giving substance of the middle heaven is made from a double portion of the Empyrean heaven and simple ethereal spirit; From the **quaternary**, because three of those parts constituting the middle heaven and the simple spirit of the lower darkness concur to the constitution of the Elementary heaven; Thus *Pythagoras*, not differing much from *Plato's* opinion, *asserts that the soul is composed of simple, double, triple, and quadruple proportion, and as if from a point, line, surface, and cube—understanding by the simple: pure uncreated form; by the double: the first composition from two; by the triple: the second from three; by the quadruple: the third made from four, or the elementary body,* as appears in the following pyramid.

Such, therefore, according to the ancient philosophers, is the proportion of the soul of the world <term>anima mundi: the "world soul" or the vital force that ancients believed animated the entire universe</term>, which in no way differs from our intention, since informed matter proportionally grows and increases as it descends. Therefore, the demonstration follows.

A. B. C. is the seat of the uncreated and incomprehensible mind, which, before the construction of the world, shone for itself and for no creature. But when it had thought within itself concerning the creation of the world, it said, **LET IT BE** <note>original: *FIAT*</note>; and immediately the splendor of its countenance shone in the darkness; *by which, to use the words of Trismegistus* <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the mythical founder of Hermeticism</note>, *all things were converted into a light that was excessive, pleasant, and sweet, and its rays, or rather the very appearance and form of* **GOD** *penetrated all the way to the center of the machine itself.* However, the matter of darkness tended in the opposite motion through the way of resistance, and in its upward motion was always diminished in its substance and made thinner, so that it finally assumed that shape of the pyramid E. ### TREATISE I. BOOK V.

...of the pyramid <note>The text begins mid-word, continuing "pyramidis" from the previous page.</note> is the true location of the Sun and the true position of nature’s equality; although all parts of the formal triangle in that heaven are exactly equal in any proportion with those of the material triangle contained in the same heaven. For this reason, it can most correctly be called the **heaven of equality**, as is declared in the demonstration itself.

Here, however, I would like the reader to know that this pyramid of ours, both formal <term>formal: relating to the "form" or spiritual essence, represented here as light</term> and material, is not to be understood mathematically in this place—the way we have depicted it for easier understanding—but purely physically. That is, it is to be understood in terms of how the aforementioned substances, both formal and material, proportionally descend or ascend from a thicker or stronger nature to a thinner and weaker one.

Thus, we understand the material elemental pyramid to be between the earth and the peak of the element of fire; its base is on the earth, and its apex is at the summit of the sphere of fire, because the smoky substance <term>fumosa substantia: the dense, physical matter of the lower elements that becomes more refined and "spiritualized" as it rises</term> gradually ascends by always diminishing—that is, moving from a coarser to a thinner spirit. Similarly, the base of the shining formal pyramid is in the aforementioned place, adjacent to the "four-quarters of light"; then, descending proportionally, it comes to three-quarters of light, then to two, and finally to a single unit or monad <term>monas: from the Greek "monos" (alone), meaning a single, indivisible point or unit of divine origin</term> at the angular point, which we have called the vanishing point.

<header>### CONCERNING THE CREATURES OF THE ETHEREAL HEAVEN.</header>

Therefore, at the point of intersection of the two triangles—namely the material and the formal—specifically between the points marked **h.** and **i.** <note>These letters refer to labels on a diagram, likely appearing on a previous page or in a related illustration, marking the boundaries of the solar sphere.</note>, is the true sphere of equality. This is the universal embrace between the male and the female, and the due and proportionate share of the true **sulfur** and **mercury** of the chemists <term>sulphur and mercury: in alchemy, these are not just chemicals but the two fundamental principles of nature—active/masculine and passive/feminine—whose union creates perfection</term>. From their union, the **solar infant** <note>original: "infans solaris." A poetic and alchemical term for the Sun, viewed as the perfect offspring of cosmic forces.</note>, the glory of the world and its true soul, was born. For this reason, it is most truly called the *sphere of the soul*.

This, then, is the explanation for the Sun’s position, site, and support in the middle of the heavens. Indeed, this intersection of the Triangles is the truest seat of the heart of the **Macrocosm** <term>Macrocosmus: the "Great World" or the entire universe, viewed as a living organism</term>, in which all things are tempered according to weight. For in this place, matter does not predominate, nor does form triumph too greatly, except in the sense that form splendidly, completely, and perfectly satisfies the hunger of matter. 

This is the true worldly unity forged from duality, in whose composition the Deity itself is seen as if in a mirror. Truly, this is that real marriage between heaven and earth, by which earthly things taste of the celestial, rejoice in them, and are blessed and given form by them. Thus we have the philosophical Sun and Moon contained within a single body; at their gracious sight, the whole world rejoices, grows green, flourishes, and every species is multiplied according to its own kind.

# BOOK SIX.

### *Concerning the Creatures of the Elemental Heaven.*

## Contents of this book.

In the creatures of the lowest heaven <note>original: "cœli infimi." In the cosmology of this era, the "lowest heaven" refers to the sublunary sphere—the atmosphere and the world we inhabit, where things are subject to change and decay.</note>, two things are to be noted:

*   **The differences between them**, for some are:
    *   **Inanimate**, namely bodies of a mixed composition <term>mixed composition: substances formed by the blending of the four basic elements—earth, water, air, and fire—rather than being pure elements themselves</term>:
        *   **First**, such as **metals** and **minerals**, which are discussed in Chapter 4.
        *   **Second**, such as **meteors** <term>meteors: in the historical sense, this refers to all atmospheric phenomena, including clouds, rain, wind, and lightning, as well as shooting stars</term>, which are discussed in the following book.
    *   **Animate** [living], also of a mixed composition:
        *   **Third**, of which kind are **plants** and **vegetables**, discussed in Chapters 2 and 3.
        *   **Fourth**, which are **animals**, discussed in Chapter 6.
*   **The causes of these differences themselves**, which are discussed in Chapter 7.

## CHAPTER I.

### *A brief recapitulation of the subjects of the preceding books:*
### *On the method of generation in these*
### *lower regions.*

<image-desc>A large historiated initial "I" depicts a man in classical robes standing beside a column within a rural landscape with mountains in the distance.</image-desc>

It has been clearly explained in the preceding parts that just as **GOD** was the highest source and origin of goodness, so too did He, freely and by His own divine will, wish first to impart His goodness to the world, and thereafter to the creatures of the world. Therefore, intending to create the world out of nothing, He separated a confused and formless spherical mass from the womb of matter <term>hyle: a Greek term used by scholars to describe the primordial, unformed "stuff" of the universe</term>. Wishing to decorate this mass with His goodness, He created a shining form on the first day; by its presence, those formless darknesses were separated from the face of the abyss and pushed down into the center of the sphere. From the thicker spirit of these darknesses arise the substances of the four elements—one of which passes and changes into another. We say that the substance of the ethereal heaven <note>original: "coeli ætherei"</note> differs from these elements both by the abundance of its shining form and by its own living activity. 

Therefore, it is manifest that the ethereal heaven is the masculine part of this material world and the acting, generating, and multiplying nature, because it is hotter, more virile, and partakes more of the lovely heat of the Empyrean heaven <note>Empyrean: the highest, most spiritual heaven, associated with pure light and fire</note>. Conversely, the lowest elemental part, which is thicker and colder, is like the female and the more passive portion of the world; its womb <note>original: "matrix"</note> is the earth itself, in which and from which all transitory creatures are formed. 

This heaven, therefore, is carried and moved everywhere above the elemental parts so that, in the manner of animals, it can be joined and mate with the lower parts as if with its female or passive partner. And just as the things to be produced in the lower regions are drawn into the womb—as if into the womb of the female whole—so also every faculty of generating is gathered into a single spherical body of the ethereal heaven, which we call the Sun, so that its light and essential heat may be distributed through the entire Macrocosm <term>Macrocosm: the "great world" or universe, viewed as a large-scale reflection of the human being</term>. 

And because that immense Architect <note>original: "Opifex"</note> wished to introduce varieties and changes into these lower things, He ordered that glowing fire—into which He contracted the nature of the middle heaven and upon which the life of all lower things depends—to move hither and thither. He did this so that by its variable presence and absence, things might more fittingly receive their constant changes in generation and corruption <note>generation and corruption: the philosophical terms for the cycle of birth/growth and decay/death</note>. 

Having learned these things, we must examine with a keen mind how the mating of the higher with the lower occurs—that is, by what method the soul <note>original: "anima"</note> is sent from heaven, and by what path it is received by a well-disposed earthly body. So that this may be more easily understood, one must know that the descent of the soul in its spirit to these lower regions is twofold: namely, one during the days of creation, and the other after the completion of the perfection of the world and its creatures. Now, the descent of the shining soul before the completion of creation was in a spirit veiled by darkness, as was said above. For the sparks of primary light were captured and are held in the first mass of darkness, or in the hidden spirit of the same, by...

<summary>This chapter recaps the creation of the world as a division between the masculine, active "ethereal heaven" and the feminine, passive "elemental earth." It establishes the Sun as the central agent of life and begins to explain how the soul descends from the heavens into physical bodies.</summary>

[transferred] ...by which the Solar body [is formed], as we said before; and sparks of secondary light are enclosed and imprisoned in the second mass of darkness, from which plants <term>vegetabilia: used here to mean all "growing things," including trees and crops</term> arise; and finally sparks of tertiary light are retained in the third mass of darkness—that is, in the dark spirit of the lowest heaven; from which minerals were created in the beginning, as we will explain more broadly below.

## CHAPTER II.

->*How the vegetative soul is daily infused from heaven*<-
->*into lower things after the completion of creation; and concerning the magnetic*<-
->*and attractive property*<-
->*of matter.*<-

Since the life of lower creatures comes from the luminous soul itself, and that soul originates from the celestial source, it must be diligently investigated by what path, or by what vehicle, this soul—contrary to its natural desire—is transferred through the ethers to these lower regions; and how it is infused into terrestrial bodies, considering that such bodies are entirely contrary to the soul's nature. This is true both because they are derived from the dark mass that opposes light, and because they are completely incapable of movement, making them the direct opposite of the soul’s naturally mobile faculty. 

It is clear, then, that just as a spiritual substance of the "middle heaven" <note>The *medium coeli* or middle heaven was thought to be the expansive region of ether between the starry firmament and the Earth’s atmosphere</note> is positioned between the lowest heaven and the source of light; so too, no spark from that source could descend into the world's lowest region unless it first passed through the Spirit of that middle region. Because all matter is always eager to reach the goal <note>original: "metam"</note> of its own perfection, and because the prime mover toward that goal is the essence of light, it is necessary that every material part of the middle heaven’s Spirit receives each passing ray of light as its own "form" <term>form: the active, organizing principle that gives matter its specific identity and life</term>. The Spirit then wraps this spark within its own sticky and tenacious substance and carries it down to the lower realms. This happens both because of the great connection between the substance of the middle heaven and that of the lower heaven, and also because matter draws the soul and form to itself through a certain magnetic power <note>original: "magnetica quadam virtute"</note>. 

Since the substance of the ether is thinner than the airy, physical body, it very easily penetrates—without any obstacle—into the "spermatic body" <note>In the science of this period, "spermatic" refers to the seed-essence or foundational matter of any living or growing thing</note> that is ready to receive it. Thus, the soul is enclosed in a double vessel: first within the airy spirit, and finally within a terrestrial body. In the same way, that ethereal spirit serving as the soul's vehicle is locked within the body just as the soul is locked within the spirit; and as the soul moves within every part of its spirit, so the spirit is spread throughout every part of the body along with its soul, creating movement toward the goal of perfection or ripening. 

In this way, spirits are added and joined to spirits, and souls to souls, every day and in great abundance, entering bodies that are well-prepared to receive them. From this process, the multiplication and generation of things happen constantly across the globe. This explains how a single grain grows into a multitude, how an animal grows without limit, and finally how minerals hasten toward the "perfection of gold"—which is to say, an inclination toward fixation <term>fixation: an alchemical term for making a volatile or "spiritual" substance stable and solid</term>. It is certainly a wonder to consider how this luminous nature, after the work of creation was finished, can descend from its rightful place with an ethereal and spiritual vehicle into these lower realms, since light and all fiery things naturally and eagerly seek to rise upward...

<meta>catchword: seek [re-...]</meta>

<summary>This page explains the mechanism by which the 'vegetative soul' or life force descends from the celestial realms into the physical world, using the ether as a vehicle and matter's 'magnetic' attraction as a pull. It attributes the growth of plants, animals, and the maturation of minerals into gold to this continuous daily infusion of heavenly light.</summary>

<keywords>vegetative soul, ether, magnetic virtue, generation, alchemy, fixation, perfection of gold, celestial influence</keywords>

--- Page 173 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of how the soul is drawn into material bodies. The previous page ended with "no spark from that source coul[d be perceived]," and this page begins by completing that thought.</meta>

...be perceived. Therefore, it is likely that this occurs because of the appetite of matter, and not from the form itself, since every natural thing tends toward its own proper place. And without a doubt, a great and powerful <term>virtus magnetica: magnetic power or virtue</term> exists in these lower composite things, attributed to them by God from the beginning, to attract and retain for themselves such a form as their species requires. By this power, the form is attracted to the matter according to the proportion and species of the desiring matter. 

We find a power of this kind in the stomach of animals, by which prepared nourishment is drawn into its cavity through the esophagus; likewise, in the lower limbs, the power of attracting "animal spirits" <note>In historical medicine, "animal spirits" were subtle fluids responsible for sensation and movement</note> to the lower parts is seen, through which touch is performed with judgment. There is, I say, a similar magnetic power and property in matter, by which higher virtues are attracted to lower things in the proportion assigned to the attracting species since its creation. 

For the matter of the earth does not desire the form of heaven otherwise than a woman desires a man; nor does it allure the form with its spirit—which it craves—by its flatteries and attractions otherwise than a lewd woman <note>original: *meretrix*</note> allures passing men by the seductive property and disposition of her coaxing eyes; indeed, it always desires the luminous form, and never ceases to attract it to itself as much as it is able. 

And although both the animal and vegetable kingdoms are richer in their ability to multiply than the mineral kingdom, they cannot approach the goal of perfection any closer than a man corrupted by greed and wealthy with much money can reach the height of immortality. For in every addition and union of a new form with the seed <note>original: *spermate*</note> of the same species, new corruptible parts of the elements are also added and joined because of the porosity of that species. This cannot happen in the mineral kingdom because of the hardness and "fixation" <note>In alchemy, fixation refers to making a volatile substance stable and solid</note> of that substance, or the constriction of its parts. Therefore, the mineral's treasure, acquired from the Sun and the rays of the Planets by the magnetic property of its matter, is continually hidden away and reserved for the perfection of its body. For this reason, it appears every year more brilliant and more similar to those solar rays, and it grows and multiplies in perfection and stability, albeit imperceptibly. That is, it is more fully decorated with formal beauty, as will be more clearly explained in the following chapters.

### CHAPTER III.

-> *That the earth is the mother of all bodies, both simple and composite,* <-
-> *and that the influences of the stars are like the seeds* <-
-> *of heaven cast into the womb of the earth; Concerning* <-
-> *the difference of composite* <-

<image-desc>Decorative woodcut initial letter 'M' showing a scholar or monk seated at a desk, surrounded by foliage and classical architecture.</image-desc>
MORIENUS THE ROMAN <note>A legendary 7th-century alchemist said to have taught the prince Khalid ibn Yazid</note> calls the earth the mother of all elements. For they strive vigorously to proceed from the earth and to return to the earth. Jean de Meun <note>original: *Mehunnius*. A French author famous for the Roman de la Rose; here cited for his philosophical and alchemical views</note> in the *Lamentation of Nature*, demonstrated clearly enough that the earth is the mother and nurse of all things, which she conceives in her womb from fire—that is, from heaven. Almost all the authorities of the philosophers conclude that heaven is the father of composite things, while the earth is their mother, carrying them in her own bowels. Ptolemy <note>Claudius Ptolemy, the Greco-Egyptian astronomer</note> and Chalcidius <note>A 4th-century philosopher known for his commentary on Plato's Timaeus</note> provide the reason for this, asserting that all celestial influences flow together into the depths of the earth; these are like the seeds <note>original: *spermata*</note> of things, just as if in

<meta>This page continues the discussion of how celestial forms are attracted to earthly matter, moving from the general principle of attraction to the specific hierarchy of all created things on Earth.</meta>

# TREATISE I. BOOK VI.

...are included in a common matrix. From this, due to the differing proportions of those descending virtues, various generations of various things are produced on earth, and diverse impressions arise. Once these things are understood, it remains evident that the earth is the mother of both simple and composite bodies, carrying them in her womb before their production, just like a female carries her fetus. We have already dealt quite abundantly with the simple bodies of the elements in the preceding sections. Here, therefore, we shall briefly discuss the differences among composite bodies, into which well-disposed "sparks" of essential light are continually infused.

The ancients generally divided composite and mixed bodies into five species of composition. The first *two* of these encompass inanimate bodies; for the mixtures of the *first composition* are <term>Meteors: in historical science, this refers to all atmospheric phenomena including clouds, rain, and wind</term>, formed from smoke, vapors, and exhalations. Those of the *second composition* are metals and other minerals. The subsequent species are animate (living); for mixtures of the *third composition* are plants and vegetables; the *fourth* are irrational animals; and the *fifth* or final species is the rational animal, Man, the most noble of all creatures. Because of his great similarity to the <term>Macrocosm: the "Great World" or the entire universe</term>, he is called the <term>Microcosm: the "Little World"</term>. 

Among these species of composite things, some are "perfectly mixed," such as minerals and all animate beings; however, only one is "imperfectly mixed"—namely, those of the first composition, which includes all types of meteors. It should be carefully observed that celestial light is more abundant in perfectly mixed bodies and is held more firmly within them than in those that are imperfectly mixed. Similarly, this light is more plentiful in animate beings than in perfectly mixed inanimate ones; and those with purely sensitive life <note>original: *animata mere sensitiva*; referring to animals that can feel but not reason</note> are richer in that celestial gift than those that are merely vegetative or irrational.

This is what the Philosophers seem to imply when they assert that *in intellectual beings, fire predominates; in sensitive beings, fire and air are equal; and in vegetative beings, water is weighed in equal balance with air*. In these latter ones, however, the dignity of the air is diminished because of the cold disposition of the water. Finally, *they maintain that minerals are composed mostly of earth and water*. From this, it is manifest that the celestial fire prevails most in humans, insofar as they are intellectual; next in other sensitive beings, insofar as air and fire predominate in them; then, at a more diminished level, in vegetables, insofar as their airy parts are tempered by the coldness of water; and finally, that the smallest portion of the luminous form is found in minerals, because earth and water hold dominion over them and, consequently, allow a smaller portion of fire into their composition.

***

## CHAPTER IV.

->*On the rise and origin of the perfectly mixed inanimate body.*<-

<margin>Jean de Meun in the *Complaint of Nature* <note>original: *Mehunnius in lament. natur.*</note></margin>
<margin>Augurello, *The Art of Making Gold*, Book 1 <note>original: *Augurel. Chrysop. lib. 1.*</note></margin>
Various opinions arise among those skilled in Philosophy regarding the life, generation, and vegetation of minerals. For *some* absolutely deny that they possess life, vegetation, nutrition, generation, or any generative seed; they instead maintain that minerals are created in the first instant from the substance of the four elements, and from that point on, they possess only essence. *Others*, however, maintain that they live hiddenly and secretly, and have the power to multiply and take on growth. They say that the Spirit which gives life is hindered by the excessive gloom and thickness of the matter; therefore, they contend that the minerals do not exercise that natural power and virtue with which they are naturally endowed. However, almost all who treat the matter of minerals with unanimous consent...

<meta>catchword: cause</meta>

<summary>This page explains the hierarchy of "mixed" bodies (Meteors, Minerals, Plants, Animals, and Man) based on their proportion of celestial light and elemental balance. It also introduces the debate on whether minerals possess a hidden form of life or are purely inanimate matter.</summary>

<keywords>Microcosm, Macrocosm, five compositions, hierarchy of nature, elemental balance, celestial light, mineral generation, Jean de Meun, Augurello</keywords>

--- Page 175 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of how composite bodies are formed from the descent of celestial virtues into earthly matter. Having established that the earth acts as a womb, the author now narrows his focus to the specific generation of minerals and metals within that "maternal" earth, contrasting the common philosophical view with his own light-based cosmology.</meta>

# ON THE CREATURES OF THE ELEMENTAL HEAVEN.

They place the general material cause of these [minerals] in a smoky matter, mixed from watery vapor and earthy exhalation. Specifically, they have designated the "near" and "proper" matter of minerals as <term>Sulphur and Quicksilver: in alchemy, these are the two fundamental principles—the masculine/combustible and feminine/metallic—from which all metals were thought to grow</term>, concluding generally that their <term>efficient cause: the force or agent that brings something into being</term> is threefold: namely, celestial, earthy, and watery heat mixing together and gradually "cooking" them in the veins of the earth. For the depths of the earth are most precious, since all celestial influences flow together into its vitals, and a heat effective enough to produce metals and precious gems is generated around the center of the earth.

<margin>Pliny, Ptolemy, Calcidius</margin>
Furthermore, they assert that the formative metallic and mineral power existing in the matter is derived from the light of the stars and the heavens, whose heat gathers together similar substances and separates the dissimilar from one another. The final cause is the mutual proportion of celestial and terrestrial virtues, effective for subduing and shaping metallic matter; for according to everyone, the heaven is the father, while the earth is the mother of all minerals, carrying them within her vitals like a fetus.

We, however, in this place according to our intention, will treat briefly of the origin of minerals first, and then of their life, growth, and multiplication, based on the traditions of several of the aforementioned philosophers. Above, in the fourth chapter of the preceding book, we declared that the solar body was born from the <term>Spirit of Darkness: the chaotic, unrefined primordial matter</term> thrust downward on the first day from the vitals of the <term>Empyrean Heaven: the highest, most spiritual part of the universe, often associated with pure light and the divine</term>, yet retaining a portion of that same essential light. Similarly, we said the vegetable body was formed from the Spirit of Darkness driven downward from the middle heaven by the presence and power of light, and from the "secondary" light. In this same way, we said the mineral body is forged from the Spirit of Darkness of the lowest heaven, together with a certain very weak spark of light, which we have called "tertian" or tertiary. For at the beginning of the third day of creation, the light of the second heaven (called secondary) penetrated downward and acted upon the threefold dark mass that had been driven down and beaten back into the lowest region.

But because two parts of that mass—one of which slipped down from the upper region and the other from the middle—snatched up a sufficient quantity of light from their own regions, the remaining part of that mass (proper to this lowest region) embraced in its vitals some quantity of this pursuing light which emanated from the source of the secondary light. Yet, because the nature of light is to thin or rarefy—wherefore, the greater its proportion and force, the thinner and more subtle it renders bodies—and since light in the other heavens is stronger and more powerful than in the lowest heaven, these portions of "tertian" light are not sufficient to refine and purify the dense and rebellious Spirits of the lowest darkness. 

From this it happens that, in this kind of mixture, the matter wins out entirely, and the form of the light is miserably and perpetually imprisoned in the belly of such matter. This is due to the excessive constriction or smallness of the pores in its body, and the great tenacity of the viscous Spirit. Hence comes the body’s thickness, weight, and solidity; hence also the scarcity of the living soul's substance in relation to its body, the sluggishness of the Spirit itself, the difficulty or "insensibility" of generation and multiplication, and its <note>original: "putativa ipsius sterilitas"</note> supposed sterility. For the soul, contaminated by heterogeneous parts wrapped in dark filth, is hindered in its movement because of the density of its Spirit, the strong composition of its bodily parts, and the untamed hardness and solid freezing of the body. The heating power of the enclosed light is therefore too meager to drive back the injuries of the mineral body's coldness. For this reason, it is not perceived to live or grow clearly in the manner of vegetables, nor is its soul (enclosed in its thicker Spirit) able to lift its head above the surface of the earth and strive toward its natural place following the example of plants; this is both because its power is weaker than that of vegetables, and because it is locked and held by firmer chains in the center of the mineral body. But since it is the appetite of every bodily thing to strive toward the goal of perfection, and since no perfection can be acquired, unless...

# TREATISE I. BOOK VI.

through that motion which a certain portion of light produces, therefore that light enclosed in the Spirit of the mineral body, although tiny and only like a spark, desires to perpetuate its body. In the manner of fire, it strives to overcome the earthy and watery disposition of that body through the mediation of its Spirit, and to subject that entire rebellious subject to its own will and nature. Thus we see an entire house catch fire and be reduced to flame by the presence of a single spark of fire. For the spark of light does not cease to labor within the mineral body until, gradually emerging as the victor, it reduces the entire mass of its body into its own nature, active quality, and splendor; just as is clearly demonstrated in the generation of gold. For although in its beginning its subject is like a thick and dark prison for its soul, in which it is detained by what seem like the strongest chains—so that it is scarcely perceived to move in its center—nevertheless, with the passage of time, it gradually draws the virtues of the luminous influences, which penetrate even to the center of the earth, to its aid. Indeed, even the mineral body itself seems to provide this same aid by its own <term>magnetic virtue: an attractive power believed to draw celestial or spiritual energy into physical matter</term>. From this, every year and every hour, though imperceptibly, the vigor of the mineral soul grows, and according to the degrees of its increase, that body is also rendered more perfect, 
<margin>/</margin> more shining, and more fixed. From these things it is manifest that minerals live secretly, flourish essentially, and hasten toward the height of perfection and are sublimated, however slowly, contrary to the assertion of the aforementioned philosophers who wished to take life and growth away from them. Therefore, the <term>Chemical Philosophers: early alchemists who sought the transformation of base matter into "gold" or spiritual perfection</term> did not act imprudently when they established a twofold mineral matter: 
<margin>/</margin> *one* they called masculine, simple, and pure, which they named sulphur, tincture, fire, the Sun, and the agent; and the *other* they called female, quicksilver <gloss>mercury</gloss>, water, the <note>original: "sputum lunae"</note> spittle of the moon, the Moon, and the patient. By the *former*, they understood its soul with the element of fire; by the *later*, they understood the vaporous spirit, which they called the waxing Moon in the <term>Hermetic: relating to the alchemical tradition of Hermes Trismegistus</term> and natural operation, according to the increase of its fiery soul. From the mutual conjunction of these, therefore, 
<margin>/</margin> the metallic body of every species is made; and the difference between mineral species arises from a greater or lesser part of sulphur and quicksilver. For if a smaller portion of sulphur and a larger portion of water concur in the mineral composition, there is generated from that composition one of those metallic bodies of which seven species are numbered: namely, Gold, or silver, or lead, or tin, or copper, or 
<margin>//</margin> iron, or quicksilver, which they call common Mercury. But if the quantity of sulphur in the composition exceeds the quantity of water, there arise from such a mixture those mineral bodies which are not of the number of the seven aforementioned metals—for example, salts and other friable <note>easily crumbled</note> things. It must be observed here, and indeed most diligently, that the freer the sulphur and Mercury of each species of mineral are from the pollution of darkness and the irregularity of heterogeneity, the purer, more perfect, and more precious a mineral of either species is produced. We conclude, therefore, that if the passive matter be gross, sooty, and thick, and its active form be conversely tiny and weak—the kind of composition found in the bowels of the earth—then that species of composite arises which is called "mineral and metallic" by the <note>original: "Arabibus"; referring to the medieval Arabic alchemical tradition that heavily influenced European thought</note> *Arabs*. It is indeed that which is forged from earth and water; but since earth itself predominates in its composition, therefore its life or soul is chained in a most opaque and dense prison; whence it is not perceived to move sensibly, or to act in the open.

## CHAPTER V.

### *On the origin of the insentient or vegetative animated body, and that it grew on the third day immediately after the existence of minerals.*

---

<image-desc>Ornamental woodcut initial 'Q' decorated with scrolled leaf and vine patterns.</image-desc>ALTHOUGH the matter of vegetables is softer and more manageable than that of minerals, and its heat is more effective and freer to carry out its operations; nevertheless, because it was necessary for the earth to be strengthened and perfectly established before things could flourish from it; and since the more solid part of the earth—which is like the shadow of the lowest heaven <note>original: "infimi cœli," referring to the elemental realm closest to Earth</note>—fell more quickly toward the center due to its weight than those parts which are lighter and thinner, it necessarily followed that after the earth reached its natural state of rest, the sparks of its light (though tiny and weak at first) began to act according to the order and desire of nature. Then, the sparks of those parts of the earth closest in weight to these followed; for they were strengthened and came to rest in their destined place immediately after the first ones; such is the matter of the darkness of the second heaven. For it is certain that created things had their origin all the sooner the less heat they possessed at their beginning. This is because a larger amount of enclosed light makes the spirit of the shadow that encloses it lighter. Therefore, minerals existed before vegetables, yet both were made on the same day; for the presence of the Sun was not required for their creation. Later, animals were created after the <term>sublimation: the process of refining a substance into a higher, more spiritual, or gaseous state</term> of the Sun in the heart of the ethereal heaven; for a greater power of light and heat was necessary for sentient beings. 

The vegetative body, therefore, although it consists of the four elements, is nevertheless dominated by water and air; however, the status of the air is diminished by the presence of water. It acquired its Soul and Spirit at its origin in this way: namely, when the secondary light of the second heaven <note>The "second heaven" typically refers to the sphere of the fixed stars and planets above the elemental world.</note> had reverberated against the shadows, and the sparks of that light were captured by the bonds of their more tenacious spirits, these shadows or darknesses of the second region were joined with those of the final region. They then thickened into the substance of the earth gradually, according to the gravity and weight of each. Once this spiritual subject of vegetables was placed and confirmed near the outer part of the earth, the sparks of the soul or enclosed light began to act upon it. Through their action, they attempt to stretch upward with their "vehicle" <note>In Hermetic philosophy, the "vehicle" or *vehiculum* is the subtle spirit that carries the soul into matter.</note> toward the beginning of their source. But because there is a great proximity between the substance of the ether and that higher part of the lower heaven, the element of fire accompanies the aforementioned ethereal Spirit with its soul, rising and being sublimated from the earth along with it. 

And since one nature of an element always embraces and chains to itself the nature of the element closest to it, the heat of the air is joined to the heat of the fire, the moisture of the water to the moisture of the air, and the coldness of the earth to the coldness of the water (as is clearly demonstrated through an experiment below). Consequently, the subtle and more volatile part of the earth mixes itself with the parts of the water and ascends in them. Thus, the soul enclosed in the spirit of plants naturally desires to ascend upward. It received help for its exaltation before the creation of the Sun from the <term>erratic stars: the planets, so called because they appear to wander across the sky compared to the fixed stars</term> and from the rays of light emitted from the higher heavens; and after the creation of the Sun, it received help from the Sun itself, which on the fourth day drew to itself the rays—both visible and invisible—that had been dispersed through the hollow of the middle heaven. For the rays exiting from the Sun penetrated the dense surface of the earth and, with their heat, opened its pores which had been constricted by cold; they thus provided a path of exit for vegetables. Hence, the stronger and more perpendicular the solar rays are, the better and more quickly things come forth from the earth, and the more easily and higher they extend above the ground. It is for this reason, therefore, that in the spring-

It describes the "spirit" of plants striving to rise toward the heavens, aided first by the stars and later by the Sun's heat opening the earth's pores.</summary>

--- Page 178 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the hierarchical creation of life, moving from the formation of minerals to the biological mechanics of plants and herbs, specifically focusing on how solar light interacts with internal "sparks" to cause growth.</meta>

->TREATISE I. BOOK VI.<-

In spring and summer, plants and herbs arise upon the earth, grow, and multiply; and the reason for this kind of growth <note>original: "vegetationis," referring to the process of becoming a vegetable or plant</note> is as follows: namely, the solar rays extending from the east join themselves with the light enclosed within the vegetable vehicle <note>original: "vehiculo vegetabili," the subtle substance that carries the life-force</note>, and they lift it up gradually but invisibly, drawing it upward through the pores of the earth. Moreover, the fire of this kind of plant is linked to the Quintessence <note>original: "Quintæ essentiæ," the "fifth essence" or purest spiritual element beyond the four physical elements</note>; thus, it is necessary for the fire to be carried upward along with the light and its vehicle. Air is related to the heat of this fire and embraces it; therefore, since air is adjacent to fire and bound to it by the bonds of friendship, it ascends immediately after the fire; similarly, water follows air, and earth follows water. 

But, because the earth cannot naturally ascend from its place due to the heaviness of its substance, it is necessary that the base and foundation of the plant be fixed in the earth as its natural place. Hence, the soul of the plant, striving upward with its Spirit toward its own proper seat <note>i.e., the heavens</note>, is hindered by the weight of the root of its body fixed in the earth, and it cannot strive higher than the custom of its species allows. Therefore, the eastern Sun, looking upon that very shining spark of the plant, is perceived to stretch and increase the plant little by little every day; this is why things are said to grow and flourish. 

Here, however, it must be especially observed that since the coldness of the earth is joined to that water, it is likely that the subtle part of the earth, ascending with the parts of water and mixing with them, constitutes the visible body of the seed or "egg" <note>original: "ovi," the author uses the concept of an egg to describe the protective casing of a seed</note> of the plant itself, in the center of which the soul is contained with its Spirit. Similarly, the remaining two invisible elements are hidden within the aforementioned visible ones: namely, fire in the earth and air in the water. 

Furthermore, the vigor of this soul is dispersed throughout the entire vegetable body, just like that of an animal; for it is virtually in the whole and in every part of it, through which every part of the plant grows and increases equally and proportionally with every other part. As for why the multiplication of the plant's egg or seed occurs unto infinity, the reason is certainly at hand; for since all multiplication proceeds from the vigor of the soul, it is necessary that there also be an increase of the soul itself, since the single soul of an individual cannot give form <note>original: "informare," the philosophical concept of giving a living shape or "form" to matter</note> to many individuals. 

But since this soul is nothing other than a spark of the essential light of the middle heaven <note>The sphere between the divine realm and the physical world</note>, it is necessary that this kind of spark be increased and strengthened by the addition and conjunction of other sparks of the same source, proportional to the species, by which infinite bodies of the same species are usually formed. This is indeed achieved by means of the solar rays; for rays are joined to rays, and spirit to spirits, no differently than sparks of fire to sparks, and drops of water to drops. From this, therefore, it follows that from one grain come infinite ones, and from one seed come innumerable ones; and from this it also happens that seeds buried in the matrix <note>womb or mother-substance</note> of the earth are wonderfully affected and delighted by the approach of the sun, through which they become rich, grow, and multiply. 

Indeed, this is that nature which generates all things, and nourishes that "ligament of the elements" <note>the force that binds the four elements together</note>, and the power of mixing, which completes the mixtures of the elements in every thing of this sublunary world, and impresses the form appropriate and proportional to its species, by which any thing is distinguished and separated from any other. We conclude, therefore, that where the matter is thinner and softer than that of minerals, and the heat of light is stronger, there that composition will lift itself upward above the surface of the earth, and will have a root as its foot and base—which we call a plant or vegetable. Because of the softness of this composition, the ethereal light and heat enclosed within it is more agile, free, and lively than that of a mineral composition. For the thinness and subtlety of the body is the cause of the soul's greater liberty, vivacity, and agility. 

Regarding the virtue of vegetable individuals, both hidden and manifest, and regarding their chemical preparation <note>original: "chimica eorum præparatione," referring to alchemical distillation and extraction</note>, we shall treat in the 4th Treatise of the second volume.
