...an ill-fated conjunction of the higher bodies: <term>Appianus</term><note>Petrus Apianus (1495–1552), a German humanist known for his works on astronomy and cartography.</note> predicted such a Comet would come in the year 1532. They also teach that foul mists are produced and resolved into dust when the Moon, after its conjunction or aspect with the Sun, seeks out Saturn (acting as the disposer of the heavens) and regards him with some aspect. They maintain that clear, white clouds mixed with redness are generated by the application of the Moon to Jupiter, when he holds dominion over the sky, after her conjunction or aspect with the Sun. They say that rain most often arises from the resolving faculty of the Moon while staying in a <term>moist mansion</term><note>original: "mansione humida," referring to one of the lunar mansions or signs associated with the element of water.</note>. Furthermore, they hold that when Mars is the disposer of the <term>Horoscope</term><note>The Ascendant or the rising sign at a specific moment.</note>, he generates saffron-colored clouds and produces thunder and lightning in the summer time, especially when the Moon, after her conjunction or aspect with the Sun, regards Mars. Indeed, they believe that massive thunder and lightning bolts arise if the Moon, after separating from Mars, regards Mercury. They assert that Venus alone, as lady of the heavens, produces thin and long-lasting rains when the Moon approaches her after separating from the Sun. Finally, they hold that when Mercury is stronger in the celestial figure and joined with the Moon, he generates great and rapid winds; they also claim that some winds arise from the power of the Moon passing through a <term>dry mansion</term>. They even affirm that snow happens in winter when some aspect of Saturn predominates with the Moon, and that hail occurs with thunder when a strong Saturn regards Mars in the summer, and so on for the rest. From these assertions of the astrologers, we conclude that our aforementioned opinions on the origin of <term>Meteors</term><note>In this period, "meteors" referred to any atmospheric phenomenon, including rain, wind, and lightning, not just falling stars.</note> are not fabrications, but are very close to the truth itself.

For in the composition of a Comet, they say the natures of Mars and Mercury concur, and they have spoken truly: for a spark of **Martial substance** flowing beneath from on high is like the soul and life of the Comet itself. Its body and substance, however, is entirely **Mercurial**—that is, clumped together from the inconstant substance of the most subtle air, having the aptitude, in the manner of Mercury, to take on any form or impression. What better way is there to argue for the generation of clouds (namely, while the air is thickened by the blast of opposing winds, which condensed mass we have called a cloud) than the fact that they are primarily generated from the aspect and opposition of different planets, which excite distinct winds in these lower regions according to their various influences? For in the aspect of the Moon with a predominating Saturn, the humidity of the one is repugnant to the dryness of the other. From a conflict of this kind, a contrary motion arises in the air, compressing it so that a cloud is generated therefrom. Similarly, two winds of contrary condition are produced in the summer time from the opposition, conjunction, or other malevolent aspect between a predominating Saturn and Mars. For Mars, because of his familiarity with fire, gathers sulfurous vapors and moves them by his power in the form of a wind against the breath produced by Saturn, just as his own body moves against that of Saturn in the ethereal heaven.

Saturn, however, being cold not only in motion but also in quality, is opposed to Mars. Whence the coldness of Saturn ignites the sulfurous matter gathered by Mars in no different a way than the spring water ignited the other artificial mass described in **Experiment 2** of the preceding chapter; hence thunder exists. But because Saturn is stronger, hail also falls. Similarly, if Jupiter has dominion in the heaven and regards Mars in the summer time with some malevolent aspect, he induces diverse and opposing motions in the air, since the humidity of Jupiter is contrary to the dryness of Mars. Therefore, that humidity of Jupiter, although warm, ignites the inflammable matter gathered by Mars in no different a way than the humidity of saliva ignited that substance described in **Experiment 3** of the preceding chapter. In that case, the humidity of the saliva, penetrating and submerging itself into that fire-bearing body, brings that fiery force—which was hidden and existing only in potential—into an inflammable act. The composition of that body is as follows:

Take 1 lb. of <term>calamitha</term><note>Likely Calamint (the herb), often used in alchemical fire-recipes, or potentially a mineral calamine.</note>, 4 oz. of asphalt, 4 oz. of saltpeter, 3 oz. of live sulfur, and 6 oz. of liquid varnish. Let them all be reduced into the form of a paste or loaf, which ought to be rolled in

Regarding what pertains to the origin of winds, it is certain that these <term>Planets</term><note>In this context, referring to the traditional seven wandering celestial bodies believed to influence earthly events.</note> provide them with great activity, and that the air itself is more prone to agitation when it senses disturbances in the ether; indeed, there is such an affinity between the air and the ether that by the motion of one, the other is most easily moved. There is no less harmony between the substance of one and the other (for they are, as it were, touching) than between the top of a string stretched from the peak of some tower to its base and the lowest part of that same string; for if its upper part is shaken, its lower part will feel it. This, then, is the cause why drier exhalations and vapors rising from the earth more easily move the air here and there according to the inclination of the Planet exciting such motions.

## CHAPTER VIII.

->*That rain, snow, thunder, lightning, winds, and finally earthquakes and springs themselves sometimes arise from supernatural causes.*<-

<image-desc>Decorative drop cap 'C' with floral and foliate scrollwork patterns.</image-desc>

CERTAIN it is and proven by experience itself that <term>meteors</term><note>In early modern science, "meteors" referred to any atmospheric phenomena, including rain, wind, and lightning, not just falling stars.</note> of every kind sometimes occur by divine will or permission beyond the laws of nature. For the sacred Scriptures testify that Almighty God has sometimes appeared in a cloud, and descended in horrendous thunder and ineffable lightning, and sometimes proceeded before the Israelites in the form of a pillar of fire, and showed Himself under the form of fire in a <note>original: "sepe," usually a hedge or bush, referring to the Burning Bush.</note> bush, and that a spring gushed from a dry rock at the command of His divine power; and infinite things of this sort have occurred. Sometimes also evil angels, with the consent of the Most High, can excite storms and lightning; for the "aerial powers," whom we said in *Book 4* are those <term>Demons of the sixth order</term><note>A classification of spirits believed to inhabit and manipulate the air.</note>, mingle themselves with thunders, lightnings, and thunderbolts, corrupt the air, and excite storms. From this order are those four angels mentioned in the <term>Apocalypse</term><note>The Book of Revelation 7:1.</note>, to whom it was given to harm the earth and the sea, holding the four winds blowing from the four corners of the earth. Indeed, in this order of Demons is contained that Prince of spirits whom we previously called <term>Meririm</term><note>A demon often associated with the "midday devil" (daemonium meridianum) mentioned in Psalm 91.</note>, that is, the "Midday Demon," a spirit burning and raging at noon, whom Paul in the letter to the <term>Ephesians</term><note>Ephesians 2:2.</note> calls the "Prince of the power of this air," as we mentioned before. According to <term>Psellus</term><note>Michael Psellus (c. 1017–1078), a Byzantine scholar famous for his treatise on demonology.</note>, aerial demons also disturb the air to the ruin and damage of humanity, and excite waves and storms in the sea and lakes, by which they utterly sink ships loaded with men and overwhelm everything present with water. There are also still others who cause chasms and trembling of the earth and stir up fire-breathing winds. For we read in the <term>first chapter of Job</term><note>Job 1:19.</note> that the Devil, by divine permission, stirred up a violent wind blowing from the wilderness, by whose blast the house in which the children of Job were feasting was leveled to the ground. What then should be said of the unspeakable and Diabolical power of the <term>Cacomagi</term><note>From the Greek 'kakos' (evil) and 'magos' (magician); refers to malevolent sorcerers or witches.</note>, when so many true and non-fictitious histories have written entire volumes about their frequent use of stirring up storms and other wickednesses perpetrated by the Demon? Among these, <term>Remigius</term><note>Nicholas Rémy (c. 1530–1612), a French judge and demonologist who claimed to have sentenced hundreds to death for witchcraft.</note> in *Book 1, Chapter 25*, states that he heard from the free and spontaneous [confession] of two hundred Cacomagi...

...by the assertion of <term>witches</term><note>original: "veneficarum." While often translated as "poisoners," in this context it refers to those practicing harmful magic or witchcraft.</note>, that on fixed and certain days they were accustomed to come in groups to the bank of some pool or stream, where very often, having received a rod from a Demon, they were used to beating the waters for a time until they raised abundant vapors and fumes. By these vapors they were carried on high, and they perceived themselves to be enclosed in thick and dark clouds. In the same chapter, the author alleges many other examples of clouds produced by the Diabolical art, which I leave to be read thoroughly by the diligent reader. Furthermore, in many places by the same author, one finds the miraculous power of witches to stir up thunder, lightning, hail, and clouds with the help of a Demon. From these accounts it is manifest that, although <term>meteors</term><note>In early modern science, "meteors" referred to any atmospheric phenomenon, including rain, wind, and lightning, not just falling stars.</note> of this kind are purely natural in their essence, nevertheless the evil Demons themselves (who, although they were made unhappy and became exiles from their fatherland because of their sins, were nonetheless undoubtedly most noble in respect to their origin and equal to the good angels) have as much power to produce meteors of this kind as the Planetary bodies themselves. Indeed, they can exercise this power even more promptly because of their constant association with the air—provided that GOD permits it; for without the creator's assent, they can achieve nothing. However, in what manner or by what reason these malevolent angels are accustomed to move these tempests, it is certain that they have not wished to reveal this to men, but rather to act more sophistically with them and their servants. For it is not likely that a rod, or a powder, or some incantation could have such power; but rather, the quiet and tranquil air is agitated by the invisible motion of these Demonic bodies, and winds of every condition are suddenly stirred up, and clouds, thunder, hail, and other things of this sort are brought in as if in a moment of time. What wonder is it, then, if the weak grasp of men is daily deceived by Diabolical subtlety, since everything the Devil does, he performs secretly and invisibly? By the allurements of flattering words, or even by threats, he endeavors to withdraw human nature from divine worship and make it rebellious against its creator.

> *Indeed, since only GOD the Best and Greatest can resist these attempts, repress his rage, and see and look deeply into his hidden works, the King of Kings and Lord of the world must be assiduously entreated from the innermost depths of the heart. We pray that He may mercifully preserve us—exposed as we are to every kind of misery—from the tyranny of that adversary and his serpentine crafts, and that He may restrain the demon's untamable wickedness with the bridles of His justice and goodness; for without His protection, nothing at all is safe, and nothing is immune from destruction. For the Lord alone is our supreme helper, without whose aid we cannot be delivered from evil.*

## CHAPTER IX.

### On the origin of springs.

Springs, from whose meeting brooks and rivers arise, are born in the <term>bowels of the earth</term><note>A common early modern metaphor for the subterranean regions where minerals and waters were thought to be "generated."</note> in various ways: Either by the expulsion of whole air held in the immense concavity of the earth, or by the rarefaction and thinning of some part of that same air and simultaneously of water hiding in the veins of the earth, or finally, by the penetration of air into the very bowels of the earth filled with water, the waters are forced to tend upward and gush forth through the pores of the earth. Moreover, the air enclosed in the belly of the earth is expelled by an immense quantity of water coming from the clouds, passing through the pores and...

# ON IMPERFECTLY MIXED BODIES

<image-desc>A woodcut or engraving illustrating a scientific experiment. A large metal pot with three legs sits over a burning fire of logs. Densely billowing clouds of steam or vapor rise from the pot. Suspended above the pot by a hook and chain is a shallow, concave lid or pan. ->*The water of pot **A.** boiling by the power of fire emits vapors which, striking against the concavity of the suspended pan **B.**, are resolved into drops by the cold disposition of the pan.*<-

How air is thickened into water through <term>subtiliation</term><note>In early modern science, "subtiliation" refers to the process of a substance becoming "subtle" or refined, such as water turning into vapor or air expanding. Here, the author discusses the reverse—how refined air might be "thickened" back into liquid.</note>, we have demonstrated by the experiment expressed at the end of book 2. For this reason also, springs are perceived to flow more abundantly in the winter time; because that central heat is preserved and strengthened through <term>antiperistasis</term><note>From the Greek *antiperistasis*, a philosophical concept where a quality is intensified when surrounded by its opposite, such as heat being concentrated inside the earth when the surface is chilled by winter air.</note>. Whence it is known that they act more strongly, and that springs are for the most part warmer in that season. There is yet another clear demonstration, and a common one, denoting this action of the air: For if a <term>bombard</term><note>original: "bombarda"; an early type of large-caliber cannon or mortar used for throwing heavy stones.</note> is loaded with <term>saltpeter</term><note>original: "sale nitri"; potassium nitrate, the primary oxidant in gunpowder. The author describes it as "nothing other than the congealing of air by the power of moist heat."</note> and sulfur, this composition in its thick substance can very easily be contained within the "belly" of said bombard; but if that condensed air, by the force of external heat bringing the faculty of the sulfur into action, is "thinned" (subtiliated), immediately that air, now reduced to a vaporous matter, cannot tolerate that place which—owing to its changed condition—is now too narrow and cramped. Instead, desiring a more spacious place where it can rest more, it suddenly breaks out into the open air and is converted as if into a dark cloud. Sometimes also air trapped between two bodies of water lifts the lighter mass of water upward and propels it from its seat even to the surface of the earth, and occupies that place for itself, as is demonstrated by the following experiment:

# TREATISE I, BOOK VII

*Water falling into the uppermost region of the basin* <note>original: "lebetis"</note> *through tube E. F. descends into the lowest region of the vessel A. B. C. D., and expels the air contained in it into the middle region of the same vessel through tube G. F. The air entering into this region gradually drives the water contained there through tube I. L., nor will the water cease to flow through that tube I. L. until the lower region is filled with water.*

*This driven water then constitutes, for the most part, a* <term>perennial spring</term><note>A spring that flows continuously throughout the year. # ON IMPERFECTLY MIXED BODIES

*With vessel A. B. C. D. filled with water, the water itself, climbing above the opening H. of the tube G. H., will exit the vessel through the same tube: nor will it cease to ascend through the larger tube E. F., until the entire water of the vessel has been evacuated through tube H. G.*

*Hero makes mention of this experiment in his book on pneumatics* <note>original: "lib. spiritualium," referring to Hero of Alexandria’s "Pneumatica"</note>*, experiment 2.*

Moreover, that air, refined <note>original: "subtiliatus," meaning made thinner or less dense</note> by heat, can expel water from the bowels of the earth to its surface is declared by this following experiment:

<image-desc>A technical scientific diagram illustrating a pneumatic experiment. At the top is a square container or furnace labeled C, D, E, F with flames rising from it. A vertical tube labeled G-A connects the base of the furnace to a large spherical vessel below. This sphere, labeled A and B, is half-filled with a liquid. A curved tube labeled I and H emerges from the side of the sphere and arches over into a smaller, ornate pear-shaped vessel labeled N, L, M, P, O, which sits on a pedestal base. The diagram shows the path of air and liquid between the three components.</image-desc>

*Let water be poured into sphere A so that half of it is filled; and let the opening B be plugged. Therefore, by the presence of fire ignited upon the small furnace* <term>arula</term><note>Latin for "small altar," here used to describe a portable brazier or heat source.</note> *C. D. E. F., the thicker air existing in it is expelled through thinning* <note>original: "subtiliatione"</note> *through tube G. A. into sphere A; this air then further thrusts down the liquid contained in it into vessel N. L. P. O. through tube H. I. L. M.*

*For the explanation* <note>original: "Proratione," likely a contraction of "Pro ratione"</note> *of this experiment, see Hero's 37th experiment.*

Finally, there is a unique experiment, which we expressed at the beginning of this treatise, in whose observation a wonderful secret regarding the flowing of springs from the earth will be perceived; for air enclosed in the earth by <term>rarefaction</term><note>The process by which a substance becomes less dense, usually through heating.</note>, and similarly water fleeing the solar force, tends toward the empty center of the earth through thinning. While the Sun remains perpendicular over the earth, bringing on the summer season, that denser air and water hide away in the bowels of the earth around that center; but indeed, as the Sun recedes from that summer region and winter approaches, the thick air and water return again from the center to their former place—that is, to the surface of the earth—and flow as before.

# TREATISE I. BOOK VII.

<image-desc>An engraving depicting a scientific demonstration of pneumatic or hydraulic principles. Two vessels are connected by a long, arching tube labeled "E" at its apex. On the right, a spherical glass flask labeled "A" rests on a rectangular pedestal "D"; the tube joins it at point "B". On the left, a decorative ceramic-style jug with a handle, labeled "C", receives the tube at point "F". The apparatus is positioned in an outdoor coastal landscape. In the background, a sailing ship is on the water, and hilly terrain is visible. In the upper right corner, a personified sun with a face shines through the clouds, emitting rays and vapors. A faint arc, possibly a rainbow or atmospheric boundary, is visible in the sky behind the vessels. Faint, additional labels (G, H, I, 3, 4) appear in the sky area.</image-desc>

For the demonstration of this experiment, see the end of Chapter 6 in Book 1.

And truly, this is a most evident demonstration, by which no one of sound mind can deny that springs—by reason of the presence or essence of the **sun** <note>original: "solaris"</note>—gush forth more or less from the veins of the earth. It is certain, however, that the increase of these springs arises from the reasons mentioned above; for in winter, the **central heat** <term>calor centralis</term><note>The internal heat of the Earth, which early scientists believed drove many geological and meteorological processes.</note> is increased through **antiperistasis** <term>antiperistasis</term><note>A historical scientific theory that a quality is intensified when surrounded by its opposite—in this case, the internal heat of the earth becomes more concentrated and active when the surface is chilled by winter cold.</note>. Thus, the very same **thinning** <term>subtiliation</term><note>original: "subtiliatio," the process of making a substance, like air or water, less dense or more vaporous through heat.</note> that the Sun caused on the surface of the earth during the summer is, conversely, caused by this internal heat within the earth’s center and bowels during the winter time. 

Consequently, it also produces those same effects: namely, by driving the waters and **air** <note>original: "aëra"</note> through thinning toward the surface of the earth, from which they had receded during the previous summer. Similarly, rain—which falls more frequently in winter because the cold forces the thick air to congeal into clouds—enters through the pores of the earth and drives out the air trapped within the earth's caverns. In many places, it also happens that this same expelled air, encountering water in its path, pushes that water along and takes its place;

->just as we have demonstrated above<-
->in the preceding experiments.<-

<summary>This page explains how seasonal changes and the "central heat" of the earth cause springs to flow, using the concept of antiperistasis to describe how internal heat intensifies in winter to push water and air to the surface.</summary>
<keywords>hydrogeology, antiperistasis, central heat, thermal expansion, springs, seasonal cycles, pneumatics</keywords>

# ON IMPERFECTLY MIXED BODIES.

## CHAPTER X.

### *A brief summary of the entire treatise brought together into one.*

<image-desc>Ornamental woodcut drop cap for the letter 'Q', featuring intricate scrollwork and foliage designs.</image-desc>WITH how much divine wonder and contemplation—after a pious meditation upon the whole Archetype <term>Archetype</term><note>In this context, the "Archetype" refers to the divine model or blueprint of the universe existing in the mind of God before creation.</note>—must we consider those two principles of created things: namely, the dark mass of matters, and that substantial light. This light was given on the first day by the inexplicable and divine will of the Creator himself for the creation of the world and its parts, and for the bringing forth of living things. It is undoubtedly clearer than the midday sun to all of you who are endowed with a sagacious mind and who look somewhat more deeply with "lynx-like eyes" <note>The lynx was a traditional symbol of keen sight that could pierce through solid objects; the author is praising readers who look past surface appearances to hidden causes.</note> into the hidden causes of things; for we can find nothing at all in this entire universe that does not participate in one or the other of the aforementioned principles. For, just as those two mentioned primary substances are contrary to each other in nature and disposition, so also whatever is found in this world to adhere more to one of them is also entirely opposed both to the opposite principle and to that which originates from it. 

There is, therefore, Light, to which Darkness is opposed. We have GOD, the Father of Light, and we have the Devil, the Prince of Darkness. For us and for the world, the Day shines forth, to which we find the Night—the veil of darkness—to be the contrary. Light produced for us the Heavens, converting the purer part of the dark mass into its own substance; yet Darkness left for us and for the Macrocosm <term>Macrocosm</term><note>The "Great World" or the universe as a whole, often contrasted with the "Microcosm" or the human being.</note> the Earth, burdened with a depth of blackness and obscurity. The essence of Light infused into us the sparks of life, but the body of darkness maliciously encloses, retains, and incarcerates the substance of these same sparks; it forces the splendor of the mind both to suffer and endure the most miserable tortures of the flesh, and—seduced by its various deceptions and snares—to deviate and wander through ignorance from the truth and the right path, as if blinded. 

From Light, finally, we have fire, form, essence, perfection, life, generation, motion or action, and beauty; but from Darkness we receive water, matter, privation <term>privation</term><note>A philosophical term meaning the absence or lack of a quality that should naturally be present.</note>, imperfection, death, corruption, rest, and ugliness. Similarly, who doubts that whiteness, exaltation, love, virtue, good, truth, knowledge, riches, and other things of that kind proceed from Light—the messenger of the highest Creator—when it is manifest and certain beyond any controversy that blackness, depression, hatred, vice, evil, falsehood, ignorance, poverty, and similar opposites proceed from Darkness, the messenger of the Devil? Oh, how great and how profound, then, is the dimension of these principles, and how inscrutable are their secrets! Though they can in no way be investigated through *a priori* <term>a priori</term><note>Reasoning from causes to effects, or from theoretical principles.</note> demonstration, God has nonetheless granted us permission to contemplate them *a posteriori* <term>a posteriori</term><note>Reasoning from observed effects back to their causes.</note>. Indeed, happy is he who can rightly know their mysteries, upon which the most profound of the Pagan <note>original: "Ethnicorum"</note> philosophers used to spend not just a year or years, but the entire span of their lives; for they were driven by a greater wonder and a more vehement zeal to inquire more deeply into these mysteries the more they found something of probability within them.

We conclude, therefore, that if anyone wishes to judge our preceding subject rightly and not rashly, he will perceive this opinion of ours to be not only probable but also (unless I am mistaken) not far distant from the truth itself. In the meantime, however, I will most gladly adapt and submit myself to the judgments of all those better versed in this divine Philosophy, praying to them again and again, that...

# TREATISE I. BOOK VII.

...may receive these writings of ours with a calm countenance, and may they—in a friendly and benevolent manner—correct the errors contained in them (which indeed I know have crept into so profound a work in no small or few numbers) with a sounder and more skillful judgment. I ask that they do so not with a **viperous tongue** <note>original: *lingua viperina*; a common metaphor for malicious, stinging, or "poisonous" criticism meant to destroy rather than improve.</note> (as very many are accustomed to do), but as befits the **sons of light** <term>sons of light</term><note>A term often used in Hermetic and mystical traditions to describe those seeking divine wisdom and spiritual enlightenment.</note> who burn with a fiery love. But to the rest of you, who have either never sown your seed in this field of **Philosophy**, or have not done so well, I earnestly pray that you do not unjustly condemn with a mind obscured by darkness those things which you do not rightly understand. Meanwhile, may that **immense and uncreated light** <note>In the author's world-view, "uncreated light" is the eternal, divine radiance of God that existed before the physical sun and stars were created.</note> preserve me from the injuries of the wicked, and keep all of you **safe and sound** <note>original: *sartos tectos*; literally "repaired and roofed," a Roman legal idiom meaning a building kept in good repair, used here to mean "completely protected."</note> and unharmed from the abyss of ignorance,

->conserving you by its living virtue and grace, adorned with<-
->eternal splendor.<-

Their harmonic consensus with the planets, 102. Their double motion, 150. They also take on the natures of the planets, 148. How they act upon these lower things according to the Philosophers, 148. They are not moved by intelligences <note>angelic beings</note>, but the faculty of moving was infused into them from the beginning by the divine will, 150. Although they are endowed with their own motion, they are not animals, ibid.

### T.
**Time** received its origin only on the fourth day, 142.

Its effects, ibid. The repercussion of the darkness of the middle heaven into the lowest region of the world, 59. How necessary that evacuation was, 133.
**Land of Fire** <note>Tierra del Fuego</note>, 117.

The mother, 99. Why it was created before the sun, 140. It does not move, contrary to Copernicus and Gilbert <note>William Gilbert, who proposed the Earth was a giant magnet and rotated</note>, 153, 154.
